**There is no difference between the transcendental forms of the Lord. Different forms are manifested due to different attachments of different devotees. Actually the Lord is one, but He appears in different forms just to satisfy His devotees.**

To explain how the one Supreme Lord manifests in countless ways, Caitanya Mahāprabhu uses the analogy of the valuable *vaidūrya* jewel, which reveals different colors according to its surroundings. These surroundings can be compared to the specific devotional moods of various worshipers. He quotes the following verse from Śri Nārada-pañcarātra:

*maṇir yathā vibhāgena nila-pitādibhir yutaḥ*  
*rūpa-bhedam avāpnoti dhyāna-bhedāt tathācyutaḥ*

**When the jewel known as** ***vaidūrya*** **touches various other materials, it appears to be separated into different colors, and consequently its forms also appear different. Similarly, according to the meditational ecstasy of the devotee, the Lord appears in different forms, although He is essentially one.**

The Vedānta-sūtra offers another analogy:

*sthāna-viśeṣāt prakāśādi-vat*

**As the sun’s rays are perceived differently in different places, the same one Lord is also perceived in various ways.**

How are we to understand this picture? Kṛṣṇa, although one, wonderfully reveals Himself according to the devotional approach of His worshiper as He Himself confirms:

*ye yathā māṁ prapadyante tāṁs tathaiva bhajāmy aham*  
*mama vartmānuvartante manuṣyāḥ pārtha sarvaśaḥ*

**As all surrender unto Me, I reward them accordingly. Everyone follows My path in all respects, O son of Pṛthā.**

Śri Vṛndāvana Dāsa Thākura presents this truth in a nutshell in the following formula:

*ye rūpa cintye dāse sei rūpa haya*

**As the servant meditates on the Lord, the Lord will reveal that specific form.**

Thus we should be aware that our *sādhana* awards the desired goal only if we constantly meditate on this goal with great faith while performing the various activities of that *sādhana*, especially our hearing and chanting. Śri Kṛṣṇa personally emphasizes this point in His teachings to Lord Brahmā, after having been worshiped by Brahmā’s famous prayers in the Brahma-saṁhitā:

*yādṛśi yādṛśi śraddhā siddhir bhavati tādṛśi*

**To whatever degree one develops his faith, that perfection one will achieve accordingly \[by one’s** ***sādhana*****\].**

To illustrate how the Lord reveals Himself according to His devotee’s meditation, Jiva Gosvāmi recounts a remarkable incident. The story tells of the exalted *brāhmaṇa* Viṣvaksena, who was roaming freely, having lost all attachment to the material world. Once, upon entering a dense and remote forest, he was suddenly confronted by the wild young son of a tribal chieftain. When the tribal chieftain’s son learned that he had met a *brāhmaṇa* able to perform various kinds of *pūjā*, he became delighted, because, as he told Viṣvaksena, just that morning he had awoken with a terrible headache that had prevented him from attending his daily *pūjā* of Lord Śiva.

In a harsh and demanding voice he ordered Viṣvaksena to perform the *pūjā* in his place. Viṣvaksena refused to accept this order. “I am an exclusive devotee of Lord Hari. I cannot worship anyone other than my *iṣṭa-deva*, the beloved Lord of my heart, Śri Viṣṇu. Find someone else to worship your Lord Śiva!” Upon hearing this blunt reply, the boy became enraged, drew his sword, and again insisted that Viṣvaksena either perform the *pūjā* or be killed.

Viṣvaksena was in a difficult situation. He did not want to lose his life, but he also wanted to maintain his spiritual principles. What should he do? After careful consideration, he agreed to perform the *pūjā*. He sat down before the Śiva-liṅgam, but before beginning, meditated as follows: “Lord Śiva is in charge of the mode of ignorance and destroys the universe at the end of creation. He also causes the mode of ignorance to increase. Lord Nṛsiṁhadeva, on the other hand, destroys the demons that are fully under the control of *tamo-guṇa.* This agitated boy is surely fully under the control of the lower modes and demoniac by nature. Therefore, while I appear to offer pūjā to Śiva, let me actually worship Lord Nṛsiṁhadeva in my heart. Since Lord Śiva is the topmost devotee of my Lord, Śri Nṛsiṁhadeva is residing in his heart and will certainly be present in this *pūjā*.”

His mind resolved, the brāhmaṇa began to chant the mantra to invoke Lord Nṛsiṁhadeva’s mercy – u*graṁ viraṁ mahā-viṣṇum*… –, while simultaneously presenting the ritual offering of flowers to the Śiva-liṅgam.

When the boy, who stood next to him, learned that the *brāhmaṇa* was uttering a mantra to invoke a different god, he became furious and raised his sword to behead Viṣvaksena. At that moment, the Śiva-liṅgam split into pieces and a ferocious Lord Nṛsiṁhadeva appeared and killed the boy, protecting His devotee from imminent danger.

There is still a famous temple in South India where Liṅga-sphoṭa Nṛsiṁhadeva, “Lord Nṛsiṁhadeva who became manifest (*sphoṭa*) from the lingam,” is worshiped.

From this incidence we learn that the Lord will reveal Himself according to our meditation on Him. This deep spiritual secret is of utmost importance when we recite the *gāyatri-mantras*, which are nondifferent from Śri Kṛṣṇa. One can chant the mantras with the aim to fulfill a variety of purposes, and one can also relate to the various personalities addressed by the same mantra. With the *brahma-gāyatri*, for example, one worshiper may address Sūryadeva, another Sūrya-Nārāyaṇa, and yet another Lord Kṛṣṇa. With the same mantra, in other words, these *bhaktas* will obtain different realizations according to their meditation. Śri Vṛndāvana Dāsa Thākura explains this point in relation to the worship of Lord Caitanya:

*ye mantrete ye vaiṣṇava iṣṭa dhyāna kare*  
*sei mata dekhaye Thākura viśvambare*

**A devotee sees Lord Viśvambhara according to the mantra he invokes while meditating on his worshipable Lord.**

Among the numberless methods of worship and moods of meditation, we must maintain a clear vision of our own *sādhya* and *sādhana*. Only then will we arrive at the desired goal. Otherwise, the mantras will carry us somewhere else, or even nowhere. Śrila Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākura also emphasized how important it is to keep the goal of one’s *sādhana* in mind:

**He who cultivates the preliminary devotional activities, anxiously keeping in view that realized state of devotion, can alone attain to that object of all endeavor. None else can have the same.**

The special concern of this publication is to present the unique angle and emphasis of our own glorious Gaudiya-Vaiṣṇava tradition. Our desire is to help those who take initiation in this *sampradāya* to attain the deepest realizations. By no means, of course, do we intend to minimize any of the other explanations or methods concerning the *gāyatri-mantras* provided by other spiritual traditions.

## 2. Saṅkalpa –fixing the goal

Whenever we chant a mantra, it is most important to be aware of its meaning. Otherwise the syllables, words, and finally the complete mantra pass through our mind almost like an announcement or advertisement flashing on a digital display in the corner of a shop window. Such ads go unnoticed and leave little impression. Only when we involve the consciousness, when we deliberately meditate on the meaning of the mantra, does it become fully effective.

The scriptures state this point strongly. In describing the five saṁskāras meant to purify a disciple, the Sanat-kumāra-saṁhitā says:

*tad-guṇān bhāvayan nityaṁ  japan mantram ahar-niśam*  
*mantrasyārthaṁ bhavāyaṁś ca premāśru-pulakāñcitaḥ*  
*ūrdhva-puṇdraṁ tataḥ kuryād bhālādiṣu vidhānataḥ*  
*tato mantra-dvayaṁ tasya   dakṣa-karṇe vinirdiśet*  
*mantrārthaṁ ca vadet tasmai yathāvad anupūrvaśaḥ*  
*dāsa-śabda-yutaṁ nāma dhāyya tasya prayatnataḥ*

**Day and night one should meditate on the Lord’s qualities and chant mantras glorifying Him. Thinking of the meaning of the Lord’s mantras, one should shed tears of love. The hairs of one’s body should stand erect in ecstasy.**  
**The spiritual master should draw vaiṣṇava-tilaka on the forehead and other places, and then he should speakthe two mantras in the disciple’s right ear. Then he should explain the meaning of the mantras, and then he should give the disciple a name, a name ending with the word** ***dāsa*** **\[servant\].**

Kṛṣṇa makes the same point in His teachings to Uddhava: *mantrasya ca parijñānam, “*a mantra is purified when chanted with proper knowledge.”

One receives a mantra from a bona fide spiritual master, who instructs one in how to chant it, what it means, and its ultimate purpose. It is essential to properly understand the meaning of the mantras one chants. Patañjali, author of the *yoga-sūtras*, also stresses this point:

**The sacred word** ***oṁ*** **connotes** ***iśvara*****, the supreme controller. One should chant** ***japa*** **of** ***oṁ*** **along with understanding its meaning (*****tad-japas tad-artha-bhāvanam*****).**

In his own inimitable way, Śrila Prabhupāda strongly advises us to understand the meaning of what we are chanting:

**Of course, those who are actually following the rules and regulations of** ***gāyatri-mantra*** **will gradually come to the spiritual understanding. But not by official chanting. That will not help. “Because, I have got** ***gāyatri-mantra*** **burple bura burra…” \[makes sound\] Finish. Not like that. Don’t cheat yourself. Try to understand!**

Here we again come to the point of “realization according to meditation”: If one has a wrong conception of the mantra’s meaning, the result will correspond to that wrong conception or desire. Therefore the great sage Yājñavalkya gave the following instructions:

**Meditate on the syllables of the mantra, the meaning of each word, and the meaning of the whole mantra. Without knowing its meaning, one will not attain the goal intended by chanting that mantra.**

The *gāyatri-mantras* are extremely potent. They are nondifferent from their presiding Deities. When we chant them perfectly, with proper understanding, Kṛṣṇa will reveal Himself through them.

By the potency of the g*āyatri-mantras*, the *sādhaka* will overcome the illusions of material existence and then, by the mercy of the holy name, eventually regain his *svarūpa*, his eternal constitutional position in the kingdom of God. To attain this goal and not something else, it is necessary to fix the mind on the right conception while meditating on *gāyatri*.

Therefore before chanting the *gāyatri-mantras*, *sādhakas* traditionally pronounce a *saṅkalpa*, a declaration of one’s intention and goal. While chanting they keep this *saṅkalpa* consciously fixed in their mind. This helps them to remain firmly focused on the mantra meditation.

Our *sampradāya* specifically recommends two processes to help the *sādhaka* firmly establish his *saṅkalpa*. The first is to chant the name of the mantra’s presiding Deity, either mentally or verbally, while touching the heart to indicate the Deity’s seat. The second is to pray to the Deity to please accept the prayer included in the mantra meditation.

At the beginning of spiritual life, one’s saṅkalpa may be more generally formulated. New *sādhakas* desire only to attain some kind of service; they do not know much detail about their specific relationship with the Lord. But as they become more advanced and purified – and as their constitutional position begins to manifest by the Lord’s mercy – the service they render will be more and more specific according to their *svarūpa*, whether they are situated in *dāsya*-, *sakhya*-, *vātsalya*-, or *mādhurya*-*rasa*.

## 3. The role of the sampradāya

As electricity is transmitted through an electric circuit, powerful spiritual knowledge is handed down through a line of disciplic succession (*sampradāya*). Śrila Prabhupāda explains:

**Whoever wants to understand the difficult subject matter of** ***kṛṣṇa-kathā*** **should accept the disciplic succession. \[…\] A perfect devotee is able to understand Kṛṣṇa through the disciplic succession, and his entrance into the kingdom of God is thereby certainly opened.**

According to the Padma Purāṇa there are four authorized lines of Vaiṣṇa-va disciplic succession. Of these four, Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the founder of the *gaudiya-vaiṣṇava-sampradāya*, accepted the line coming from Lord Brahmā through Madhvācārya and His own spiritual master, Iśvara Puri. Therefore our line is properly called the *brahma-madhva-gaudiya-sampradāya*. (The word *gaudiya* is derived from Gauda-deśa, another name for Bengal. It hints at Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu, who appeared there.)

Since the potency of mantras is manifest when they are heard from the lips of a spiritual master appearing in the *paramparā*, the *gāyatri-mantras* are called *guru-datta-mantras* (*guru-datta* – given by the guru). At the time of the second or *pañcarātrika* initiation, the spiritual master gives the disciple mantras that are authorized by the *pañcarātrika* scriptures and that have been passed down through his disciplic succession. The scriptures strongly emphasize that mantras that do not come through the tradition of spiritual masters will not bring the desired result:

*sampradāya-vihinā ye    mantrās te niṣphalā matāḥ*

**Unless one is initiated by a bona fide spiritual master in the disciplic succession, the mantra that one might have received is without effect.**

The word *niṣ-phala* literally means, “bearing no fruit.” Śrila Prabhupāda writes in this connection:

**The conditions for hearing the transcendental message of the Absolute Truth are set forth herein. The first condition is that the audience must be very sincere and eager to hear. And the speaker must be in the line of disciplic succession from the recognized** ***ācārya*****. The transcendental message of the Absolute is not understandable by those who are materially absorbed. Under the direction of a bona fide spiritual master, one becomes gradually purified. Therefore, one must be in the chain of disciplic succession and learn the spiritual art of submissive hearing.**

How does the process of disciplic succession work? A wonderful statement in this regard is found in the prayers of the demigods to Śri Kṛṣṇa after He had appeared in Mother Devaki’s womb:

*svayaṁ samuttirya sudustaraṁ dyuman*  
*bhavārṇavaṁ bhimam adabhra-sauhṛdāḥ*  
*bhavat-padāmbhoruha-nāvam atra te*  
*nidhāya yātāḥ sad-anugraho bhavān*

**O Lord, You resemble the shining sun. You are always ready to fulfill the desire of Your devotee. When** ***ācāryas*** **completely take shelter under Your lotus feet in order to cross the fierce ocean of nescience, they leave behind on earth the method by which they cross, and because You are very merciful to Your other devotees, You accept this method to help them.**

Śrila Prabhupāda writes in his purport to this verse:

**This statement reveals how the merciful** ***ācāryas*** **and the merciful Supreme Personality of Godhead together help the serious devotee who wants to return home, back to Godhead. \[…\] The duty of the guru is to find the means, according to the time, the circumstances, and the candidate, by which one can be induced to render devotional service. \[…\] Kṛṣṇa will accept a devotee who strictly follows the regulative principles and the method prescribed in the various books and literatures published by the authorities. The** ***ācārya*** **gives the suitable method for crossing the ocean of nescience by accepting the boat of the Lord’s lotus feet, and if this method is strictly followed, the followers will ultimately reach the destination, by the grace of the Lord. This method is called** ***ācārya-sampradāya*****.**

Let us read Śrila Prabhupāda’s wonderful statement again: “The *ācārya* gives the suitable method for crossing the ocean of nescience by accepting the boat of the Lord’s lotus feet, and if this method is strictly followed, the followers will ultimately reach the destination, by the grace of the Lord.” We should acknowledge the unique role of the *sampradāya* in revealing the way of meditation to the practicing devotee in regard to his or her spiritual tradition, especially in reference to the specific meaning and orientation of the *gāyatri-mantras*.

## 4. Following Śri Gaurāṅga

From the Preface of the Caitanya-caritāmṛta, we can learn how brilliantly Śrila Prabhupāda defines the goal of the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement – *sādhya* – and the method to reach that goal – *sādhana* – according to Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s instructions:

**There is no difference between the teachings of Lord Caitanya presented here and the teachings of Lord Kṛṣṇa in the Bhagavad-gitā. The teachings of Lord Caitanya are practical demonstrations of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s teachings. \[…\] Lord Caitanya therefore teaches direct worship of Lord Kṛṣṇa, who appeared as the foster child of the King of Vraja. He also teaches that the place known as Vṛndāvana is as good as Lord Kṛṣṇa. \[…\] Lord Caitanya also teaches that the highest mode of worship in the highest perfectional stage is the method practiced by the damsels of Vraja. \[…\] Lord Caitanya also teaches that Śrimad-Bhāgavatam is the spotless narration of transcendental knowledge and that the highest goal in human life is to develop unalloyed love for Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead.**

Śrila Prabhupāda refers here to a famous verse spoken by Śrinātha Cakravarti, who authoritatively describes the most important aspects of Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s conclusions (*siddhānta*):

*ārādhyo bhagavān vrajeśa-tanayas tad-dhāma vṛṇdāvanaṁ*  
*ramyā kācid upasānā vraja-vadhū-vargeṇa yā kalpitā*  
*śrimad-bhāgavataṁ purāṇam amalaṁ premā pum-artho mahān*  
*śri-caitanya-mahāprabhor matam idaṁ tatrādaro na paraḥ*

**According to Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s opinion, the following statements deserve the highest regard:**

**1. One should worship the Supreme Personality of Godhead, the son of Nanda Mahārāja, Śri Kṛṣṇa.**  
**2. Śri Kṛṣṇa’s transcendental abode, Vṛndāvana, is equally worshipable.**  
**3. The most pleasing form of worship from the Lord’s point of view is the worship performed by the gopis of Vṛndāvana.**  
**4. Śrimad-Bhāgavatam is the spotless Purāṇa.**  
**5. Pure love of God (*****prema*****) is the ultimate goal of life for all.**

Considering the difficulties of living in the modern world, Śrila Prabhupāda has designed the fundamental principles of his Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement carefully, particularly the different items of *sādhana*. This has been an enormous task. To keep the spiritual tradition intact, while at the same time adjusting it to the specifications of time, place, and circumstance, is a master’s work. It requires unique insight and, more, Kṛṣṇa’s merciful empowerment. Śrila Prabhupāda’s accomplishment has enabled us to follow directly in Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s footsteps even though, according to Vedic standard, we are in a most degraded condition.

This accommodation applies to our chanting of *gāyatri* as well. Through Śrila Prabhupāda we can learn the meaning of the *gāyatri-mantras* as our glorious *sampradāya* presents it. It is certainly the Lord’s merciful arrangement that we have contacted this potent line of disciplic succession, which has the potential to educate us in how to reach Lord Kṛṣṇa according to the direct teachings of Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Please remember the wonderful prayer of the demigods to Śri Kṛṣṇa quoted before, where they say, “*ācaryas* \[…\] leave behind on earth the method by which they cross, and because You are very merciful to Your other devotees, You accept this method to help them.” This is a beautiful meditation. For our benefit, our founder-*ācārya* Śrila Prabhupāda has left behind the boat by which we can cross the ocean of material existence. Not only that, but he first took the boat left by the previous *ācāryas* and fashioned it according to our needs. Now we simply have to board the boat and stay on board.

With these words I wish to humbly point out the tremendous spiritual benefits that can be obtained by following Śrila Prabhupāda’s authorized perspective on the *gāyatri-mantras.* To understand spiritual subject matter we ultimately require Kṛṣṇa’s mercy. This mercy will be transmitted into our hearts from the heart of a pure devotee who is pleased with us. Spiritual subjects cannot be successfully understood by performing academic acrobatics or eclectic research. They must be revealed.

For readers who come from a different spiritual tradition than me: Please don’t mind my enthusiasm for the teachings of my own spiritual master. You may take my words simply as an inspiration for your personal commitment to your own tradition.

## 5. Conclusion

Some of us who have been chanting the *gāyatri-mantras* for quite some time will become motivated by this chapter’s encouragement to undergo *gāyatri* reform. Perhaps we may have developed the habit of hurrying our mantras through the mind without being aware of what they mean. If that is the case, it is time for reform. Newcomers who have only recently received their *gāyatri-mantras* should practice right from the beginning to concentrate on the meaning of the mantras and to develop the proper prayerful attitude while chanting *gāyatri*.

## III. Becoming qualified

## 1. Śreyas and preyas

At this point a question may arise: What qualifications does one need in order to receive *mantra-dikṣā* and to derive the desired transcendental benefit from chanting the *gāyatri-mantras*? At the beginning of his Śrimad-Bhāgavatam purports Śrila Prabhupāda clearly explains:

**This** ***gāyatri-mantra*** **is meant for spiritually advanced people. When one is successful in chanting the** ***gāyatri-mantra*****, he can enter into the transcendental position of the Lord. One must therefore acquire the qualities of a** ***brāhmaṇa*****, or be perfectly situated in the mode of goodness in order to chant the** ***gāyatri-mantras*** **successfully and then attain to the stage of transcendentally realizing the Lord, His Name, His fame, His qualities and so on.**

To gain a thorough understanding of this statement, let us examine this subject from a more conceptual point of view. Any object or person is defined by four aspects: its name, form, qualities, and activities or effects. For example, let us look at the drum used in *kirtana*:

name  *mṛdaṅga*

form  a cone-shaped clay body covered with cow skin (from a cow that died a natural death)

qualities  the small head produces a high, ringing sound; the large head sounds deep, almost like rolling thunder

activities it accompanies ecstatic *kirtana* with rhythmic sounds, and when played by an expert, includes all shades of tone.

People can also be defined by these four attributes. Thus if someone has the name “*brāhmaṇa*” but does not possess the qualities and activities of a *brāhmaṇa*, then that person does not contain all the necessary constituents of the name. He would be like a *mṛdaṅga* that produces no sound. Such a *mṛdaṅga* is not really a drum. *Brāhmaṇas* are meant to display the qualities and activities of the twice-born. Otherwise they are not *brāhmaṇas* and their chanting of *gāyatri-mantras* will not be effective.

As aspirants for spiritual life, we are meant to become qualified. But we must be careful to situate ourselves according to our ability (*adhikāra*) instead of accepting positions or engagements for which we lack the qualities. Initiation that takes place only on the external level is not only useless, it can even be counterproductive. It may even lead to fanaticism. Śri Kṛṣṇa is stern in His instructions to Uddhava on this point:

*sve sve ’dhikāre yā niṣṭhā sa guṇaḥ parikirtitaḥ*  
*viparyayas tu doṣaḥ syād ubhayor eṣa niścayaḥ*

**Being fixed in the position for which one is qualified \[*****sve sve adhikāre*****\] is virtuous. The opposite, accepting a position for which one is not qualified, is irresponsible and considered impious. This is the definite conclusion.**

People in the modern world are accustomed to getting everything cheaply and therefore often prefer appearance to substance. It is common in this world to accept positions for which one lacks the necessary qualifications. While persons who do this may appear to benefit, the benefit is shortlived (*preyas*). Sooner or later, their lack of qualification will cause them to unceremoniously fall from their artificially usurped positions. If someone aspires for the position of a *brāhmaṇa* out of desire for prestige, his second initiation will not benefit him. Śrila Prabhupāda called such persons “two-paisa *brāhmaṇas*” since anyone can purchase a *brāhmaṇa* thread for two paisas in the bazaar. Although one may be expert at cheating the world of men, including oneself, one can never cheat the universal laws.

There is no such thing as successful cheating in spiritual life, because spiritual activities are performed before a different audience, the Supreme Personality of Godhead and the saints. The Lord and His pure devotees are not deceived by appearance devoid of substance. It is essential that we develop the proper qualities and strive for that which ensures us long-term spiritual benefits (*śreyas*). Unqualified persons who look only for shortterm results (*preyas*), and thus take the advanced *gāyatri-mantra* initiation can cause more harm than good, both to themselves and to others.

When I was a fifteen-year-old boy, two rotten molars gave me a painful but convincing lesson on this point. It was clear that a root canal was unavoidable, but being strictly a non-hero in the dentist’s chair, I requested the dentist to fix the holes quickly and gently and cover them with beautiful golden crowns. Fortunately, the dentist was not impressed with my childish behavior. She told me that she had to go deep to the root of the problem and that I should expect two days of pain. To cover the bad teeth with gold crowns would aggravate the problem, since the teeth would rot away under the crowns and one day the pain would be even worse. I had to accept her advice and learn that lesson.

If one wishes to receive the higher blessing of mantra initiation, one must be prepared to perform more serious responsibilities.

## 2. Rising to sattva-guṇa

One essential step toward becoming a true *brāhmaṇa*, a person situated in transcendence, is to learn to live in the mode of goodness. In His teachings to Uddhava, Śri Kṛṣṇa states: :

*sāttvikāny eva seveta pumān sattva-vivṛddhaye*  
*tato dharmas tato jñānaṁ  yāvat smṛtir apohanam*

**A person who has revived his direct knowledge of the eternal soul will easily drive away the illusion of identifying with the material body and mind. This wrong identification with body and mind is caused by the three modes of material nature. Therefore, as long as one has not yet attained the transcendental stage where one directly perceives the soul, one must cultivate a life in** ***sattva-guṇa*****. The reason is that by increasing the mode of goodness, one automatically can understand and practice religious principles, and by such practice one’s transcendental knowledge is awakened.**

In other words, it is easy to rise from the mode of goodness to the plane of pure goodness (*viśuddha-sattva*), the transcendental level free of any tinge of the lower modes (*tamas* and *rajas*). This is because a person situated in *sattva-guṇa* is able to mold his spiritual life in such a way that irreligion is vanquished and transcendental realization is automatically awakened. The mode of goodness is like a springboard to the transcendental stage. Under the influence of the lower modes of passion and ignorance, however, it is almost impossible to come directly to the transcendental level.

Viśvanātha Cakravarti Thākura explains that *rajo-guṇa* and *tamo-guṇa* are the causes of five powerful obstacles, which keep the door to transcendence solidly locked:

1\. *laya*  the tendency to sleep during chanting, hearing, and remembering

2\. *vikṣepa*  distraction, which prevents one from becoming absorbed in chanting

3\. *apratipatti*  chronic lack of enthusiasm, which prevents one from performing devotional service, even when not troubled by sleepiness or distraction

4\. *kaśāya*  the reappearance of old habits, such as anger, greed, and pride, during one’s practice of hearing and chanting

5\. *rasāsvāda*  having no taste for *rasa*, the blissful mellows experienced in a transcendental relationship with Śri Kṛṣṇa.

A person afflicted by these obstacles is unable to taste the nectar of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Surely all devotees must have experienced these five disturbing guests at one time or another and asked themselves, disheartened, when they will ever become free of them. The only solution is to rise to the mode of goodness, leaving behind *rajo-guṇa* and *tamo-guṇa*, the modes that cause these obstacles. Devotees established in *sattva-guṇa* become completely happy; once situated in goodness they find it easier to rise to the transcendental platform of unalloyed goodness.

If we examine what we have been through as a movement over the past thirty-five years, we will find many examples that prove how difficult it is for a person situated in ignorance to jump to transcendental God consciousness. By Kṛṣṇa’s special mercy such persons may experience enthusiasm at the beginning of their spiritual lives, but they will fail to maintain a steady level of *sādhana* over time. Later, they fall back to the place from which they started their spiritual adventure.

Although there are many causes of spiritual falldown, we should be aware that cultivating a life in *sattva-guṇa* is an essential prophylaxis against failure. Śrila Prabhupāda therefore says:

**One cannot be a devotee unless one at least qualifies as a** ***brāhmaṇa.*** **\[…\] But that is not the end of it. \[…\] One has to surpass the stage of a** ***brāhmaṇa*** **and reach the** ***vasudeva*** **stage to understand the Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa. \[…\] When a qualified** ***brāhmaṇa*** **factually becomes a Vaiṣṇava in the enlivened stage of liberation, he can know what is actually the Personality of Godhead.**

The practice of chanting *gāyatri* provides a good example for how necessary the qualities of goodness are. Such qualities include among others, peacefulness and self-control. We cannot advance without them. For example, as we will explain later, it is a *brāhmaṇa’s* duty to chant *gāyatri-japa* during the three *sandhyās* of the day. A person who is still strongly influenced by passion and ignorance – and who has therefore not developed peacefulness and self-control – will not be able to follow this instruction on a regular basis. As a result, he or she will unintentionally commit offenses against the presiding deities of the mantras, who after some time will withdraw their mercy from this “passionate *brāhmaṇa*.” Such persons will not experience transcendental revelation while chanting and will therefore find their enthusiasm for spiritual practice gradually fading.

*Sattva-guṇa* will help us attain *niṣṭhā-bhakti*, where devotional service is performed in a steady, peaceful, and self-controlled way, with unshakable faith nourished by spiritual revelation. Of course, if we practice concentrated *śravaṇam* and *kirtanam*, carefully avoiding all offenses, we will certainly attain *niṣṭhā*. But how will we avoid the offenses if we are still influenced by the five unwanted guests invited by *rajas* and *tamas*? It will benefit our practice if we become keenly aware of the need to rise to the mode of goodness.

As a driver has to occasionally look at road signs to ascertain whether he is still on course, a person who follows the road of *bhakti* has to look for signs to ascertain if he is becoming free from the lower modes and rising to the mode of goodness. If he is attentive, he will be able to correct himself and thus, with the help of his spiritual activities, reach the desired destination.

Śrila Śridhara Svāmi gives us an important hint about what signs to look for in ourselves to see if we are on the proper course:

**The most important criterion for deciding whether to deal with someone as a** ***brāhmaṇa*** **or as a member of another** ***varṇa*** **is the presence or absence of self-control and similar brahminical qualities.**

We can safely conclude that we will experience true Kṛṣṇa conscious realization only upon being established in the mode of unalloyed goodness, and that the general way to reach this stage is to develop *sattva-guṇa*, thus becoming free from the dangerous influences of *tamo*- and *rajo-guṇa.* Kṛṣṇa confirms this in His instructions to Uddhava:

*sattvenānyatamau hanyāt  sattvaṁ sattvena caiva hi*  
*ād dharmo bhaved vṛddhāt  puṁso mad-bhakti-lakṣaṇaḥ*  
*dharmo rajas tamo hanyāt  sattva-vṛddhir anuttamaḥ*  
*āśu naśyati tan-mūlo  hy adharma ubhaye hate*

**By development of material goodness one can conquer the modes of passion and ignorance, and by cultivation of transcendental goodness one may free oneself even from material goodness. When the living entity becomes strongly situated in the mode of goodness, then religious principles, characterized by devotional service to Me, become prominent. \[…\] Religious principles, strengthened by the mode of goodness, destroy the influence of passion and ignorance. When passion and ignorance are overcome, their original cause, irreligion, is quickly vanquished.**

For further illumination on this topic, please study Śrimad-Bhāgavatam 1.2.19-21, including Śrila Prabhupāda’s enlightening purports.

## 3. The qualities of a brāhmaṇa

One way to answer the question how to rise to *sattva-guṇa* is to study what it means to be a *brāhmaṇa*, because being a *brāhmaṇa* means being firmly established in the mode of goodness. Kṛṣṇa lists nine qualities of a *brāhmaṇa*:

*śamo damas tapaḥ śaucaṁ  kṣāntir ārjavam eva ca*  
*jñānaṁ vijñānam āstikyaṁ  brahma-karma svabhava-jam*

**Peacefulness, self-control, austerity, purity, tolerance, honesty, knowledge, wisdom and religiousness – these are the natural qualities by which the** ***brāhmaṇas*** **work.**

Such statements tend to discourage us. How will we ever develop such qualities? But there is an effective way. If we practice activities belonging to the qualities of *sattva-guṇa*, we will automatically rise to the brahminical platform. The principle is the same for those wishing to become entangled in the lower modes. By engaging in activities belonging to those modes, such as drinking alcohol or engaging in illicit sex, one develops the qualities belonging to ignorance and passion.

Here is an example, one close to our hearts as practicing devotees. Have you ever noticed how you develop humility toward others almost automatically simply by behaving in a modest way when you are around them? Behaving humbly allows one to actually become humble. Have you ever realized how satisfying it feels to act with consideration toward others, and to sincerely try to understand and respect their needs? Behaving with humility and consideration are acts in the mode of goodness. Acting in the mode of goodness works like magic. It’s like taking a subtle elevator and finding oneself moving toward a higher level of existence.

Let us now discuss how to apply the principle of acting to attain a quality in relation to the nine qualities of a *brāhmaṇa* listed above.

**1.** ***Śama*** **– peacefulness**

Peacefulness is the perfect way to attain the Lord’s mercy, because peacefulness is the source of forgiveness. It is easiest to become peaceful when one understands that everything is moving under Kṛṣṇa’s control.

No one is independent; everyone and everything is part and parcel of Kṛṣṇa and acts by His supreme desire. To become peaceful one should become childlike. Children are completely dependent on their parents. Devotees can feel the sun of peacefulness rising in their heart as soon as they feel their dependence on the Supreme Lord’s merciful guidance. One can practice this conscious connection with Kṛṣṇa by offering one’s words, mind, and activities in the Lord’s service, even when things appear to be going wrong.

**2.** ***Dama*** **– self-control**

One can gain control over the mind and senses through detachment and by engaging in spiritual practices. To become truly self-controlled, however, it is essential that one achieve satisfaction and taste in one’s spiritual practices. Only then will one maintain the strength to overcome the agitations of the mind and senses.

**3.** ***Tapas*** **– austerity**

One can become austere especially by rising early for spiritual activities, ideally during the *brahma-muhūrta*. One should also practice *yukta-vairāgya* by accepting everything favorable for one’s Kṛṣṇa consciousness and rejecting everything unfavorable.

**4.** ***Śauca*** **– purity**

One can become pure by practicing both internal and external cleanliness. Physical purity is achieved by bathing and washing regularly, internal purity by attentively chanting the Lord’s names. Śrila Prabhupāda stressed the importance of physical purity:

**Because they are not clean,** ***śaucam*****.** ***Śauca*** **is the qualification of** ***brāhmaṇa*****. We are getting sacred thread but we are neglecting how to become, how to remain,** ***śaucam*****,** ***śuci*****.** ***Śucināṁ śrimatāṁ gehe yoga-bhraṣṭo ’bhijāyate*****.** ***Śuci*** **means first-class** ***brāhmaṇa*****. So we are accepting sacred thread to become first-class** ***brāhmaṇa*****,** ***śuci*****, but we do not know, after eating, we have to wash our hand. We are taking the handkerchief and finished. So this kind of** ***brāhmaṇa*****, what they will do? That is not even a civilized man. So you should be very, very careful how to follow the rules and regulation.**

**5.** ***Kṣānti*** **– tolerance**

One practices tolerance in two ways, first, by forgiving the minor and larger injustices one has suffered, and second, by continuing to serve others.

Tolerance is based on the spiritual vision of equality, and can best be developed by practicing serious *bhajana*. Tolerance is a powerful attribute; it is the basis of forgiveness, nonviolence, peacefulness, compassion, meekness, and mercy. A devotee who cultivates this extraordinary spiritual quality can remain undisturbed even in the midst of great disturbance.

**6.** ***Ārjava*** **– honesty**

Honesty is developed by living and speaking the truth. In other places, Śrila Prabhupāda translates *ārjava* as “simplicity.” Simplicity means “freedom from duplicity.” In this sense, one becomes honest by leading a simple life. Śrila Prabhupāda stressed:

**The** ***brāhmaṇa*****, their duty is how to practice to control the mind, to control the senses,** ***śamo damas titikṣā*****, to be tolerant, to be very simple, simple living, not very gorgeous living. Whatever is absolutely necessary, a** ***brāhmaṇa*** **will accept, not more than that. That is simplicity, simple living, high thinking.**

**7.** ***Jñāna*** **– knowledge**

One gains knowledge by studying the scriptures and hearing from competent sources. This should be a daily practice for each *vaiṣṇava*.

**8.** ***Vijñāna*** **– wisdom**

One develops wisdom by applying the knowledge one has acquired. One should also pray sincerely to the Lord for realization.

**9.** ***Āstikya*** **– religiosity**

One becomes religious by practicing concentrated and regulated *sādhana*. *āstikya* includes faith in scripture, because through such faith one develops a theistic attitude. Śrila Prabhupāda writes:

**The word** ***āstikya*****, “faith,” implies that we should not interpret the words of scripture but take it “as it is.” When Kṛṣṇa says in Bhagavad-gitā, “Surrender to Me,” one should not think himself wiser than Kṛṣṇa and claim that it is not to the person Kṛṣṇa whom we have to surrender but to the spirit within Kṛṣṇa. “Faith” also means to practice devotional service without motivation and without interruption.**

## 4. The engagements of a brāhmaṇa

Now an important question may arise: If I accept *brāhmaṇa* initiation, should I act as a *brāhmaṇa* regardless of my nature, or should I stick to the duties assigned to me by my *varṇa*, which I have acquired from my previous lives? While examining this question, let us first see how the scriptures describe a *brāhmaṇa’s* six main engagements:

**1.** ***paṭhana***   **by studying the Vedas,** ***brāhmaṇas*** **become learned scholars**  
**2.** ***pāṭhana***   ***brāhmaṇas*** **teach the knowledge they acquire to others (this is also called** ***adhyāpana*****)**  
**3.** ***yajana   brāhmaṇas*** **become expert in worshiping the Lord or the demigods**  
**4.** ***yājana***   **they teach others how to execute this worship**  
**5.** ***pratigraha***   **they become qualified to receive alms from others**  
**6.** ***dāna***   **they distribute the wealth they acquire in charity.**

These six activities provide the basis of how a brāhmaṇa earns his livelihood. Śrila Prabhupāda cites the Manu-saṁhitā for scriptural evidence:

*ṣaṇṇāṁ tu karmaṇām asya triṇi karmāṇi jivikā*  
*yajanādhyāpane caiva viśuddhāc ca pratigrahaḥ*

**Of the** ***brāhmaṇa’s*** **six occupational activities, three are meant to maintain his life (jivika):** ***yājana*****, to worship the Lord,** ***adhyāpana*****, to teach the Vedas, and** ***viśuddhāt pratigraha*****, to accept alms from a pure source.**

As Śrila Prabhupāda explains, this prevents a *brāhmaṇa* from earning a salary:

**A** ***brāhmaṇa*** **cannot take up any professional occupational duty for his livelihood. The** ***śāstras*** **especially stress that if one claims to be a** ***brāhmaṇa*****, he cannot engage in the service of anyone else \[by accepting a regular payment\]; otherwise he at once falls from his position and becomes a** ***śūdra*****.**

This statement may naturally lead to the following question: Should *brāhmaṇa* initiated devotees who work for a salary give up their jobs and lifestyles? But this is not the point of the discussion, as Śrila Prabhupāda clearly explains in the following conversation:

**Pañcadravida: A** ***śūdra*****, if he is working, he cannot take brāhmaṇa initiation, but he can take** ***hari-nāma*****. Is that it?**

**Prabhupāda: \[…\] A devotee, because he is working as a** ***śūdra*****, he is not a** ***śūdra*****; neither he is a** ***brāhmaṇa*****. He is already on the spiritual platform. But for management we have to divide like that. One can do the** ***śūdra’s*** **work nicely – let him be engaged in that way. Why he should imitate a** ***brāhmaṇa*****?**

**Mahaṁsa: Does he get second initiation?**

**Prabhupāda: Everything he will get.**

**Mahaṁsa: He gets.**

**Prabhupāda: Yes. Second initiation means he is recognized: “Now he has become a fully competent** ***vaiṣṇava*****.”**

**Pañcadravida: What if the person working as śū*****d*****ra says, “I can do so much. I can…,”**

**Prabhupāda: First of all, try to understand. A devotee is neither** ***brāhmaṇa*** **nor** ***śūdra*****. He may act like a** ***śūdra*****, but he is not a** ***śūdra*****. He may act like a** ***brāhmaṇa*****; he is not a** ***brāhmaṇa*****. He is a** ***vaiṣṇava*****.**

In other words, the whole field of material duty, engagement, and livelihood belongs to the discussion of *varṇa* and *āśrama*, whereas *mantra-dikṣā* is a transcendental affair untouched by the devotee’s conditioned nature. Prabhupāda taught us that a *vaiṣṇava-brāhmaṇa* is different from a *varṇāśrama-brāhmaṇa*. Although having received initiation in the *pañcarātrika* system, a *vaiṣṇava-brāhmaṇa’s* emphasis lies on *bhāgavata-mārga*, not on *pañcarātrika-vidhi*. This is what it means to be transcendental to *varṇāśrama-dharma.*

We should also consider that in Vedic tradition *dikṣā* was given to the three higher *varṇas* (*brāhmaṇas*, *kṣatriyas*, and *vaiśyas*). Therefore, even from the aspect of Vedic *dharma*, receiving *gāyatri* initiation doesn’t necessarily mean one has to adopt the duties and lifestyle of a *brāhmaṇa*. If the initiate is a *kṣatriya* or *vaiśya* by nature, he will continue to act according to the *dharma* of his respective *varṇa*.

On the other hand, Śrila Prabhupāda repeatedly stressed the point that a Vaiṣṇava has to rise to a higher standard. Although he never demanded that his disciples follow the countless traditional rules of the *brāhmaṇa-varṇa*, he obviously expected them to develop a brahminical life based on purity, sense and mind control, truthfulness, and compassion:

**The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement has been started especially to create qualified** ***brāhmaṇas*** **to broadcast spiritual knowledge all over the world, for thus people may become very happy.**

Śrila Prabhupāda usually spoke of *brāhmaṇa* initiation, not *mantra-dikṣā*, to emphasize that receiving the *gāyatri-mantras* means that one should always try his or her best to rise to the brahminical platform according to the nine qualities previously discussed (*śamo damas tapaḥ śaucaṁ*…). Actually, a number of devotees who received *brāhmaṇa* initiation personally from Śrila Prabhupāda had to recite these qualities by heart and promise to live by them.

Even before being initiated into the *gāyatri-mantras*, *vaiṣṇavas* must possess certain preliminary qualifications. Then with the help of the *gāyatri-mantras*, they will be able to transcend the influence of material nature and eventually overcome their material designations (*varṇa and āśrama*). At that time they act as true *vaiṣṇava-brāhmaṇas* on the pure platform of *śuddha-sattva.*

Until that time, second initiated disciples will practice their *dharma* according to their conditioned nature. Exactly what form this practice takes depends on their individual natures. Disciples will want and accept their guru’s help in determining the particulars of their practice. Still, all devotees, especially those who have received *mantra-dikṣā*, should observe some general rules. A devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa should always aspire to act on the spiritual platform. To help us attain this, the *ācāryas* have provided easy engagements that qualify one as a *brāhmaṇa*. The following three specific areas will help us to quickly rise to the brahminical platform:

\- practice good *sādhana*

\- engage in *pūjā* either occasionally or regularly

\- spread Kṛṣṇa’s mercy by preaching

All devotees can perform these activities in one way or another, although each person may perform them according to his or her own social and personal status. Even if one lacks the outstanding qualities of a traditional Vedic *brāhmaṇa*, these three engagements will quickly mold his or her life and character. Even the serious attempt to practice these activities will qualify one for advancement. To illustrate this point, Śrila Prabhupāda once described how a young judge can become qualified: “The bench will teach you how to act.” Similarly, a devotee aspiring to become a qualified *brāhmaṇa* simply has to act from the “court bench” of these three engagements. He or she will become expert and pure sooner or later.

## 5. Faith in guru

To remind the *sādhaka* of the substantial importance of the guru in spiritual life, one of the *gāyatri-mantras* is dedicated to *śri-gurudeva*. It is only by the spiritual master’s mercy that the disciple receives these mantras. Even after initiating the disciple and bestowing these mantras, the *guru* continues to play a vital role in the disciple’s life, offering further instruction and guidance in spiritual practice. Therefore one should try to come closer to the spiritual master, remaining ever eager to learn from him.

*Mantra-dikṣā* is known as *upanayana*, a word with a twofold meaning. First, it means “being brought closer to the spiritual master” (*nayana* – leading), and second, “opening of the spiritual eyes” (*nayana* – eye; literally: “that which leads the way”). Both readings encourage the devotee who has either received *mantra-dikṣā* or is aspiring for it to deeply reflect on his or her relationship to *śri-gurudeva* and on the quality of his or her own discipleship.

We should never forget that it is the mercy of a pure *vaiṣṇava* that removes the numerous obstacles on the path of spiritual elevation. And since the spiritual master is particularly destined to accept the devotional service of his disciples and direct it to the Lord, he can help them to advance in a relatively short time – provided they exhibit sincerity and faithful surrender.

Nārada Muni stresses this point in his teachings to Yudhiṣṭhira Mahārāja about the proper conduct of civilized men. After having offered numerous means and tips on how to become free of the “enemies in the heart” – lust, anger, and so on – and rise to the plane of *śuddha-sattva*, he concludes:

*etat sarvaṁ gurau bhaktyā  puruṣo hy añjasā jayet*

**All this can be obtained automatically by serving the spiritual master with faith and devotion (*****gurau bhaktyā*****).**

**The qualities of a good disciple**

In reflecting on the relationship with his or her spiritual master, a mature disciple will try to become more qualified. In the Uddhava-gitā Śri Kṛṣṇa personally describes the qualifications of a bona fide disciple:

*amānya-matsaro dakṣo nirmamo dṛdha-sauhṛdaḥ*  
*asatvaro ’rtha-jijñāsur anasūyur amogha-vāk*

**The disciple must have the following qualifications:**  
**1. He must give up interest in the material bodily conception.**  
**2. He must give up material lust, anger, greed, illusion, madness and envy.**  
**3. He should be interested only in understanding the science of God, and he should be ready to consider all points in this matter.**  
**4. He should no longer think, “I am this body,” or, “This thing belongs to me.”**  
**5. One must love the spiritual master with unflinching faith.**  
**6. One must be very steady and fixed.**  
**7. The bona fide disciple should be inquisitive to understand transcendental subject matter.**  
**8. He must not search out faults among good qualities**  
**9. He should no longer be interested in material topics. His only interest should be Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead.**

**Six kinds of incompetent disciples**

The scriptures mention six kinds of unacceptable disciples:

*alir bāṇo jyotiṣakaḥ stabdhi-bhūtaḥ kimekakaḥ*  
*preṣita-preṣakaś caiva ṣad ete sevakādhamāḥ*

**These are the six kinds of persons among the lowest sevakas (servants or disciples):**  
**1.** ***ali***   **those who are as fickle and unsteady as bees**  
**2.** ***bāṇa***   **those who talk back in protest, piercing their master like an arrow**  
**3.** ***jyotiṣaka***   **those who procrastinate, who do not follow instructions immediately, but who wait and wait … and wait**  
**4.** ***stabdhi-bhūta***   **those who are lazy or inactive**  
**5.** ***kimekaka***   **those who refuse to try anything on their own**  
**6.** ***preṣita-preṣaka***   **those who pass on the direct orders they have received from their guru to others without carrying out the order themselves.**

Such disciples often fail to see their guru with spiritual vision and thus tend to find fault in him.

***Dakṣiṇā*** **– remunerating the guru**

We cannot estimate the value of a single instruction given for our guidance on the spiritual path by any material calculation, what to speak of trying to repay it with money. Still, in order to show gratitude toward the spiritual master, and to solidify the sweet relationship with him, a thankful disciple should make a substantial attempt to satisfy and remunerate the guru (*dakṣiṇā*). The following conversation between Kṛṣṇa and His brāhmaṇa friend and schoolmate Sudāmā illustrates this point:

**O** ***brāhmaṇa*****, do you remember what happened to us while we were living with our spiritual master? Once our guru’s wife sent us to fetch firewood, and after we entered the vast forest, O twice-born one, an unseasonal storm arose, with fierce wind and rain and harsh thunder.**

**Then, as the sun set, the forest was covered by darkness in every direction, and with all the flooding we could not distinguish high land from low. Constantly besieged by the powerful wind and rain, we lost our way amidst the flooding waters. We simply held each other’s hands and, in great distress, wandered aimlessly about the forest.**

**Our** ***guru*****, Sāndipani, understanding our predicament, set out after sunrise to search for us, his disciples, and found us in distress. Then, he said, “O my children, you have suffered so much for my sake! The body is most dear to every living creature, but you are so dedicated to me that you completely disregarded your own comfort.”**

Then Sāndipani Muni speaks the following important words:

*etad eva hi sac-chiṣyaiḥ kartavyaṁ guru-niṣkṛtam*  
*yad vai viśuddha-bhāvena sarvārthātmārpaṇaṁ gurau*

**This indeed is the duty of all true disciples: to repay the debt to their spiritual master by offering him, with pure hearts, their wealth and even their very lives.**

Few devotees are affluent, struggling to pay their rent, so they may wonder how they can afford to remunerate their spiritual master. However, the *dakṣiṇā* that really satisfies the spiritual master is not paid in mundane currency. Śri Kṛṣṇa explains this remarkable point to Uddhava:

*dakṣiṇā jñāna-sandeśaḥ*

**Real remuneration to the spiritual master is to teach others the knowledge one has received from him.**

In his commentary on this verse, Viśvanātha Cakravarti Thākura writes:

**The word** ***jñāna*** **means that after experiencing the spiritual joy of** ***kirtana*****, etc., one instructs one’s own relatives and friends about his realizations. This is the real gift to the spiritual master and not donations of wealth, clothing or whatever.**

This conclusion is in perfect line with Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s instruction:

*yāre dekha, tāre kaha ‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa*  
*āmāra ājñāya guru hañā tāra’ ei deśa*

**Instruct everyone to follow the orders of Lord Śri Kṛṣṇa as they are given in the Bhagavad-gitā and Śrimad-Bhāgavatam. In this way become a spiritual master and try to liberate everyone in this land.**

Following this mood, Śrila Prabhupāda encouraged us to repay our debt to him by “preaching vigorously,” just as he did for his spiritual master.

## 6. The joy of being a brāhmaṇa

Whenever devotees hear about developing better qualities, they become both encouraged and discouraged. They are encouraged because they understand that Kṛṣṇa consciousness is not cheap; rather, the practices are substantial and valuable. They can see that there is always room for improvement. Their discouragement comes from an honest investigation of their own heart. The conditioned souls’ hearts are almost like a coal mine. The more they wash, the blacker the water. We seem to have an unlimited stock of bad *saṁskāras*, which have been accumulated over countless lifetimes. A humble devotee will ask, “Do I have a realistic chance at becoming qualified?”

If we evaluate our own qualities we simply have to say no. We possess no power to become qualified by ourselves. But if we add Kṛṣṇa to our evaluation, we can humbly but confidently say “yes” to this question. Alone we have no chance; with Kṛṣṇa’s help we have a bright future. A devotee who understands this one point will take full shelter of Kṛṣṇa by chanting His holy names with great faith.

In the Caitanya-caritāmṛta Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmi lists twenty-six Vaiṣṇava qualities, such as kindness to all, honesty, equality toward everyone, mildness, peacefulness, freedom from material desire, moderate eating, and so on. All these traits, however difficult they may seem to attain, follow one single quality as servants follow their king. That quality is *kṛṣṇa-eka-śaraṇa*, exclusive surrender to Kṛṣṇa.

Our heart belongs to Kṛṣṇa. It is presently occupied by foreign invaders and their followers in the form of lust, greed, anger, etc. In this conditioned state, our heart resembles a country whose king has been driven out by his powerful enemies. The moment *kṛṣṇa-bhakti* enters the heart is like the moment the king and his entourage re-enter the capital in full power. The king’s enemies are forced to leave the city as he and his followers take up again their proper positions. Therefore the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam explains that all the demigods and their exalted qualities, such as religiosity, knowledge, and renunciation, become manifest in the body of those who have developed unalloyed devotion for the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Vāsudeva.

Where did *bhakti* get such extraordinary power – power that supercedes all other potencies? From Kṛṣṇa! When we practice *bhakti*, especially the processes of *śravaṇam* and *kirtanam*, we associate directly with the Supreme Lord. His association cleanses the dirt from the mind and very soon we find ourselves rising to pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We simply have to apply this process with full faith. Please study the following purport by Śrila Prabhupāda carefully and with great joy if you wish to gain a deep understanding of this point:

**One who directly associates with the Supreme Lord by hearing of His activities cleanses the dirt from his mind and very soon comes to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Sūta Gosvāmi confirms this in Śrimad-Bhāgavatam:**

*śṛṇvatāṁ sva-kathāḥ kṛṣṇaḥ puṇya-śravaṇa-kirtanaḥ*  
*hṛdy antaḥ-stho hy abhadrāṇi vidhunoti suhṛt-satām*

**The Supreme Lord within everyone’s heart becomes very pleased when a person hears narrations of His activities, and He personally cleanses the dirt from the mind of the listener.**

***Hṛdy antaḥ-stho hy abhadrāṇi vidhunoti*****: He washes off all dirt from the mind. Material existence is caused by dirty things within the mind. If one can cleanse his mind, he immediately comes to his original position of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and thus his life becomes successful. Therefore all the great saints in the devotional line very strongly recommend the process of hearing. Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu introduced the congregational chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra to give everyone a chance to hear Kṛṣṇa’s holy name, for simply by hearing Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/Hare Rama, Hare Rama, Rama Rama, Hare Hare, one becomes purified (*****ceto-darpaṇa-mārjanam*****). Therefore our Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is chiefly engaged in chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra all over the world.**

**After one’s mind becomes cleansed by chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, one gradually comes to the platform of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and then reads books like Bhagavad-gitā, Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, Caitanya-caritāmṛta and The Nectar of Devotion. In this way, one becomes more and more purified of material contamination. As stated in Śrimad-Bhāgavatam:**

*naṣṭa-prāyeṣv abhadreṣu nityaṁ bhāgavata-sevayā*  
*bhagavaty uttama-śloke bhaktir bhavati naiṣṭhiki*

**“By regularly hearing the Bhāgavatam and rendering service unto the pure devotee, all that is troublesome to the heart is practically destroyed, and loving service unto the glorious Lord, who is praised with transcendental songs, is established as an irrevocable fact.”**

**In this way, simply by hearing of the powerful activities of the Lord, the devotee’s heart becomes almost completely cleansed of material contamination, and thus his original position as an eternal servant who is part and parcel of the Lord becomes manifest. While the devotee engages in devotional service, the passionate and ignorant modes of material nature are gradually vanquished, and then he acts only in the mode of goodness. At that time he becomes happy and gradually advances in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.**

**All the great** ***ācāryas*** **strongly recommend that people be given a chance to hear about the Supreme Lord. Then success is assured. The more we cleanse the dirt of material attachment from our hearts, the more we will be attracted by Kṛṣṇa’s name, form, qualities, paraphernalia and activities. This is the sum and substance of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.**

This is the extraordinary power of *bhāgavata-dharma*, the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In his instructions to King Nimi, the great sage Śri Kavi confirms that it is Kṛṣṇa Himself who provides His devotee with the required qualification:

**Even ignorant living entities can very easily come to know the Supreme Lord if they adopt those means prescribed by the Supreme Lord Himself. The process recommended by the Lord is to be known as** ***bhāgavata-dharma*****, or devotional service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead.**

**O King, one who accepts this process will never blunder on his path in this world. Even while running with eyes closed, he will never trip or fall.**

This is an interesting statement: “Even while running with eyes closed, he will never trip or fall.” Śrila Śridhara Svāmi explains:

**Even if running with both eyes closed, a devotee on this path of** ***bhāgavata-dharma*** **will not stumble. “Closing one’s eyes” refers to being in ignorance \[of standard Vedic literatures\]. As it is said, “The śruti and smṛti scriptures are the two eyes of the** ***brāhmaṇas*****. Lacking one of them, a** ***brāhmaṇa*** **is half blind, and deprived of both he is considered completely blind.”**

Śrila Jiva Gosvāmi quotes the following verse from the *smṛti-śrāstra* in his Bhakti-sandarbha 173:

*śruti-smṛti mamaivājñe yas te ullanghya vartate*  
*ājñā-cchedi mama dveṣi  mad-bhakto’pi na vaiṣṇavaḥ*

**The** ***śruti*** **and smṛti literatures are to be understood as My injunctions, and one who violates such codes is to be understood as violating My will and thus opposing Me. Although such a person may claim to be My devotee, he is not actually a vaiṣṇava.**

Śrila Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākura, who is known for his uncompromising standards, concludes in his commentary to the above verse:

**In all** ***dharmas*** **other than** ***bhāgavata-dharma*** **one must consider the conditioned soul’s qualification. But a soul surrendered to the Lord is never confounded by error, even if unqualified in all other respects.**

**His feet never stumble, and he never falls. Even though wandering in the world at whim, he always resides in an auspicious place by the influence of his unswerving worship. The unique potency of** ***bhāgavata-dharma*** **does not appear in any of the other** ***dharmas*** **of the world. There is no comparison between a surrendered individual who has taken shelter of** ***bhāgavata-dharma*** **and the practitioner of any other** ***dharma*****.**

**The extraordinary mercy Kṛṣṇa bestows upon the followers of** ***bhāgavata-dharma*** **is the reason He calls this process** ***susukhaṁ kartum avyayam*****, “everlasting and joyfully performed.”**

However, a sincere devotee will never take undue advantage of the Lord’s inconceivable mercy but will endeavor to become qualified while simultaneously praying for the Lord’s further mercy and help: “My dear Lord, I am most sinful and unqualified. I have been trying for so long to forget You. Now I desire to take shelter at Your lotus feet. From this day on I am Yours. Whatever I possess – my body, mind, words, and whatever else – I now offer at Your lotus feet. Please make me qualified so that I can render eternally pleasing service unto You. Please do with me as You like.”

May this chapter help you by shedding light on how to attain the quality of goodness and finally rise to transcendental goodness. Transcendental goodness will enable each of us to chant the *gāyatri-mantras* with full success and eventually realize the Supreme Lord’s form, name, qualities, and pastimes.

## IV. The practice

## 1. The importance of sādhana

The initiation into Vedic mantras is accompanied by detailed instructions on how to practice the chanting. The Prāgjyotiṣa *brāhmaṇa* mentioned in the first chapter (page 25) serves as an example of someone who received a mantra along with instructions on how to chant it. The Bṛhad-Bhāgavatāmṛta states, *tad-dhyāna-ādi-vidhāna-ādhyam*: the goddess gave him the mantra along with the prescriptions (*vidhāna*) for meditation and so on (*dhyāna-ādi*). Sanātana Gosvāmi comments:

**Kāmākhyā Devi gave the** ***brāhmaṇa*** **not only the mantra but also the method for meditating on the mantra’s Deity; and, as implied by the word** ***ādi*** **(“and so on”), she also told him how to touch the body to sanctify it with the syllables of the mantra and how to worship the Deity through various means, including appropriate hand gestures.**

Not knowing how to chant the *gāyatri-mantras* is like owning a precious musical instrument that, although capable of producing the finest melodies, is unfortunately used as if it were a hammer. For lack of proper instruction, the owner of this fine instrument will not be able to produce the desired music. In the same way, if we chant *gāyatri* without properly knowing the prescribed methods of chanting, the result will be quite dissatisfying.

In Vedic times, when people were much more sophisticated than nowadays, they were expertly guided by learned *brāhmaṇas*. Thus they were easily able to understand and apply the complex and elaborate system of Vedic rituals. In this age, however, it is rare, if not impossible, to find such expert *brāhmaṇas*. Śrila Prabhupāda writes:

**Such** ***brāhmaṇas*** **are not only rare, but also not available in this age, and therefore for the purpose of spiritual upliftment in this fallen age, the Gosvāmis prefer the purificatory processes under the** ***pañcarātrika*** **formulas to the Vedic rites.**

Following Śrila Prabhupāda’s general guideline, we have not described in this book methods that are no longer practical. Rather, we have tried to concentrate on the methods of *bhakti-sādhana* that are in line with our *sampradāya’s* teachings, and particularly with those of Śrila Prabhupāda.

## 2. What is sādhana?

In the Gaudiya tradition, the process of spiritual upliftment is generally called *sādhana*. The verbal root *sādh* basically means “to attain a goal” or “to be successful.” Thus *sādhana* refers to any method adopted to obtain a specific goal (*sādhya*), whatever that goal may be. Without *sādhana* one cannot obtain *sādhya*, or the goal of one’s practice.

There are numerous *sādhana* practices, and each of them is designed to obtain a particular goal. Those who desire material enjoyment practice the *sādhana* of *karma-yoga*, those who desire impersonal liberation practice the *sādhana* of *jñāna-yoga*, and those who desire mystic perfection practice the *sādhana* of *aṣṭāṅga-yoga*. Those who aspire to attain their eternal loving service to Śri Kṛṣṇa, however, which is the original constitutional position of the eternal soul, adopt *bhakti-yoga* as their *sādhana*.

Not only *Gaudiya-vaiṣṇavas*, but many other spiritual schools practice the *bhakti* path. Although they may focus on different moods and methods in their practice, their goal is one: to serve and satisfy Śri Kṛṣṇa in one of His unlimited forms. The goal (*sādhya*) is *bhāva*, ecstatic love of God. Here is Śrila Rūpa Gosvāmi’s definition of *sādhana*:

*kṛti-sādhyā bhavet sādhya-bhāvā sā sādhanābhidhā*  
*nitya-siddhasya bhāvasya prākaṭyaṁ hṛdi sādhyatā*

**When transcendental devotional service, by which love for Kṛṣṇa (*****bhāva*****) is attained, is executed by the senses, it is called** ***sādhana-bhakti*****. Such devotion eternally exists within the heart of every living entity. The awakening of this eternal devotion is the potentiality of devotional service in practice.**

The last line of this verse is important. This *bhāva* is *nitya-siddha*, eternally perfected, and it is waiting in a dormant state in the living entity’s heart (*hṛdi*). The awakening (*prakaṭyam*) of this love is the potentiality (*sādhyatā*) of *sādhana-bhakti*. In other words, the attainment of love of God by practicing *sādhana* is a natural process for the conditioned soul. It is not an artificial imposition; rather, it is an immanent aspect of the *jiva’s* eternal consitutional nature. Śrila Prabhupāda gives an illustrating example:

**This practice is not for developing something artificial. For example, a child learns or practices to walk. This walking is not unnatural. The walking capacity is there originally in the child, and simply by a little practice he walks very nicely. Similarly, devotional service to the Supreme Lord is the natural instinct of every living entity. \[…\] This consciousness, though lying dormant in those who are materially contaminated, is found in every living entity. And, when purified, this is called Kṛṣṇa consciousness.**

Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu, in his explanation of the above verse to Sanātana Gosvāmi, emphasized the same point:

*nitya-siddha kṛṣṇa-prema ‘sādhya’ kabhu naya*  
*śravaṇādi-śuddha-citte karaye udaya*

**Pure love for Kṛṣṇa is eternally established in the hearts of the living entities. It is not something to be gained from another source. When the heart is purified by hearing and chanting, this love naturally awakens.**

Hearing and chanting are the main *aṅgas*, or limbs, of *bhakti-sādhana*. They purify the devotees’ heart and enable them to receive the manifestation of the Lord’s *hlādini-śakti* (pleasure-giving potency) and *cit-śakti* (knowledgegiving potency). Then the devotees develop *bhāva*, which leads to *prema*, the *sādhaka’s* ultimate goal.

The methods for chanting the *gāyatri-mantras* are as manifold as the various ways of *sādhana*. The following rules and recommendations will help the practitioner derive maximum benefit from chanting the mantras. We have divided them into external rules (methods) and internal aspects (mental attitudes). We are aware that it may not be possible to observe all of them at all times and and in all places. Nevertheless, it is good to know these rules and recommendations and to apply them whenever and wherever possible. Spiritual life is an individual process; what works for one person might not work for others. We would like to encourage the readers to find their own preferences or emphases, while at the same time maintaining the essential practices that apply to everyone.

## 3. Basic types of pañcarātrika-mantras

Various mantras are employed in *arcana* during the different phases of worship. According to Śrila Jiva Gosvāmi these mantras are all *nām-ātmaka-mantras*. That is, they are based on the Lord’s holy name (*nāma-ātmaka* – having the name as their nature). The following mantras are the basic types used in Deity worship:

1\. *dhyāna-mantras* meditation mantras used to mentally invoke the Lord’s form, activities, *dhāma*, etc.  
2\. *bija-mantras* “seed mantras” used for meditation and to purify the articles used in the worship.  
3\. *mūla-mantras* “root mantras,” being the Deity’s essence, are recited as each article of worship is offered as a means of addressing the Lord.  
4\. *stutis, stotras* prayers chanted at the end of worship to glorify the Lord’s name, form, etc.  
5\. *praṇāma-mantras* mantras used to offer obeisances to the Lord at the end of worship.  
6\. *gāyatri-mantras*    mantras used to worship the Lord by evoking the three principles of *sambandha*, *abhidheya*, and *prayojana*.

## 4. Methods of proper chanting

***Sandhyā*** **– the proper times**

It is important to chant *gāyatri* at the proper times (*sandhyās*), as the Vedas strongly emphasize:

*sandhyā-hino ’śucir nityam anarhaḥ sarva-karmasu*  
*yad anyat kurute karma na tasya phala-bhāg bhavet*

**One who does not perform his prayers and worship at the proper times (*****sandhyā-vandana*****) is deemed impure forever and is unfit to do any other** ***karma*****. Without doing** ***sandhyā-vandana*****, any activity done by him, such as studying scripture, chanting prayers, offering gifts, performing ceremonies, and even going to temples to worship, will be futile and fruitless.**

And the Agni Purāṇa confirms:

*evaṁ sandhyā-vidhiṁ kṛtvā gāyatriṁ ca japet smaret*  
*gāyatry-uktāni śāstrāṇi bhargaṁ prāṇāṁs tathaiva ca*

**One should perform the duties prescribed for the three** ***sandhyās*****. At those times one should perform** ***gāyatri-japa*****, while meditating on the mantras’ words as they are revealed in** ***śāstra*****, on the transcendental effulgence (*****bharga*****), and on the life-air (*****prāṇa*****).**

Lord Kṛṣṇa also instructed Uddhava in this regard:

*sandhyopāstyādi-karmāṇi vedenācoditāni me*  
*pūjāṁ taiḥ kalpayet samyak- saṅkalpaḥ karma-pāvanim*

**Fixing the mind on Me, with perfect determination (*****saṅkalpa*****), one should worship Me by his various prescribes duties, such as chanting the** ***gāyatri-mantras*** **at the three junctions of the day (*****sandhyā-upāsti-ādi*****). Such performances are enjoined by the Vedas and purify the worshiper of reactions to fruitive activities.**

The words *samyak-saṅkalpaḥ*, “with perfect determination,” are significant. Kṛṣṇa strongly advises Uddhava that chanting of *gāyatri* should be accompanied by a *saṅkalpa*, a firm resolution about the object of one’s meditation, as we have already discussed on page 45.

**The meaning of “*****sandhyā*****”**

The word *sandhyā* means “that which connects” or “junction.” The morning *sandhyā* is the junction between night and day. The noon *sandhyā* is the junction between forenoon and afternoon. The evening *sandhyā* is the junction between day and night. At these times the atmosphere is most suitable for meditation.

Technically, there is a fourth *sandhyā*, namely the junction between one day and the next at midnight. In Vedic times some *ṛṣis* observed *sandhyā-vandana*, the rituals and prayers at all the *sandhyā* times, even the midnight *sandhyā*. You can read more about the culture and history of *sandhyā-vandana* in the Questions and Answers section (page 298).

Each of the *sandhyās* is presided over by one of Lord Viṣṇu’s divine consorts. During the morning *sandhyā* one addresses Gāyatri Devi, during the noon *sandhyā*, Sāvitri Devi, and during the evening *sandhyā*, Sarasvati Devi. These three goddesses are expansions of Śrimati Rādhārāṇi. Devotees who fail to chant their mantras at one of the *sandhyās* are considered to have neglected and thus offended the personification of the respective *sandhyā*. In many places in the scriptures the importance of observing the *sandhyās* is stressed, such as in the following:

**If the sun should rise or set while a student is purposely indulging in sleep, ignoring the precepts of law, he must fast for a day, muttering the** ***gāyatri*** **one thousand and eight times.**

***Sandhyā*** **and the three** ***guṇas***

The word *sandhyā* can also be explained as follows: *sam* means “well” and *dhyā* refers to *dhyāna*. Thus *sandhyā* means “proper *dhyāna*” or “meditation on Kṛṣṇa.” Here the three *guṇas* play an important role. To be able to meditate properly, as explained in the last chapter, one has to come to the platform of *sattva-guṇa* and from there try to attain the transcendental level of *śuddha-sattva*. The practice of *sandhyā-vandana* (worship or meditation during the *sandhyās*) helps us overcome the lower modes of nature. To understand this, we have to examine how the *guṇas* are related to the different times of the day.

The dawn and morning time is the period of *sattva*, noon belongs to *rajas*, and dusk and evening to *tamas*. This is a cosmic law well established in the Vedic teachings. After waking in the morning, especially in the hours before and during *brahma-muhūrta*, the mind is peaceful, calm, and clear. With the mind in that state, meditation on the Lord is especially advantageous. This is why worship during the *prātaḥ-sandhyā* (morning worship) is prescribed.

As the day progresses, one becomes absorbed in work and other activities. This is the time of day when *rajo-guṇa* (the mode of creation and passion) exerts its influence. Thus before going to lunch break, during which the rush of *rajas* is interrupted for an hour or two, one is advised to again turn the mind toward the Lord by remembering through proper meditation that all one’s labor is directly or indirectly meant for Kṛṣṇa’s service. Only after performing this meditation should one eat and rest. In this way, *rajo-guṇa* is checked and *sattva-guṇa* is allowed to rule one’s mental condition. This is the significance of *madhyāhnika*, noontime worship.

Then as evening descends and work ends, the mind is turned toward the desire to eat, sleep, or perform activities averse to a high standard of spiritual life. By this, the third and lowest mode, *tamo-guṇa*, the mode of idleness and ignorance, threatens to dominate. Therefore before finishing one’s daily activities one should undergo a special effort to escape the grip of *tamas*. By turning the mind again to Kṛṣṇa and offering everything one has done and achieved to Him, one turns again to *sattva-guṇa*. This is the significance of *sāyam-vandana*, evening worship.

Persons who sincerely practice *sandhyā-vandana* three times a day throughout their life will surely escape the lower modes of nature and establish themselves in *sattva-guṇa*, the brahminical platform. For them, spiritual advancement will come swiftly and joyfully.

**The times of the** ***sandhyās***

Devotees often ask about the exact times of the three *sandhyās*. Since they correspond with the sun’s position, one has to observe the sun’s course:

Sandhyā   Start Point   End Point    Morning  24 minutes before sunrise  24 minutes after sunrise    Noon  24 minutes before sun is in zenith  24 minutes after sun is in zenith    Evening   24 minutes before sunset   24 minutes after sunset

In my own practice of devotional life, I try as often as possible to spend Kārttika in Vṛndāvana, away from the frantic and often unregulated life of a city preacher in the West. In Vṛndāvana I have found it astonishingly helpful to observe the precise times of the *sandhyās*. When we cannot follow such a strict schedule, the following chart may offer us a hint at the most suitable alternative times:

**Suitable times for chanting** ***gāyatri***

Morning Sandhyā  Noon Sandhyā  Noon Sandhyā    Best  just before sun rises, stars are still visible  sun is in the zenith sun is just setting,  stars are not yet visible    Second Best  sun and stars are not visible  as close to that moment as possible  sun has set, stars are not yet visible    Third Best  sun is up, stars have disappeared  sun has set, stars are visible

As with all aspects of our devotional service, we may sometimes have to adjust these principles according to time and circumstances. Thus especially in the extreme northern or southern countries, where the times of dawn and sunset differ significantly according to the season, it is not crucial to consider these exact times. It may even happen, as I experienced one summer in Iceland that the sun remains in the sky day and night – there was no sunset or sunrise! In such circumstances, morning *gāyatri* can be chanted after *maṅgala-āratika*, and evening *gāyatri* up until 9:30 P.M. (as long as the Deities are still awake).

With amusement I remember one devotee from ISKCON’s early days who was said to stop everything just so he could chant *gāyatri* at the exact time. For example, while shopping in a big city, as soon the sun arrived at its zenith, he would stop in the midst of a busy pedestrian precinct and stand transfixed, chanting his *gāyatri-mantras*. He continued this strict observance until he heard that there are more important rules, such as chanting in a place free of disturbance.

Also, the circumstances of our service, especially when we are preaching, may make it impossible to chant *gāyatri* at each *sandhyā*.
