By chanting *brahma-gāyatri*, such yogis systematically raise their consciousness along the seven *cakras*, starting from the lowest level, the sensual plane, and ending at the highest, the thousand-petal *cakra*, the plane of direct God realization. Kṛṣṇa describes a similar process to Uddhava:

*hṛdy avicchinam oṁkāraṁ ghaṇṭa-nādaṁ bisorṇa-vat*  
*prāṇenodirya tatrātha punaḥ saṁveśayet svaram*

**Beginning from the** ***mūladhara-cakra*****, one should move the life air continuously upward like the fibers in the lotus stalk until one reaches the heart, where the sacred syllable** ***oṁ*** **is situated like the sound of a bell. One should thus continue raising the sacred syllable upward the distance of twelve** ***aṅgulas*****, and there the** ***oṁkāra*** **should be joined together with the fifteen vibrations produced with** ***anusvara*****.**

This small excursion to the world of mystic yoga illustrates how the *brahma-gāyatri-mantra* and the *praṇava* (*oṁ*) are working on all levels of existence.

**Meditation**

Meditate on the divine sun of Kṛṣṇa, which is rising and spreading light through the lower (*bhūḥ*), upper (*bhuvaḥ*), and higher (*svaḥ*) planetary systems. May that divine light also inspire and enlighten my own small universe consisting of body, mind, and intellect.

*kṛṣṇa – sūrya-sama; māyā haya andhakāra*  
*yāhāṇ kṛṣṇa tāhāṇ nahi māyāra adhikāra*

**Kṛṣṇa is compared to sunshine, and** ***māyā*** **is compared to darkness.**  
**Wherever there is sunshine, there cannot be darkness. As soon as one takes to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the darkness of illusion will immediately vanish.**

Śrila Jiva Gosvāmi quotes several verses from the Agni Purāṇa to shed more light on the meaning behind the *brahma-gāyatri-mantra*. By meditating on these words before chanting *brahma-gāyatri*, we can become aware that this mantra is directing us toward the Supreme Lord in His transcendental abode:

**One should meditate on Gāyatri, on Sāvitri – who has manifested as the sun-god (*****savitur*****) – and on Sarasvati, the goddess of speech. One should also meditate on the spiritual effulgence (*****bhargaḥ*****) of the Lord, which is described in many Vedic hymns. Then one should meditate on the Lord’s supreme abode (*****vareṇyam*****), which is the best of all effulgent locations. This abode is the highest of all desired goals, above** ***svarga-loka*** **and impersonal liberation (*****apavarga*****).**

**To attain real liberation (*****vimukti*****), I meditate (*****dhimahi*****) on the eternal, pure, all-knowing, effulgent Lord, the one supreme master of all. He is Śri Viṣṇu, the origin of the impersonal** ***brahmajyoti*****, the creator, maintainer, and destroyer of the material world. Some who perform** ***yajña*** **offer prayers and oblation to Śiva, some to Durgā, some to Sūrya, and some to Agni. But as the Vedas declare, it is Viṣṇu who is the father of Agni and all the devas.**

**Thus the words devasya** ***savitur vareṇyam*** **indicate the abode of Viṣṇu, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Dhimahi means, “we meditate” or “we hold within the mind.” And** ***dhiyo yo nah pracodayāt*** **means, “May Lord Viṣṇu inspire the intelligence of us, the conditioned souls who are bound by** ***karma*****, which sometimes is visible and sometimes waits unseen.”**

**In this way one should meditate on the Supreme Lord. He may be seen in the circle of the sun. He is SadāŚiva. He is Brahman.**

**He is Viṣṇu. His realm is the supreme abode. The words devasya savitur vareṇyam describe this supreme abode. I am the servant of that Lord, who resides in the sun and who inspires all living beings to perform pious and impious deeds.**

We can summarize and refine this meditation as follows: By their mercy, the goddesses Gāyatri, Sāvitri, and Sarasvati – who are all manifestations of Śrimati Rādhārāṇi – will reveal the divine effulgence of the Lord and His supreme abode. This is the abode of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Let us therefore meditate on the Supreme Lord, His abode, and His divine consort Śri Rādhā. I am the servant of that Lord, who can be seen in the circle of the sun, who is the origin and foundation of everything, and who inspires all living beings to perform their good and bad deeds according to their desires.

## 2. The Guru-mūla-mantra

**Explanation**

*aiṁ* – (the “seed” of this mantra); *namaḥ* – \[I offer\] respectful obeisances; *gurave* – unto my spiritual master.

**Translation**

I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master.

**Illumination**

We can approach Śri Bhagavān only through his *bhakta*:

**He who worships the spiritual master and then worships Me (Bhagavān) attains perfection. Otherwise, one’s worship is fruitless.**

This is confirmed in the following well-known verse:

*ye me bhakta-janāḥ pārtha na me bhaktāś ca te janāḥ*  
*mad-bhaktānāṁ ca ye bhaktās te me bhakta-tamā matāḥ*

**My dear Pārtha, one who claims to be My devotee is not so. Only a person who claims to be the devotee of My devotee is factually My devotee.**

By worshiping one’s spiritual master one obtains his *kṛpā-śakti*. One should therefore always request the spiritual master’s permission to serve the Lord.

**Meditation**

In our temples we meditate every morning on the spiritual master as described in the Śri Gurvaṣṭaka prayers (*saṁsāra-dāvānala-lidha-loka…*). It is very helpful to study these prayers deeply before singing them and to contemplate their meaning during *maṅgala-āratika*.

Śrila Raghunātha Dāsa Gosvāmi offers another beautiful way to see the *guru*, namely, as the confidential servant of Śri Kṛṣṇa:

*guru-varaṁ mukunda-preṣṭhatve smara padam ajasraṁ manaḥ*

**O mind, meditate constantly on Śri-guru as the one who is very dear to Lord Mukunda (*****mukunda-preṣṭha*****).**

In addition, one can see the spiritual master as the carrier of the *svarūpa-śakti*, the Lord’s energy that is a combination of *saṁvit-śakti* (represented by Nityānanda Rāma) and *hlādini-śakti* (represented by Śrimati Rādhārāṇi). Śrila Prabhupāda explains:

**The spiritual master is always considered either one of the confidential associates of Śrimati Rādhārāṇi or a manifested representation of Śrila Nityānanda Prabhu.**

In Pañcarātra-pradipa we find a beautiful meditative prayer to the spiritual master:

*śri-guro paramānanda premānānda-phala-prada*  
*vrajānanda-pradānānda- sevāyāṁ mā niyojaya*

**My dear spiritual master, who gives the fruit of the highest bliss – the bliss of love of Godhead – please engage me in the blissful service of Śri Kṛṣṇa, who bestows bliss upon the land of Vraja.**

## 3. The Guru-gāyatri-mantra

**Explanation**

*vidmahe* – let us try to understand; *gurudevāya* – the spiritual master; *kṛṣṇa-ānandāya* – who is always in blissful Kṛṣṇa consciousness; *dhimahi* – let us meditate; *tat* – that; *guruḥ* – the spiritual master; *pracodayāt* – may he enthuse; *naḥ* – us.

**Translation**

Let us try to understand the spiritual master, who is always in blissful Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Let us meditate on him as he enthuses us.1

**Illumination**

Here the spiritual master is described as *kṛṣṇānandāya*. This is the dative case of *kṛṣṇānanda*. According to the rules of Sanskrit grammar, the following derivations of *kṛṣṇānanda* are possible:

1\. *kṛṣṇaḥ yasya ānandam* – he whose entire happiness in life is Kṛṣṇa, because his heart is filled with ecstatic love of Kṛṣṇa (*yasya* – whose; *ānan-dam* – happiness; *kṛṣṇaḥ* – is Kṛṣṇa)

2\. *kṛṣṇasya ānandam asti* – he who is the source of Kṛṣṇa’s happiness through his pure loving devotional service. (*asti* – he is; *ānandam* – \[the source of\] happiness; *kṛṣṇasya* – of Kṛṣṇa)

3\. *kṛṣṇaḥ ānanda ca* – he who represents Kṛṣṇa combined with Rādhārāṇi (here, ānanda means *hlādini-śakti*, or Śrimati Rādhikā)

The word *pracodayāt* comes from the Sanskrit root *pra-cud*, “to set in motion, drive on, urge, or impel.” The particular grammatical form applied here is called “optative”: it expresses a desire, request, or mild order. Possible translations are:

1\. May he enthuse us.

2\. May he impel us.

3\. May he appear fully.

4\. May he be pleased and engage us in his service.

The essential meaning, however, is “May he enthuse us.” The spiritual master enthuses his disciples by encouraging them to serve Kṛṣṇa. Śrila Prabhupāda is the personification of such enthusiasm. As he himself pointed out, “How could I have come to America at such an old age unless I was enthusiastic?” Śrila Prabhupāda always claimed that he was successful because he followed his spiritual master’s order, which was the source of his enthusiasm.

**Meditation**

At the end of Śri Bhajana-rahasya Śrila Bhaktivinoda Thākura offers a brief manual for worship called Saṁkṣepa-arcana-paddhati. There he recommends the following meditation:

**In the morning, one should remember the spiritual master as being situated in Śri Navadvipa, having two eyes and two arms, as being peaceful and as bestowing all benedictions and fearlessness upon chanting his name.**

**Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu is sitting in a jeweled pavilion at Yogapiṭha in the eternal abode of Māyāpura, Navadvipa. On his right side sits Nityānanda Prabhu and on his left side sits Gadādhara Paṇdita. Advaita Prabhu is standing on the front right side with folding hands offering prayers. On the front left side Śrivāsa Paṇdita is standing holding an umbrella. Śri Gurudeva is sitting on an altar below.**

**In this way meditate on sitting near Śri Gurudeva and performing the worship.**

Please also remember the beautiful meditation on being in the presence of Śri-guru, which we have presented on page 107 in the last verse of the *bhūta-śuddhi* prayers:

*divyaṁ Śri-hari-maṇdirādhya-tilakaṁ kaṇṭhaṁ sumālānvitaṁ*  
*vakṣaḥ śri-hari-nāma-varṇa-subhagaṁ śri-khaṇda-liptaṁ punaḥ*  
*pūtaṁ sūkṣmaṁ navāmbaraṁ vimalatāṁ nityaṁ vahantiṁ tanuṁ*  
*dhyāyet śri guru-pāda-padma-nikaṭe sevotsukaṁ cātmanaḥ*

**One should meditate on oneself sitting at the feet of one’s spiritual master, one’s pure body clothed in fine and new cloth and decorated in wonderful** ***tilaka*****, one’s chest marked with the holy name and anointed with sandalwood pulp, and one’s neck bedecked with a garland.**

## 4. The Gaura-mūla-mantra

**Explanation**

*kliṁ* – (the “seed” of this mantra); *namaḥ* – I offer respectful obeisances; *gaurāya* – unto Lord Gaurāṅga.

**Translation**

I offer my respectful obeisances unto Lord Gaurāṅga.

**Illumination**

Gaura is the combined form of Śri Śri Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Therefore the four-syllable *gaura-gopāla-mantra*, *gau-ra-aṅ-ga*, is identical to the four-syllable mantra, *rā-dhā-kṛṣ-ṇa*. Śrila Prabhupāda states:

**Śrila Bhaktivinoda Thākura explains the** ***gaura-gopāla-mantra*** **in his Amṛta-pravāha-bhāṣya. Worshipers of Śri Gaura-sundara accept the four syllables** ***gau-ra-aṅ-ga*** **as the** ***gaura-mantra,*** **but pure worshipers of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa accept the four syllables** ***rā-dhā-kṛṣ-ṇa*** **as the** ***gaura-gopāla-mantra*****. However, vaiṣṇavas consider Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu nondifferent from Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa (*****Śri-kṛṣṇa- caitanya rād-hā-kṛṣṇa nahe anya*****). Therefore one who chants the mantra** ***gaurāṅga*** **and one who chants the names of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa are on the same level.**

It was Śri Rāmānanda Rāya, Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s eternal associate, who perceived that Lord Caitanya was none other than Lord *śyāma-sundara* covered by Śrimati Rādhārāṇi’s divine effulgence. Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmi recorded this incident, describing how in great ecstasy Rāmānanda Rāya told Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu what he was experiencing:

**At first I saw you appear like a** ***sannyāsi*****, but now I see you as** ***śyāmasundara*****, the cowherd boy. I now see you appearing like a golden doll, and your entire body seems to be covered by a golden luster.**

The *ācāryas* explain that in order to understand Śrimati Rādhārāṇi’s mood, Kṛṣṇa entered Her heart and emerged after assuming Her transcendental effulgence. This golden form is Lord Caitanya, who is worshiped with the *Gaura-mūla-mantra*.

**Meditation**

One of the most beneficial meditations on Lord Gaurāṅga is the famous verse from Śrimad-Bhāgavatam that predicts His appearance in *kali-yuga*:

*kṛṣṇa-varṇaṁ tviṣākṛṣṇaṁ sāṅgopāṅgāstra-pārṣadam*  
*yajñaiḥ saṅkirtana-prāyair yajanti hi su-medhasaḥ*

**In the age of Kali, intelligent persons perform congregational chanting to worship the incarnation of Godhead who constantly sings the names of Kṛṣṇa. Although His complexion is not blackish, He is Kṛṣṇa Himself. He is accompanied by His associates, servants, weapons and confidential companions.**

Another illuminating verse about Lord Caitanya is found in the Bṛhad-Bhāgavatāmṛta:

*sva-dayita-nija-bhāvaṁ yo vibhāvya sva-bhāvāt*  
*su-madhuram avatirṇo bhakta-rūpeṇa lobhāt*  
*jayati kanaka-dhāmā kṛṣṇa-caitanya-nāmā*  
*harir iha yati-veśaḥ śri-śaci-sūnur eṣaḥ*

**Realizing that the ecstasy of His beloved devotees is even sweeter than His own, out of greed the Lord descended to this world in the form of His own devotee. Dressed as a** ***sannyāsi*****, golden in effulgence, He bears the name Śri Kṛṣṇa Caitanya. All glories to that Lord Hari, who has appeared as the son of Mother Śaci.**

Finally, here is a particularly beautiful verse composed by Śri Raghunandana Gosvāmi. Making use of the eight declension cases in Sanskrit grammar, it contains eight prayers of full surrender. We have included this verse in our *maṅgalācaraṇa* and would like to recommend it as worth learning by heart:

*gauraḥ sac-caritāmṛtāmṛta-nidhiḥ gauraṁ sadaiva stute*  
*gaureṇa prathitaṁ rahasya bhajanaṁ gaurāya sarvaṁ dade*  
*gaurād asti kṛpālur atra na paro gaurasya bhṛtyo ’bhavaṁ*  
*gaure gauravam ācarāmi bhagavān gaura prabho rakṣa mām*

**I pray to Śriman Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu whose ecstatic transcendental pastimes are like a river of nectar. Gaura has given the path of confidential devotional service. I will completely surrender to Gaura. Is there anyone more merciful than Gaura? I will become a servant of Gaura. I shall preach the glory of Gaura. May my Lord Gaura protect me.**

Please study the list below with the word explanations. It shows how intricately this verse takes advantage of the Sanskrit grammar to create these eight prayers. In Sanskrit the grammatical cases are formed by specific endings, which are marked here in bold:

1\. Nominative case (naming the subject):  
*gaur****aḥ*** *sat-carita-amṛta-amṛta-nidhiḥ*  
Lord Gaura is the nectar-reservoir of divine nectarean pastimes.

2\. Accusative case (stating the object):  
*gaur****am*** *sadā eva stute*  
I always chant the glories of Gaura.

3\. Instrumental case (denoting “with, by, through”):  
*gaur****eṇa*** *prathitam rahasya-bhajanam*  
The most esoteric form of worship \[the mood of the gopis\] was preached by Gaura.

4\. Dative case (denoting “unto, for”):  
*gaur****āya*** *sarvam dade*  
I offer everything unto Gaura.

5\. Ablative case (denoting “from, than”):  
*gaur****āt*** *asti kṛpāluḥ atra na paro*  
Other than Gaura, there is no one in this world as merciful.

6\. Genitive case (possessive; denoting “of, among”):  
*gaur****asya*** *bhṛtyaḥ abhavam*  
I will become the servant of Lord Gaura

7\. Locative case (position; denoting “in, on, at”):  
*gaur****e*** *gauravam ācarāmi*  
I act in reverence centered on Gaura.

8\. Vocative case (direct address):  
*bhagavān gaur****a*** *prabho rakṣa mām*  
O Gaura Prabhu! O Supreme Lord! Please protect me!

## 5. The Gaura-gāyatri-mantra

**Explanation**

*vidmahe* – let us try to understand; *caitanyāya* – Lord Caitanya; *dhimahi* – let us meditate; *viśvam-bharāya* – on Him who is the maintainer of the universe; *tat* – that; *gauraḥ* – Lord Gaurāṅga; *pracodayāt* – may He enthuse; *naḥ* – us.

**Translation**

Let us meditate on Lord Caitanya, the maintainer of the universe, who has enthused us to do so.

**Illumination**

**The meaning of** ***Caitanya***

Śrila Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākura explains the meaning of the name *caitanya* as follows:

**The word** ***caitanya*** **means: He who makes the world alive to the affairs of Kṛṣṇa. Mahāprabhu took** ***sannyāsa*** **to generate** ***caitanya*** **(life) in the** ***jivas*** **who were without it. This He did by the liberal distribution of** ***kirtana*** **and** ***prema*****. Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s** ***kirtanas*** **were by no means ordinary glorifications of the Lord; they were imbued with the highest mellows of love of Godhead.**

Śrila Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmi gives a similar statement about the extraordinary nature of Lord Gaurāṅga’s *kirtana*:

**Thus He spread** ***kirtana*** **even among the untouchables. He wove a wreath of the holy name and** ***prema*****, with which He garlanded the entire material world.**

In other words, we can meditate on Caitanya Mahāprabhu not only as the *yuga-avatāra* who gave us the religious principle for this age of Kali, but as the bestower of the highest of all spiritual achievements, *kṛṣṇa-prema*, pure love of Kṛṣṇa, in one of the confidential *rasas*. This is the essence of the word *caitanya*, and it is also emphasized in one of Rūpa Gosvāmi’s prayers:

*anarpita-cariṁ cirāt karuṇāyavatirṇaḥ kalau*  
*samarpayitum unnatojjvala-rasāṁ sva-bhakti-śriyam*  
*hariḥ puraṭa-sundara-dyuti-kadamba-sandipitaḥ*  
*sadā hṛdaya-kāṇdare sphuratu vaḥ śaci-nandanaḥ*

**May the Supreme Lord who is known as the son of Śrimati śaci Devi be transcendentally situated in the innermost core of your heart. Resplendent with the radiance of molten gold, He has descended in the Age of Kali by His causeless mercy to bestow what no incarnation has ever offered before: the most elevated mellow of devotional service, the mellow of conjugal love.**

In order to glimpse Lord Caitanya’s true nature, we have to become aware that He is directly Śri Kṛṣṇa appearing in Śrimati Rādhārāṇi’s mood. By personally tasting Her devotion for Him, He tries to understand

1\. the glory of Her love

2\. His own sweetness that Rādhārāṇi experiences from Her position as Śri Kṛṣṇa’s beloved

3\. the unlimited degree of Her happiness upon tasting Śri Kṛṣṇa’s wonderful qualities

**The meaning of** ***Viśvambhara***

Śri Gaurāṅga’s name Viśvambhara means “the maintainer of the universe.” Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmi explains:

**In His early pastimes He is known as Viśvambhara because He floods the world with the nectar of devotion and thus saves the living beings. The verbal root** ***dubhṛñ*** **\[which is the root of the word** ***viśvambhara*****\] indicates nourishing and maintaining. He \[Lord Caitanya\] nourishes and maintains the three worlds by distributing love of God.**

When Caitanya Mahāprabhu appeared, He induced an ample rainfall of His auspicious mercy. This mercy nourished and maintained all living beings, especially His devotees. According to the great Vaiṣṇava author Kavi-karṇapūra, Lord Caitanya distributes His mercy in three ways:

1\. By driving away lamentation and making everything pure, He bestows transcendental bliss (*pronmilad-āmodayā*).

2\. By vanquishing all misconceptions regarding the proper understanding of the revealed scriptures He inspires everyone to understand the truth of devotional philosophy and to search for the Supreme Lord, Śri Kṛṣṇa. (*śāmyat-śāstra-vivādayā*).

3\. By distributing all the transcendental mellows He makes the living entities joyful, intoxicated by divine love (*rasa-dayā*).

In other words, Lord Caitanya maintains the universe not by ordinary food grains but by distributing the “grains” of transcendental nectar. This is illustrated by a beautiful verse that Śrila Prabhupāda sent to all temples as soon as it was translated:

**Man becomes strong and stout by eating sufficient grains, but the devotee who simply eats ordinary grains but does not taste the transcendental pastimes of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Kṛṣṇa gradually becomes weak and falls down from the transcendental position. However, if one drinks but a drop of the nectar of Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes, his body and mind begin to bloom, and he begins to laugh, sing and dance.**

Śrila Prabhupāda comments:

**All the devotees connected with the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement must read all the books that have been translated (the Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, Bhagavad-gitā and others); otherwise, after some time, they will simply eat, sleep and fall down from their position. Thus they will miss the opportunity to attain an eternal, blissful life of transcendental pleasure.**

**Meditation**

In Śrila Bhaktivinoda Thākura’s Saṁkṣepa-arcana-paddhati we find a beautiful meditation on Lord Gaurāṅga:

**I worship Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu, whose hair is bound with strings of pearls and whose moonlike face bears the nectar of His gentle smile. His beautiful golden body is adorned with lovely garments, anointed with sandalwood and aguru pulp and bedecked with garlands and various glistening ornaments. He is extremely charming, absorbed as He is in enjoying the sweet mellows of dancing. His dress is more splendid than even Cupid’s, and He is being served by all his devotees and associates.**

The Pañcarātra-pradipa contains the following *praṇāma-mantra* for worship of Lord Caitanya:

*ānanda-lilā-maya-vigrahāya*  
*hemābha-divyac-chavi-sundarāya*  
*tasmai mahā-prema-rasa-pradāya*  
*caitanya-candrāya namo namas te*

**I offer my repeated obeisances unto Śri Caitanya-candra, who is the embodiment of blissful pastimes, who is so beautiful, having a dazzling luster, like gold, and who bestows the mellow of** ***mahā-prema*****, the highest love of Śri Śri Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa.**

Śrila Prabodhānanda Sarasvati has composed a prayer by which we can enter deeply into the mood of taking exclusive shelter at the lotus feet of Lord Gaurāṅga:

*saṁsāra-duḥkha-jaladhau patitasya kāma-*  
*krodhādi-nakra-makaraiḥ kavali-kṛtasya*  
*durvāsana-nigaditasya nirāśrayasya*  
*caitanya-candra mama dehi padāvalambham*

**O Caitanya-candra! Having fallen into the ocean of repeated births and deaths in this material world, I am suffering again and again. The crocodiles of lust, anger, and so forth are devouring me, and thus I am chained to wicked desires. Please give this person, who has no other shelter, the shelter of your lotus feet.**

## 6. The Gopāla-gāyatri-mantra

**Explanation**

*svāhā* – offering oblations; *kṛṣṇāya* – unto Lord Kṛṣṇa; *govindāya* – unto Lord Govinda; *gopi-jana* – of the gopis; *vallabhāya* – unto the lover.

**Translation**

I offer oblations (*svāhā*) unto Lord Kṛṣṇa, who is Govinda, the lover of the *gopis*.

**Illumination**

Of all the *dikṣā-mantras*, the *gopāla-mantra* is the most important. Sanātana Gosvāmi, the *guru* of all Gaudiya-Vaiṣṇavas, has broadcast its glories in his famous work, Bṛhad-Bhāgavatāmṛta. The Hari-bhakti-vilāsa also describes its immense potency in several verses, like the following:

**As soon as the** ***gopāla-mantra*** **touches the devotee’s heart, it brings results, just as fire burns everything it touches.**

*atha śri-gopāladeva-mantra-māhātmyam*  
*mantras tu kṛṣṇadevasya sākṣād bhāgavato hareḥ*  
*sarvāvatāra-bijasya sarvato viryavat-tamaḥ*

**Hear now the glories of the** ***gopāla-mantra*****: Of all mantras, the mantra of Lord Kṛṣṇa, who is the original Personality of Godhead, the seed of all incarnations, is the most powerful.**

The *gopāla-mantra* is preceded by the powerful *bija-mantra* *kliṁ*, which is the *kāma-bija,* the seed of all desires. Although the principal purpose of the *gopāla-gāyatri* is to “make the soul run after all-attractive Kṛṣṇa,” as Śrila Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākura explains, it can also fulfill material desires if that is the chanter’s intention. Śrila Vyāsadeva confirms:

**The ten-syllable** ***gopāla-mantra*** **is the highest mantra. This mantra stops** ***saṁsāra*** **\[the circle of repeated births and deaths\], dispels sins and ignorance, gives victory in battles, fearlessness in forests, and safety in floods. It gives wealth and all types of prosperity to the devotees. The** ***gopāla-mantra*** **bestows strength, vigor, heroic valor, a beautiful celestial body, and the eight mystic powers. This mantra increases** ***bhakti*** **to the lotus feet of Śri Kṛṣṇa.**

The *gopāla-gāyatri* chanted today in the Gaudiya tradition has eighteen syllables. The ten-syllable mantra mentioned in the above verse is the *gopāla-mantra* chanted by Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu (*gopijanavalla bhāya svāhā*). This mantra is included in the eighteen-syllable mantra, and thus both are called *gopāla-gāyatri*.

As the above verse reveals, the *gopāla-mantra* gives everything from victory in battle to *prema-bhakti*. Thus for a devotee of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa it is best to chant this mantra in a spirit of eager longing for the greatest of all wealth, pure love for the Divine Couple. Otherwise, the mantra’s potency might help us to “powerfully” maintain our entanglement in material life. Reading books like Bṛhad-Bhāgavatāmṛta and associating with dedicated devotees will convince us that love of Kṛṣṇa is the highest attainment. To those who chant with a purely spiritual intention, the *gopāla-mantra* will manifest Kṛṣṇa’s forms and pastimes according to the chanter’s individual *rasa*.

No doubt these are wonderful prospects. Let us not become discouraged if these benefits do not stand immediately at our door as soon as we start to chant. As a mango fruit needs time to mature, the fruits of chanting will come when we have matured. Until then, we should enthusiastically, persistently, and patiently worship the Lord by chanting this mantra at the proper times, with our utmost concentration. We should always remember that the *gopāla-mantra* is like a divine shining jewel in our hands, which, although still covered, is ready to reveal its brilliance at the proper time.

**The six components of the Gopāla-mantra**

In the Brahma-saṁhitā Lord Brahmā describes the *gopāla-mantra* as having six limbs \[*ṣaṭ-aṅga*\]. Śrila Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākura comments by explaining the six components of the *gopāla-gāyatri*:

1\. *krsnāya kṛṣṇa-svarūpa* – the true nature or proper self of Kṛṣṇa

2\. *govindāya kṛṣṇasya cin-maya-vraja-lilā-vilāsa-svarūpa* – the true nature of Kṛṣṇa’s transcendental pastimes in Vraja

3\. *gopijana tat-parikara-gopijana-svarūpa* – the true nature of His spiritual associates in Vraja, the *gopas* and *gopis*.

4\. *vallabhāya tad-vallabha* – the true nature of self-surrender to Kṛṣṇa in the footsteps of the *gopis*

5\. *svā śuddha-jivasya cid-(jñāna)-svarūpa* – the true nature of the unalloyed individual soul, who is full of spiritual knowledge

6\. *hā cit-prakṛtir arthāt kṛṣṇa-seva-svabhāva* – the true nature of transcendental service to Kṛṣṇa that is awakened with pure cognition

***Sambandha*****,** ***abhidheya*** **and** ***prayojana*** **in the Gopāla-mantra**

By realizing this knowledge, one’s intimate personal relationship with Lord Kṛṣṇa is established (*sambandha*). Then one attains *niṣṭhā*, absolute firmness in devotional practice (*abhidheya*). Finally, one realizes the supreme goal (*prayojana*) in the form of blissful service to the supreme male, Śri Kṛṣṇa, in the mood of a maidservant of Rādhārāṇi.This description shows how the *gopāla-gāyatri* reveals the *sambandha*-, *abhidheya*-, and *prayojana*-*tattvas*. Thus *kṛṣṇāya* is a meditation on Madana-mohana, *govindāya* on Govinda, and *gopijanavallabhāya* on Gopinātha. Śrila Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākura reveals the presence of these three aspects in the *gopāla-gāyatri-mantra* in his own unique language:

**1. \[About** ***sambandha*****\] The meaning is that** ***rasa*** **is only the transcendental service of the central refuge Śri Kṛṣṇa, as predominating aspect of the Absolute, by one’s ego as the spiritual maid of the predominated moiety \[half part\] of the absolute integer \[in other words: by seeing oneself as a** ***gopi*****, a maidservant of Śrimati Rādhārāṇi who is predominated by Śri Kṛṣṇa\], attended with pure devotion in the shape of one’s entire self-surrender.**

**2. \[About** ***abhidheya*****\] The pastime in Goloka or in Gokula during the stage of devotional progress is the meditative worship through the mantra.**

**3. \[About** ***prayojana*****\] During the stage of perfection the pastimes manifest themselves as the unrestrained transcendental jubilations.**

Śrila Bhaktivinoda Thākura emphasizes that our spiritual practice must be based firmly on scriptural cultivation and association with devotees, especially with those *sādhus* who chant the holy name without offense.

**Meditation**

Since the *gopāla-gāyatri* is a meditation on Kṛṣṇa in His forms as Madana-mohana (the *sambandha-adhideva*), Govinda (the *abhidheya-adhideva*) and Gopinātha (the *prayojana-adhideva*), the most direct meditation is to offer obeisances to these three Deities by reciting Their *praṇāma-mantras*:

*jayatāṁ suratau paṅgor mama manda-mater gati*  
*mat-sarvasva-padāmbhojau rādhā-madana-mohanau*

**Glory to the all-merciful Rādhā and Madana-mohana! I am lame and ill advised, yet They are my directors, and Their lotus feet are everything to me.**

*divyad-vṛndāraṇya-kalpa-drumādhaḥ*  
*śrimad-ratnāgara-siṁhāsana-sthau*  
*śrimad-rādhā-śrila-govinda-devau*  
*preṣṭhālibhiḥ sevyamānau smarāmi*

**In a temple of jewels in Vṛndāvana, underneath a desire tree, Śri Śri Rādhā-Govinda, served by Their most confidential associates, sit upon an effulgent throne. I offer my most humble obeisances unto Them.**

*śrimān rāsa-rasārambhi vaṁśi-vaṭa-taṭa-sthitaḥ*  
*karṣan veṇu-svanair gopir gopināthaḥ Śriye ’stu naḥ*

**Śri Śrila Gopinātha, who originated the transcendental mellow of the rāsa dance, stands on the shore in Vaṁśi-vaṭa and attracts the attention of the cowherd damsels with the sound of His celebrated flute. May they all confer upon us their benediction.**

Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmi remembers how Lord Nityānanda, by His causeless mercy, gave him Śri Madana-mohana as his Lord and master and how also Śri Govinda and Śri Gopinātha were revealed to him:

**The mercy of Lord Nityānanda showed me Śri Madana-mohana and gave me Śri Madana-mohana as my Lord and master. He granted to one as low as me the sight of Lord Govinda. Words cannot describe this, nor is it fit to be disclosed.**

**On an altar made of gems in the principal temple of Vṛndāvana, amidst a forest of desire trees, Lord Govinda, the son of the King of Vraja, sits upon a throne of gems and manifests His full glory and sweetness, thus enchanting the entire world. By His left side are Śrimati Rādhārāṇi and Her personal friends. With them Lord Govinda enjoys the** ***rāsa-lilā*** **and many other pastimes.**

**Lord Brahmā, sitting on his lotus seat in his own abode, always meditates on Him and worships Him with the mantra consisting of eighteen syllables. Everyone in the fourteen worlds meditates upon Him, and all the denizens of Vaikuṇṭha sing of His qualities and pastimes.**

**The sweetness of Govinda**

Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmi continues by quoting a famous verse:

**The goddess of fortune is attracted by Kṛṣṇa’s sweetness, which Śrila Rūpa Gosvāmi has described in this way:**

*smerāṁ bhaṅgi-traya-paricitāṁ sāci-vistirṇa-dṛṣṭiṁ*  
*vaṁśi-nyastādhara-kiśalayām ujjvalāṁ candrakeṇa*  
*govindākhyāṁ hari-tanum itaḥ keśi-tirthopakaṇṭhe*  
*mā prekṣiṣṭhās tava yadi sakhe bandhu-saṅge ’sti raṅgaḥ*

**“My dear friend, if you are indeed attached to your worldly friends, do not look at the smiling face of Lord Govinda as He stands on the bank of the Yamunā at Keśi-Ghāṭa. Casting sidelong glances, He places His flute to His lips, which seem like newly blossomed twigs. His transcendental body, bending in three places, appears very bright in the moonlight.”**

This is perhaps one of the most intriguing descriptions of Kṛṣṇa’s all-attractive sweetness in His form as Govinda, and a wonderful meditation while chanting *gopāla-gāyatri.* Śrila Prabhupāda comments:

**In this verse Rūpa Gosvāmi indicates that if one wants to be satisfied in the pleasure of society, friendship and love, he need not seek shelter at the lotus feet of Govinda, for if one takes shelter under His lotus feet he will forget that minute quantity of so-called pleasure. One who is not satisfied with that so-called pleasure may seek the lotus feet of Govinda, who stands on the shore of the Yamunā at Keśi-tirtha, or Keśi-Ghāṭa, in Vṛndāvana and attracts all the gopis to His transcendental loving service.**

**How Brahmā sees the Lord**

It is also inspiring to meditate on Lord Brahmā’s vision after he received the gopāla-gāyatri-mantra. After having engaged in austerities for a long time, Brahmā described his meditation as follows:

**There exists a divine lotus of a thousand petals, augmented by millions of filaments, in the transcendental land of Goloka. On its whorl, there exists a great divine throne on which is seated Śri Kṛṣṇa, the form of eternal effulgence of transcendental bliss, playing on His divine flute resonant with the divine sound, with His lotus mouth. He is worshiped by His amorous milkmaids with their respective subjective portions and extensions and also by His external energy \[who stays outside\] embodying all mundane qualities.**

In the Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad Lord Brahmā speaks the following beautiful verses, after having been asked by the four Kumāras about Kṛṣṇa’s form, how to chant His mantra, and how to worship Him. These verses reveal the actual form of Vraja-kiśora, the youthful cowherd boy in Vṛndāvana:

**Kṛṣṇa appears like a cowherd boy. His complexion is like a monsoon cloud. He stays under a desire tree.**

**A person who meditates with full devotion on two-armed Lord Kṛṣṇa, the supreme controller, whose eyes are like two beautiful lotus flowers, whose complexion resembles a monsoon cloud, whose garments shine like lightning, who is holding His hands in the** ***jñāna-mudrā*****, the sign of transcendental knowledge, who wears a garland of forest flowers, who is surrounded by** ***gopas*****,** ***gopis*****, and** ***surabhi*** **cows, who rests under a desire tree, who is decorated with divine ornaments, who stands in the middle of a red lotus flower, and who is fanned by breezes that carry the spray of the waves of the Yamunā – such a person becomes liberated from repeated birth and death.**

**Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s separation from the Lord**

Finally, as an inspiration for your own practice, hear how deeply absorbed Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu was when He meditated on the *gopāla-mantra*, and how filled with transcendental emotion in the mood of *vipralambha-bhāva* (separation from the Lord):

**One day the Lord sat down in a solitary place and began to meditate on His ten-syllable mantra. After some time, when the Lord regained His external consciousness, He began to shed tears and call for Kṛṣṇa:**

**“O My dear Kṛṣṇa! O father! O Hari, My life and soul! Where did you go after stealing My heart? I saw My Lord, but now, where has He gone?”**

**Then He began to cry. Absorbed in sentiments of ecstatic love, His entire body covered with dust, He started to recite various verses. Loudly He cried out in distress, “Where did You go, leaving Me behind, My dear child Kṛṣṇa?”**

**That Lord who was previously most grave now became greatly agitated in ecstatic love. He rolled on the ground, cried loudly, and floated in the ocean of devotional feelings of separation.**

**Then, after some time, Nimāi’s students came and pacified Him with great care.**

## 7. The Kāma-gāyatri-mantra

**Explanation**

*vidmahe* – let us try to understand; *kāma-devāya* – the master of the senses; *dhimahi* – let us meditate; *puṣpa-bāṇāya* – on Him who is carrying arrows of flowers; *tat* – that; *anangaḥ* – the transcendental Cupid; *pracodayāt* – may he enthuse; *naḥ* – us.

**Translation**

Let us meditate on the master of the senses, who carries the arrows of flowers, and thus become enthused by Him, the transcendental Cupid.

**Illumination**

The *kāma-gāyatri* reveals the *lilās* of Śri Gopijanavallabha, the beloved of the *gopis*, and a prayer to attain the service of this transcendental Cupid. The *upāsya* (object worshiped by this mantra) is Deva-Deva, the Lord of Lords. He is described as *śṛṅgara-rasa-rāja-svarūpa-abhinna*. This means that He is nondifferent (*a-bhinna*) from the embodiment (*svarūpa*) of *śṛṅgara-rasa-rāja*, the king of the *rasa* of conjugal love. He is Madana, the transcendental Cupid in person. He is Śri Kṛṣṇa, the all-attractive Lord, the son of Nanda Mahārāja. He eternally resides in His abode of Śri Vṛndāvana-dhāma.

Śri Kṛṣṇa’s celebrated flute has eight holes, which produce eight highly attractive pitches. These pitches attract all living beings to His loving devotional service. The fifth pitch is particularly reserved for the *gopis*. This transcendental vibration is manifest as *kāma-gāyatri*.

Thus the *kāma-gāyatri* holds a unique position among the *gāyatri-mantras* because it is addressing the Lord’s most elevated and confidential pastimes, namely, His loving relationship with His foremost devotees, the *gopis*.

Usually a *kṛṣṇa-bhakta* maintains three desires, and they are each fulfilled by the *kāma-gāyatri-mantra:*

1\. to attain Kṛṣṇa

2\. to establish a loving relationship with Him

3\. to engage favorably in His loving devotional service

Although the *kāma-gāyatri-mantra* is considered the most superior of all *gāyatri-mantras,* its full realization can be acquired only at an advanced stage of devotion. For this end it requires the transcendental potency of the *gopāla-mantra*. In other words, these two mantras are complementary; they should be chanted together. Lord Brahmā’s example in Brahma-saṁhitā shows that he too had to first perfect his chanting of the *gopāla-mantra* before being qualified to receive the *kāma-gāyatri*, and this took him a long time. Also, *kāma-gāyatri* emphasizes *prayojana-tattva* (the ultimate goal). The *gopāla-mantra,* which introduces the chanter to the Lord and deals equally with all three tattvas, is a suitable introduction to it.

**The meaning of Kāmadeva**

*Kāma-devāya* means “unto the Master of the senses.” Śrila Śridhara Svāmi notes that as the transcendental Cupid, Kṛṣṇa intends to conquer the material world with His unequaled effulgence, by awakening the desire in everyone to return to Him. Śukadeva Gosvāmi makes a similar statement:

*anugrahāya bhaktānāṁ mānuṣaṁ deham āsthitaḥ*  
*bhajate tādṛśiḥ kridā yāḥ śrutvā tat-paro bhavet*

**When the Lord assumes a humanlike body to show mercy to His devotees, He engages in such pastimes as will attract those who hear about them to become dedicated to Him.**

Śrila Viśvanātha Cakravarti Thākura comments that “such pastimes” refers especially to His *rāsa-lilā* with His beloved *gopis* in Vṛndāvana. The *rāsa-lilā* has the “inconceivable spiritual potency to attract the polluted heart of the conditioned souls.”

Śukadeva Gosvāmi reveals such pastimes in detail in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam:

**Śri Kṛṣṇa went with the** ***gopis*** **to the bank of the Yamunā, where the sand was cooling and the wind, enlivened by the river’s waves, bore the fragrance of lotuses. There Kṛṣṇa threw His arms around the** ***gopis*** **and embraced them. He aroused Cupid in the beautiful young ladies of Vraja \[…\] by joking with them, glancing at them and laughing with them. In this way the Lord enjoyed His pastimes.**

**Kāmadeva Kṛṣṇa and Cupid**

The *kāma-gāyatri* is named after its *mantra-devata*, the transcendental Kāmadeva Kṛṣṇa, the master of the senses. All living beings are meant to satisfy Him with their spiritual senses.

In the material world we find a reflected counterpart of Kāmadeva Kṛṣṇa. This is Cupid, the almighty ruler of the material sphere. Armed with five arrows, Kāmadeva controls the world. His arrows are described as follows:

1\. *harṣaṇa* delighting, gladdening, thrilling with joy and desire

2\. *rocana* appealing, charming, giving pleasure and satisfaction

3\. *mohana* deluding, confusing, leading astray

4\. *śoṣaṇa* withering, parching, drying up

5\. *māraṇa* killing, destroying

“Love makes the world go round,” as the saying goes. This love, being so strong even on the mundane plane, can easily pull us toward Kṛṣṇa if it becomes transcendentally purified.

The *kāma-gāyatri* helps us to overcome lust, the material reflection of true love, which binds the soul to the repeated cycle of birth and death. *Kāma-gāyatri* is a guide to transcendental reality. As soon as the chanter’s consciousness becomes sufficiently purified, he or she will receive an impression of the spiritual world, revealed by the mantra’s transcendental sound vibration.

Śrila Prabhupāda even mentioned that those who are highly elevated and who chant *kāma-gāyatri* will be able to hear Lord Kṛṣṇa’s beautiful flute music as He calls His most confidential devotees.

**The meaning of Puṣpabāṇa**

Puṣpabāṇāya refers to Kṛṣṇa carrying five flower arrows, as previously mentioned. Whereas the mundane Kāmadeva uses his arsenal of arrows to agitate the heart, Kṛṣṇa’s arrows pacify the heart from the fire of mundane lust. Their effect is spiritually stimulating. Kṛṣṇa uses them to awaken the transcendental emotions of *bhāva* and *prema*, by completely capturing the five senses of His dear devotees:

1\. His beautiful form, which includes His overall appearance, dress, ornaments, garlands, etc., attracts His devotees’ eyes. This refers also to His restless eyes and His effulgent body, which looks like a fresh monsoon cloud surcharged by a brilliant lightning. This lightning is either His *dhoti* or Śrimati Rādhārāṇi.

2\. His bodily fragrance, which is mixed with the aroma of the wonderful substances that anoint Him, such as aguru, camphor, and sandalwood pulp, attracts His devotees’ sense of smell.

3\. The sounds that emanate from Kṛṣṇa, which include the sound of His voice, His flute, the jingling of the bells and other ornaments that decorate the Lord, and the rustling of His *dhoti* as He comes closer on Vraja’s forest paths, attract His devotees’ ears.

4\. The touch of Kṛṣṇa’s body, His lotus feet, hands, chest, etc., attracts His devotees’ sense of touch. Kṛṣṇa’s touch is described as cooler than moonbeams.

5\. Devotees taste the Lord by tasting His food remnants, the betel leaves He has chewed, or, in *mādhurya-rasa*, His lips. These tastes attract the devotees’ sense of taste.

Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmi describes these arrows in relation to Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s transcendental ecstasies:

**When Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu realized Lord Jagannātha to be Kṛṣṇa Himself, Lord Caitanya’s five senses immediately became absorbed in attraction for the five attributes of Lord Kṛṣṇa.**

In his purport, Śrila Prabhupāda explains these five attributes, which are seen as the transcendental Cupid’s five arrows:

**Śri Kṛṣṇa’s beauty attracted the eyes of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, Kṛṣṇa’s singing and the vibration of His flute attracted the Lord’s ears, the transcendental fragrance of Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet attracted His nostrils, Kṛṣṇa’s transcendental sweetness attracted His tongue, and Kṛṣṇa’s bodily touch attracted the Lord’s sensation of touch. Thus each of Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s five senses was attracted by one of the five attributes of Lord Kṛṣṇa.**

**The meaning of Anaṅga**

Kṛṣṇa is called Anaṅga, the transcendental cupid. The mundane Kāma-deva is also called Anaṅga (*an* – without; *aṅga* – limbs). He is Kṛṣṇa’s worldly reflection. The story of how he received this name is connected to the incident of Dakṣa offending Lord Śiva:

After Śiva’s wife Sati had given up her body, unable to tolerate her father Dakṣa’s blasphemy against her husband, Śiva became mortified. Taking her body in his arms and dancing wildly, he threatened to destroy the universe with his wrath. Lord Viṣṇu had to personally intervene. With his transcendental *cakra* He cut Sati’s body into many pieces. Wherever a piece of her body fell, that place became a holy *tirtha*. Seeing this, Lord Śiva became peaceful and sat down to meditate. Soon, he entered a deep trance.

Sati, Lord Śiva’s eternal associate, took birth again, this time as Umā, the daughter of the Himalayas. In due course, she began to meditate on Śiva, desiring to again become his wife.

Around that time, the demigods were facing great difficulties. The *asuras*, their eternal enemies, had become strong and were constantly attacking their celestial kingdom. The *devas* needed a powerful and expert commander-in-chief to lead them successfully in battle.

Deciding that the most suitable person for the task would be a son of Śiva, the lord of destruction, and knowing that Umā was his eternal consort, the demigods approached Umā and asked her to conceive a child with Śiva.

Umā agreed. But when she approached her lord in the Himalayas, she found him absorbed in such deep *samādhi* that he did not notice her at all. Umā tried her best to attract his attention, but all her attempts failed. Disheartened, the demigods approached Kāmadeva. His powerful arrows seemed their last hope in breaking Lord Śiva’s trance so that he could fall in love with Umā.

Kāmadeva was hesitant to accept such a task, knowing well the destructive power of Śiva’s wrath, and knowing, too, that Śiva would certainly be angry to have his meditation disturbed. Only when the demigods both assured him of their protection and convinced him of the importance of his mission did Kāmadeva agree. Taking his bow he went high into the mountains, hid behind a bush, and shot his powerful arrows at Lord Śiva. Lord Śiva’s meditation broke abruptly. Angrily, he looked around and discovered Cupid hidden behind a bush, his flower bow still in his hand. Lord Śiva’s fierce glance instantly burnt Cupid to ashes, and from that time on he became known as Anaṅga, “one without a body.”

By the mercy and protection of the *devas*, even without a body Anaṅga has kept his power to enter the material bodies of all living entities and exert his influence upon them – an experience that almost any living entity feels whenever he or she falls in love.

To complete the story, the demigods’ desire was fulfilled. When Śiva saw his beautiful wife Parvati in a new body, he was naturally attracted and conceived a child with her. As a result, their son Kārttikeya was born. Kārttikeya later became the commander-in-chief of the demigods.

**Kāma-gāyatri leads to** ***rāsa-lilā***

Understanding that powerful Cupid is a mere reflection of Kṛṣṇa, the transcendental Anaṅga, we can glimpse the overwhelmingly attracting power of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s *rāsa-lilā.* In this world, lust is meant to satisfy our senses. Such an attempt is always met with disappointment. If we can turn lust into an intense eagerness to please Kṛṣṇa, we will find real success. The *kāma-gāyatri-mantra* is meant to point our desires in that direction. You will find more information on this topic on page 243, where the relationship between *kāma-gāyatri* and spontaneous devotional service is described.During one Navadvipa-maṇdala-parikrama, Śrila Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākura spoke confidentially about the subtle truth expressed in the *kāma-gāyatri-mantra*. While honestly assessing our limited eligibility for such elevated subjects, let us humbly appreciate the vast ocean of truths and sentiments the *kāma-gāyatri* can offer us.

**If we are fortunate enough that the light emanating from Kṛṣṇa’s toenails illuminates our hearts, then we will hear the sound of His flute and, as servants of Kṛṣṇa’s beloved** ***gopis*****, will throw aside all duties and run to meet Him on the site of the** ***rāsa*** **dance. When we arrive there, our male or female bodies will merge with the material energy. \[…\]**

**Gentlemen! All of you are seeking your own ultimate good. Give up all forms of pretension – whether artificially taking a type of dress or artificially making a display of spiritual emotion or devotion. Give up the worship of women and effeminacy. Surrender yourself to the eternal service of Śrimati Rādhārāṇi and become handmaidens of Śri Rūpa Mañjari. Just as Rādhā and Her girlfriends are always totally absorbed in the service of Kṛṣṇa, and just as the** ***mañjaris*** **are always engaged in the service of Rādhā and Her eight chief** ***sakhis*****, we should also serve Kṛṣṇa, the divine Cupid, by satisfying His desires in spiritual bodies as divine females.**

**When bewildered by external considerations, the various goddesses or human women like Rudrāṇi, Indrāṇi, Brahmāṇi, Varuṇāni, Tārā, Bhārati, or Svāhā think, “My mortal husband is Rudra, Indra, Brahmā, or some other god or human.” However, when they realize their eternal spiritual nature, they recognize that Kṛṣṇa alone is their eternal husband and that Rādhārāṇi is His most beloved. Therefore the best way to serve Kṛṣṇa is to become the handmaiden of Śrimati Rādhārāṇi and Her girlfriends and servants.**

**Someone who offers everything he has to the Lord is said to be liberated. Someone who holds back on making such a sacrifice is said to be bound, or indifferent to the Lord’s service.**

*tomāra kanaka bhogera janaka*  
*kanakera dvāre sevaka mādhava*  
*kāminira kāma nahe tava dhāma*  
*tāhāra mālika kevala yādava*  
*vaiṣṇavi pratiṣṭhā tāte kara niṣṭhā*  
*tāhā nā bhajile labhibe raurava*

**“Your money is a source of sense enjoyment; use it to serve Mādhava. Desiring to charm women is not your business: Kṛṣṇa is their only true enjoyer. Be determined to attain the prestige of being a Vaiṣṇava. If you do not strive for this, you will end up in hell.”**

Please also read on page 194 about Lord Brahmā’s realizations upon receiving the *kāma-gāyatri-mantra*.

**Meditation**

**The moons on Kṛṣṇa’s body**

The syllables of the *kāma-gāyatri* can be contemplated as moons on Kṛṣṇa’s transcendental body. This meditation is explained by Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu to Śrila Sanātana Gosvāmi:

**Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, is identical with the Vedic hymn known as the** ***kāma-gāyatri*****, which is composed of twenty-four and a half syllables. Those syllables are compared to moons that arise in Kṛṣṇa. Thus all three worlds are filled with desire.**

**The face of Kṛṣṇa is the king of all moons, and the body of Kṛṣṇa is the throne. Thus the king governs a society of moons.**

**Kṛṣṇa has two cheeks that shine like glowing gems. Both are considered full moons. His forehead is considered a half moon, and the spot of sandalwood there is considered a full moon.**

**His fingernails are many full moons, and they dance on the flute in His hands. Their song is the melody of that flute. His toenails are also many full moons, and they dance on the ground. Their song is the jingling of His ankle bells.**

**Kṛṣṇa’s face is the enjoyer king. That full-moon face makes His shark-shaped earrings and lotus eyes dance. His eyebrows are like bows, and His eyes are like arrows. His ears are fixed on the string of that bow, and when His eyes spread to His ears, He pierces the hearts of the** ***gopis*****.**

**The dancing features of His face surpass all other full moons and expand the marketplace of full moons. Although priceless, the nectar of Kṛṣṇa’s face is distributed to everyone. Some purchase the moon rays of His sweet smiles, and others purchase the nectar of His lips. Thus He pleases everyone.**

**Kṛṣṇa has two reddish, widely spread eyes. These are ministers of the king, and they subdue the pride of Cupid, who also has beautiful eyes. That face of Govinda, which is full of happiness, is the home of the pastimes of beauty, and it is very pleasing to everyone’s eyes.**

In other words, there are twenty-four-and-a-half moons on Kṛṣṇa’s transcendental body, and they are compared to the twenty-four-and-a-half syllables in the *kāma-gāyatri:*

\- Kṛṣṇa’s face

\- His two cheeks

\- the beautiful tilaka mark on His forehead

\- His ten fingertips

\- His ten toenails

\- and His forehead is the half moon.

If you would like to know why the *kāma-gāyatri-mantra* contains a half syllable, please see page 290 for a detailed explanation.

**Meditation on Kāmadeva Kṛṣṇa**

Instead of coming under the grip of Kṛṣṇa’s reflection (the mundane god of love) and trying in vain to satisfy the material senses, which are dumb coverings of our spiritual ecstatic body, we are meant to satisfy the master of the senses, the transcendental Kāmadeva, and enjoy *rasa* in our constitutional position with Him.

A good practice that can help us come closer to this end is to chant the *kāma-gāyatri* with full concentration and awareness of the mantra’s meaning as an offering of love to Kṛṣṇa. Śri Rāmānanda Rāya explains this idea in his conversation with Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu:

*vṛndāvane ‘aprākṛta navina madana’*  
*kāma-gāyatri kāma-bije yāṅra upāsana*

**In the spiritual realm of Vṛndāvana, Kṛṣṇa is the spiritual, ever-fresh Cupid. He is worshiped by the chanting of the** ***kāma-gāyatri-mantra,*** **with the spiritual seed** ***kliṁ*****.**

Śrila Prabhupāda offers the following inspiring purport to this verse:

**The spiritual realm of Vṛndāvana is always spiritual. The goddess of fortune and the** ***gopis*** **are always present there. They are Kṛṣṇa’s beloveds, and all of them are as spiritual as Kṛṣṇa. In Vṛndāvana, Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Person and is the husband of all the** ***gopis*** **and the goddess of fortune. The trees in Vṛndāvana are wish-fulfilling trees. The land is made of touchstone, and the water is nectar. Words are musical vibrations, and all movements are dancing. The flute is the Lord’s constant companion. The planet Goloka Vṛndāvana is self-luminous like the sun and is full of spiritual bliss. The perfection of life lies in tasting that spiritual existence; therefore everyone should cultivate its knowledge. In Vṛndāvana, spiritual cows are always supplying spiritual milk. Not a single moment is wasted there – in other words, there is no past, present or future. Not a single particle of time is wasted. Within this material universe, the devotees worship that transcendental abode as Goloka Vṛndāvana. Lord Brahmā himself said, “Let me worship that spiritual land where Kṛṣṇa is present.” This transcendental Vṛndāvana is not appreciated by those who are not devotees or self-realized souls because this Vṛndāvana-dhāma is all spiritual. The pastimes of the Lord there are also spiritual. None are material.**

**According to Śrila Narottama Dāsa Thākura \[…\] one first has to be purified of all material desires and all attraction for fruitive activity and speculative knowledge if one wishes to understand Vṛndāvana. \[…\]**

**Everything in the material world is dull, but in the spiritual world everything is alive. Both Kṛṣṇa and His parts and parcels desire enjoyment. In the spiritual world, however, such desires are spiritual. No one should mistakenly consider such desires to be material. In the material world, if one is sexually inclined and enjoys sex life, his enjoyment is temporary. Enjoyment in the spiritual world never vanishes but is relished continuously. Sex pleasure in the spiritual world appears to the enjoyer to be more and more relishable with each new facet of its expression. In the material world, however, sex enjoyment becomes distasteful after only a few moments. The taste for it never endures. Because Kṛṣṇa appears very much sexually inclined, He is called the new Cupid in the spiritual world. There is no material inebriety in such desire, however.**

***Gāyantaṁ trāyate yasmād gāyatri tvaṁ tataḥ smṛtā:*** **one who chants the** ***gāyatri-mantras*** **is gradually delivered from the clutches of matter. In other words, that which delivers one from material entanglement is called** ***gāyatri*****. The Madhya-lilā (22.125) of Caitanya-caritāmṛta provides an explanation of** ***gāyatri*****:**

*kāma-gāyatri-mantra-rūpa, haya kṛṣṇera svarūpa,*  
*sārdha-cabbiśa akṣara tāra haya*  
*se akṣara ‘candra’ haya, kṛṣṇe kari’ udaya,*  
*trijagat kailā kāmamaya*

**“Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, is identical with the Vedic hymn known as the** ***kāma-gāyatri*****, which is composed of twenty-four and a half syllables. Those syllables are compared to moons that arise in Kṛṣṇa. Thus all three worlds are filled with desire.”**

**The** ***kāma-gāyatri-mantra*** **is just like a Vedic hymn, but it is the Supreme Personality of Godhead Himself. There is no difference between the** ***kāma-gāyatri*** **and Kṛṣṇa. Both are composed of twenty-four and a half transcendental syllables. The mantra depicted in letters is also Kṛṣṇa, and the mantra rises just like the moon. Due to this, there is a perverted reflection of desire in human society and among all kinds of living entities. In the** ***kāma-gāyatri*** **Kṛṣṇa is called Kāma-deva, Puṣpa-bāṇa and Anaṅga. Kāma-deva is Madana-mohana, the Deity who establishes our relationship with Kṛṣṇa; Puṣpa-bāṇa (“He who carries an arrow made of flowers”) is Govinda, the Personality of Godhead who accepts our devotional service; and Anaṅga is Gopijanavallabha, who satisfies all the** ***gopis*** **and is the ultimate goal of life. This** ***kāma-gāyatri*** **simply does not belong to this material world. When one is advanced in spiritual understanding, he can worship the Supreme Personality of Godhead with his spiritually purified senses and fulfill the desires of the Lord.**

## VII. The examples

## 1. Śri Kṛṣṇa, the ideal gṛhastha

The spiritual path becomes easier to imagine when we hear examples of successful practitioners who can both inspire us and show us the way.To teach all human beings about the importance of chanting gāyatri, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śri Kṛṣṇa, chanted the mantras Himself while performing His transcendental pastimes in Dvārakā:

**Lord Kṛṣṇa would immediately get up from bed exactly at the appearance of** ***brahma-muhūrta*****. An ideal householder should learn from the behavior of Lord Kṛṣṇa how to rise early in the morning, however comfortably he may be lying in bed embraced by his wife.**

**After rising from bed, Lord Kṛṣṇa would wash His mouth, hands and feet and would immediately sit down and meditate on Himself. \[…\] He was teaching us that** ***brahma-muhūrta*** **should be utilized for meditation on Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. By doing so Kṛṣṇa would feel very much satisfied and similarly we will also feel transcendentally pleased and satisfied, if we use the** ***brahma-muhūrta*** **period to meditate on Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa and if we think of how Śri Rukmiṇi Devi and Kṛṣṇa acted as ideal householders to teach the whole human society to rise early in the morning and immediately engage in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. \[…\]**

**After His meditation, the Lord would regularly bathe early in the morning with clear sanctified water, then He would change into fresh clothing, cover Himself with a wrapper and then engage Himself in His daily religious functions.**

**Out of His many religious duties, the first was to offer oblations into the sacrificial fire and silently chant the** ***gāyatri-mantras.*** **Lord Kṛṣṇa as the ideal householder executed all the religious functions of a householder without deviation. When the sunrise became visible the Lord would offer specific prayers to the sun-god.**

When Nārada Muni came to visit Kṛṣṇa in His various palaces in Dvārakā, he witnessed the Lord chanting *gāyatri*:

**Somewhere Lord Kṛṣṇa was observing the rituals for worship at sunset by refraining from speech and quietly chanting the** ***gāyatri-mantras*****.**

## 2. Śri Gaurāṅga in search of Gopāla

Before Caitanya Mahāprabhu started the *saṅkirtana* movement in Navadvipa, He went to Gayā to offer respects to the departed soul of His father, Jagannātha Miśra. There He received initiation into the sacred *gopāla-mantra* from His spiritual master, Iśvara Puri. Immediately, He found Himself filled with such an indescribable urge to seek out Lord Madana-gopāla, the mantra’s Deity, that He was prepared to go instantly to Vṛndāvana, even though it was already nighttime. Only the devas, who requested Him to postpone His visit to Vraja, could prevent Him from abandoning everything and beginning His pilgrimage immediately.

During His return journey to Navadvipa, Caitanya Mahāprabhu passed through the village Kanāi Nāṭaśālā. There, Lord Madana-gopāla briefly revealed Himself and then disappeared. This incident threw Mahāprabhu into an ocean of separation, and from that day onward, He urgently sought out the Lord of His heart. From this incident, Lord Gaurāṅga’s *saṅkirtana* movement was born, saturated with the mood of separation and an urgent search for Kṛṣṇa.

Please remember the first aspect of the *gāyatri-mantras’* “twofold blessing” described by Śrila Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākura (see page 5): “One aspect is that it tends to make the pure soul run after all-attractive Śri Kṛṣṇa, the Lord of Gokula and the divine milkmaids. This is the acme of the spiritual tendency of *jivas*.” Lord Śri Gaurāṅga, playing the role of His own devotee, offered the perfect example of this: After having received the mantra He was prepared on the spot to run and meet Kṛṣṇa in Vṛndāvana.

This description also reminds us of Parikṣit Mahārāja. After seeing Lord Kṛṣṇa only once while he was still in his mother Uttarā’s womb, he spent the rest of his life searching for Him, examining every face to see if it was Kṛṣṇa’s.

Such longing in separation was the mood of Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s entire lilā, not only after his experience in Kanai Nāṭaśālā, where this mood manifested externally for the first time. Even before that incident, Caitanya Mahāprabhu was always feeling Śrimati Rādhārāṇi’s mood of hankering for Kṛṣṇa – that was His actual reason to appear as Lord Gaurāṅga. Only after His experience in Kanai Nāṭaśālā, however, did He formally begin His *saṅkirtana* movement, beginning with His nocturnal *kirtanas* in Śrivāsa Thākura’s house. Later, these *kirtanas* were taken out into Navadvipa’s streets and to other places.

In this way the *gopāla-mantra* played a crucial role in the inauguration of the saṅkirtana movement.

## 3. Brahmā receives the Gāyatri-mantras

We have already discussed the “twofold blessing” bestowed upon Lord Brahmā at his initiation into the *gāyatri-mantras*. Now let us hear more about this incident, since it reveals remarkable details both about the subtle way in which Brahmā received the transcendental sound vibration, and about the mantra’s wonderful potencies:

**On coming out of the lotus, Brahmā, being guided by the divine potency, tuned his mind to the act of creation under the impulse of previous impressions. But he could see nothing but darkness in every direction.**

**Then the goddess of learning Sarasvati, the divine consort of the Supreme Lord, spoke to Brahmā who saw nothing but gloom in all directions, “O Brahmā, this mantra \[*****gopāla-gāyatri*****\] will assuredly fulfill your heart’s desire. Practice spiritual association by means of this mantra; then all your desires will be fulfilled.”**

**After hearing this message, Brahmā engaged in austerities for a long time in order to please Govinda, Kṛṣṇa, the Lord of** ***śvetadvipa*****. He meditated on Lord Kṛṣṇa like this:**

**“There exists a divine lotus of a thousand petals, augmented by millions of filaments, in the transcendental land of Goloka. On its whorl, there exists a great divine throne on which is seated Śri Kṛṣṇa, the form of eternal effulgence of transcendental bliss, playing on His divine flute resonant with the divine sound, with His lotus mouth. He is worshiped by His amorous milkmaids with their respective subjective portions and extensions and also by His external energy \[who stays outside\] embodying all mundane qualities.”**

After meditating on the *gopāla-gāyatri* for long time, Lord Brahmā became initiated for a second time, namely into the *kāma-gāyatri*:

**Then Gāyatri Devi, mother of the Vedas, being made manifest by the divine sound of Śri Kṛṣṇa’s flute, entered into the lotus mouth of Brahmā through his eight ear holes. Brahmā having thus received the** ***gāyatri-mantra*** **attained the status of the twice-born, having been initiated by the supreme primal** ***guru*****, Śri Kṛṣṇa Himself.**

**Enlightened by the remembrance of that mantra, Brahmā became acquainted with the ocean of truth. Then he worshiped Śri Kṛṣṇa, the essence of all Vedas, with this hymn.**

“This hymn” comprises Brahmā’s famous prayers to Lord Govinda: *govindam ādi-puruṣaṁ tam ahaṁ bhajāmi*. Just see how these wonderful prayers are a direct result of the transcendental realization Lord Brahmā gained by chanting the *gāyatri-mantras*. By the mantras’ potency, he received a complete vision of Śri Goloka Vṛndāvana. Śrila Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākura explains this power as follows:

**The sound of Kṛṣṇa’s flute is the transcendental blissful sound; hence the original import of all Veda is present in it. The** ***gāyatri*** **is Vedic rhythm. It contains a brief meditation and prayer.** ***Kāma-gāyatri*** **is the highest of all the** ***gāyatri-mantras*****, because the meditation and prayer contained in it are full of Kṛṣṇa’s transcendental** ***lilās*****, which are not to be found in any other** ***gāyatri*****. \[…\]**

**In this** ***gāyatri*****, the realization of the transcendental pastimes of Śri Gopijana-vallabha after perfect meditation and the prayer for the attainment of the transcendental god of love are indicated.**

In other words, *kāma-gāyatri*, being generated from Kṛṣṇa’s flute, leads the devotees directly to Kṛṣṇa’s intimate pastimes in Vṛndāvana. Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākura further describes what Brahmā experienced after gaining these realizations:

**Brahmā thought thus within himself, “By the recollection of** ***kāma-gāyatri*** **it seems to me that I am the eternal maidservant of Kṛṣṇa.” Though the other mysteries in regard to the condition of the maidservant of Kṛṣṇa \[in other words: the details of his particular** ***svarūpa*****\] were not revealed to him, Brahmā, by dint of his searching self-consciousness, became well acquainted with the ocean of truth.**

## 4. Bharata Mahārāja meditates on the supreme sun-god

After the great world emperor, Bharata Mahārāja, had abandoned his kingdom to spend his time exclusively practicing devotional service, he attained astonishing states of God consciousness. Śukadeva Gosvāmi describes:

**Naturally, his love for Vāsudeva, Kṛṣṇa, increased more and more and melted his heart. Consequently he gradually lost all attachment for regulative duties. The hairs of his body stood on end, and all the ecstatic bodily symptoms were manifest. Tears flowed from his eyes, so much so that he could not see anything. Thus he constantly meditated on the reddish lotus feet of the Lord. At that time, his heart, which was like a lake, was filled with the water of ecstatic love. When his mind was immersed in that lake, he even forgot the regulative service to the Lord.**

In his ecstatic love for the Lord, Bharata Mahārāja recited a prayer to Lord Nārāyaṇa situated in the sun, each morning as the sun rose. Śrila Prabhupāda explains:

**The predominating Deity within the sun is Hiraṇmaya, Lord Nārāyaṇa. He is worshiped by the** ***gāyatri-mantra*****. \[…\] In the Ṛg Veda, the predominating Deity of the sun is worshiped by this mantra:**

*dhyeyaḥ sadā savitṛ-maṇdala-madhya-varti*  
*nārāyaṇaḥ sarasijāsana-sanniviṣṭaḥ*

**Nārāyaṇa sits on His lotus flower within the sun. \[…\] Actually Nārāyaṇa within the sun is maintaining the entire universe; therefore Nārāyaṇa should be worshiped by the** ***gāyatri-mantra*** **or the** ***Ṛg mantra*****.**

This incident also proves Śrila Prabhupāda’s statement on page 148 that the supreme sun-god, worshiped in the *brahma-gāyatri*, is Kṛṣṇa, or Nārāyaṇa.

## 5. Dhruva Sees the Lord through mantra-dikṣā

The Śrimad-Bhāgavatam tells the amazing story of five-year-old Dhruva, who left his home to search for Nārāyaṇa because only the Lord could fulfill his burning desire to obtain a kingdom greater than that of his grandfather. Following his mother Suniti’s instruction, Dhruva performed mystic meditation in a secluded forest on the bank of the Yamunā. Upon hearing of this, Śri Nārada Muni approached Dhruva and advised him to go to Madhuvana in Vraja-bhūmi and chant the twelve-syllable mantra, *oṁ namo bhagavate vāsudevāya*.

Accepting Nārada’s instruction and then performing incredibly rigid austerities, little Dhruva quickly received the Lord’s *darśana*. Śri Viṣṇu actually felt impelled to appear before his worshiper, urged by the fervent prayers of the demigods whose existence was threatened by Dhruva’s unprecedented *tapasya*. Maitreya ṛṣi describes:

**When Dhruva Mahārāja became practically one in heaviness with Lord Viṣṇu, the total consciousness, due to his fully concentrating, and closing all the holes of his body, the total universal breathing became choked up, and all the great demigods in all the planetary systems felt suffocated and thus took shelter of the Supreme Personality of Godhead.**

After hearing the demigods’ prayers, Lord Viṣṇu replied:

**My dear demigods, do not be perturbed by this. It is due to the severe austerity and full determination of the son of King Uttānapāda, who is now fully absorbed in thought of Me. He has obstructed the universal breathing process. You can safely return to your respective homes. I shall stop this boy in his severe acts of austerities, and you will be saved from this situation.**

What gave little Dhruva the strength to overpower the mighty demigods and gain control of the total cosmic energy? What made him compel the Lord to appear before him? Was it the vigor of his austerities? Śrila Jiva Gosvāmi explains:

**The** ***sādhaka*** **first becomes trained and purified through the process of becoming twice-born. Afterwards, through** ***mantra-sādhana*** **he attains** ***darśana*** **of the presiding Deity of the mantra.**

**For example, Dhruva Mahārāja left his home at the age of five years old to worship Śri Nārāyaṇa, although he had no knowledge of the procedure for His worship. Nārāyaṇa, who dwells in the hearts of all, sent Devarṣi Nārada to Dhruva, who was staying on the bank of the Yamunā at Mathurā. When Śri Nārada saw Dhruva’s firm determination, he bestowed** ***mantra-dikṣā*** **upon him and told him, “Go to Madhuvana and perform** ***bhajana*** **of Śri Bhagavān there. He will give you His** ***darśana*** **very soon.” As a result of performing severe** ***sādhana*** **in accordance with Nārada’s instructions, Dhruva received the Lord’s** ***darśana*** **within six months.**

**This history illustrates that the acceptance of** ***dikṣā*** **is absolutely necessary in order to perform the** ***sādhana*** **and** ***bhajana*** **for realizing the Supreme Lord.**

In other words, Dhruva’s rigid *tapasya* was only a by-product of his *sādhana*, which he performed according to Nārada Muni’s instructions. The main source of the potency that brought him the Lord’s *darśana* was his *dikṣā* by Nārada Muni and his *mantra-sādhana*.

## 6. Gopa-kumāra’s journey to Goloka Vṛndāvana

Our last example, the story of Gopa-kumāra from Bṛhad-Bhāgavatāmṛta, offers the most detailed glorification of the *gāyatri-mantras*. Although describing only the *gopāla-gāyatri*, the points made here can easily be applied to the other mantras. The Bṛhad-Bhāgavatāmṛta is an elaborate explanation of the wonderful world of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, in which the form, qualities, and pastimes of the Lord of Vṛndāvana are fully revealed. Śrila Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākura praised the Bṛhad-Bhāgavatāmṛta by saying it embodied the “final essence of all the books of instructions.” Śrila Prabhupāda also highly appreciated this work:

**Śri Sanātana Gosvāmi Prabhu, the teacher of the science of devotional service, wrote several books, of which the Bṛhad-Bhāgavatāmṛta is very famous; anyone who wants to know about the subject matter of devotees, devotional service and Kṛṣṇa must read this book.**

Once, while herding his cows near Govardhana Hill, Gopa-kumāra, a cowherd boy, met a great spiritual personality who was fully absorbed in Kṛṣṇa conscious mellows. Attracted by his saintly character and his singing and chanting of the Lord’s glories, Gopa-kumāra began to serve him. By starting to meet regularly, they developed an affectionate relationship. Out of love, the saint initiated Gopa-kumāra into chanting the *gopāla-mantra*, saying, “This mantra will fulfill all your desires.” Afterwards he fainted, overcome by ecstatic emotions. Upon regaining his external consciousness, Gopa-kumāra’s *guru* disappeared into the forest without explaining anything more.

Grateful, Gopa-kumāra began to regularly chant the *gopāla-mantra* with great faith. As a result he became purified and materially detached. He also developed an eagerness to find the Lord of the universe. Leaving behind family and friends, he started his spiritual search.

One day while wandering near Vṛndāvana, he observed a poor *brāhmaṇa* worshiping his *śālagrāma-śilā*. Attracted by the *brāhmaṇa’s* devotional service, Gopa-kumāra inquired from him, wondering about the meager conditions of the *brāhmaṇa’s* worship. The *brāhmaṇa* told him of the much more elaborate worship of the country’s ruler. In the ruler’s city, Gopa-kumāra learned, there was a beautiful temple where the Supreme Lord was worshiped in great opulence. Irresistibly attracted, Gopa-kumāra traveled to that city (Mathurā) and began to live under the merciful protection of the local Vaiṣṇavas. The king, being childless, found such good qualities in Gopa-kumāra that he adopted him as his son. Consequently, after the king’s death Gopa-kumāra was appointed as the next king. Now he was able to serve the Lord personally in kingly opulence.

Throughout this time, however, Gopa-kumāra continued to chant his mantra; besides being moved by deep reverence for his *guru* he was also inspired by the amazing results he had received several times from his practice. Some time later he turned over his kingdom to other family members and went to Jagannātha Puri to worship Lord Jagannātha, the Lord of the universe, in great transcendental happiness. By the Lord’s arrangement, he was even crowned king of Puri.

Next Gopa-kumāra heard that the Lord dwells personally in the heavenly planets and is worshiped by Lord Indra. By his mantra’s power he quickly attained residence in the heavenly regions and was able to have the *darśana* of Lord Upendra (Vāmana Deva). After some time, the powerful devas requested him (on Lord Upendra’s advice) to occupy Indra’s post, since their current Indra had to hide himself due to some misconduct. As the new Indra, Gopa-kumāra was able to worship Lord Upendra in person.

It is important to note that Gopa-kumāra had never been properly educated and knew nothing about the earth and the universe. All he had was faith in the *gopāla-mantra*. We can also see that due to his simple nature and the mantra’s protection, Gopa-kumāra never became attached to the kingly opulence he was offered in dramatically increasing ways at the various steps in his journey. Rather, by the mercy of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, whom he regularly worshiped in his *gopāla-mantra*, he was protected from *māyā’s* obstructive influence.

Eventually Gopa-kumāra met his *guru* again, who blessed him with the following words:

*tvam etasya prabhāveṇa cira-jivi bhavānv-aham*  
*idṛg-gopārbha-rūpaś ca tat-phalāpty-arha-mānasaḥ*

**By the potency of this mantra may you live a very long time, may you always keep this appearance of a cowherd boy and may you develop the appropriate mentality for realizing the mature fruit of this mantra.**

These three blessings are intricate; they were intended to bring Gopa-kumāra’s spiritual journey to a successful end:

1\. That he would “live a long life” (*cira-jivi*) means he would be able to enjoy the realization of whatever he might desire, including elevation to higher planets where the lifespan is much longer than that on earth.

2\. That he would “keep this appearance of a cowherd boy” (*gopa-arbha*) means he would avoid both old age and physical illness by always remaining young. It also hints at the ultimate destination of his journey: the pasturing fields of the transcendental domain of Vraja-loka, where the Lord of the *gopāla-mantra* sports in His divine and complete manifestation of Śri Kṛṣṇa, the transcendental cowherd boy. In other words, this blessing assured Gopa-kumāra to go even beyond Vaikuṇṭha and become a personal associate of Śri Kṛṣṇa.

3\. That he would “develop the appropriate mentality for realizing the mature fruit of this mantra” (*tat-phala-āpti-arha-mānasaḥ*) means that his mind would not become confused or agitated by the immediate fulfillment of his various desires. Rather, the mantra would keep his intelligence fixed on the final goal: he would always continue to long for Vraja-bhūmi.

His *guru* also promised that he would appear at important stations in Gopa-kumāra’s life to provide further guidance.

It is interesting that Gopa-kumāra received instructions on how to chant his mantra only gradually, not all at once. If his *guru* had immediately given him all the details of his *sādhana*, he probably would have quickly perfected it. His fate, however, was to first experience a number of material and spiritual positions.

Gopa-kumāra’s journey is unique; his search for the *gopāla-mantra’s* Deity made him travel through the higher planetary systems and offered him high material and spiritual positions and perfections. He even became Lord Brahmā, and also learned to directly see various forms of God through meditation.

His innate simplicity (*saralatā*) allowed Gopa-kumāra to accept all this only in relationship to his devotional service. His continued chanting of the powerful *gopāla-mantra* protected him; he never became distracted by material or spiritual power. It is wonderful to see that no obstacle – material or spiritual – could obstruct the gradual development of his attraction to Lord Madana-gopāla. His beloved Lord briefly revealed Himself several times along with His unique pastimes, and these revelations urged Gopa-kumāra to continue his journey.

Every time Gopa-kumāra met his *guru*, he received more detailed instructions on how to chant his mantra. He learned to mark his body with the mantra’s syllables and to systematically meditate on its meaning. Other great personalities, such as Nārada Muni, appeared as his *śikṣā-gurus* and described Madana-gopāla’s beautiful form, qualities, and supremely enchanting Vṛndāvana pastimes.

A significant turning point in Gopa-kumāra’s journey occurred when he met the Viṣṇudūtas and received instruction from them. Glorifying the supreme benefits of *nāma-saṅkirtana* they advised him that chanting the holy name is the highest spiritual practice. Among the many reasons they offered, two were particularly convincing for him:

1\. *Nāma-saṅkirtana* engages the senses of speech and hearing. Thus the mind, the king of all senses, is brought under constant control and can continually remember Kṛṣṇa.

2\. As a potent mantra attracts a distant object, *nāma-saṅkirtana* attracts the Supreme Personality of Godhead. In this way, by the Lord’s mercy, devotees who chant the holy name develop an extraordinary taste for their spiritual practice; as a rather insignificant, yet quite helpful by-product, they become free from both the manifest and unmanifest reactions of past *karma*.
