The Viṣṇudūtas also instructed Gopa-kumāra about the proper attitude while chanting the holy name: the mood of separation. One should become like the *cātaka* bird. Accepting water only from the clouds, never from the ground, the *cātaka* bird is calling desperately for rain during dry and cloudless weather. In the same way, one should chant in this dry and burning world of *samsāra* with intense longing for the soothing lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa, accepting no other shelter than His holy name.

Taking these instructions to heart, Gopa-kumāra became an enthusiastic chanter of the Lord’s holy names. Here is his favorite song:

*śri-kṛṣṇa gopāla hare mukunda*  
*govinda he nanda-kiśora kṛṣṇa*  
*hā śri-yaśodā-tanaya prasida*  
*śri ballavi-jivana rādhikeśa*

**O Śri Kṛṣṇa, Gopāla, Hari, Mukunda! Govinda! O Nanda-kiśora! Kṛṣṇa! O darling son of Śri Yaśodā, please show me Your favor! O life of the divine cowherd girls, O Lord of Rādhikā!**

Throughout his search Gopa-kumāra’s journey was again and again directed to Gokula Vṛndāvana, the Lord’s abode on this earth, which he perceived at each station with ever increasing spiritualized vision. Due to the holy *dhāma’s* transcendental influence, his understanding of Madana-gopāla grew gradually deeper until he perceived Him as the Lord of transcendental *rasa* surrounded by His extraordinary loving devotees.

The finale of the narration is overwhelmingly ecstatic. After Gopa-kumāra obtained the qualification to enter Goloka Vṛndāvana in the spiritual sky, he was allowed to meet Lord Madana-gopāla, the Lord of his beloved mantra, as He returned home from herding the cows. Inundated with transcendental emotion, the Lord embraced His dear servant and asked him, “Where have you been for so long?”

## 7. Conclusion

Just as the life of Haridāsa Thākura, the ācārya of the holy name, can greatly inspire a *sādhaka* in his chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa, the above examples can bestow us with renewed vigor in our chanting of *gāyatri*. We especially recommend that the readers absorb themselves in Gopa-kumāra’s nectarean story, Bṛhad-Bhāgavatāmṛta.

It is a great privilege that we have been allowed to receive such powerful mantras that are able to mold our life and understanding. Let us chant these mantras again and again in a humble and submissive mood, and try to enter deeper into their secrets. As an ocean gradually deepens into unfathomable depths, so these mantras are capable of revealing to the serious chanter deeper and deeper secrets, provided the chanter approaches them with both attention and dedication – full attention and full dedication!

## VIII. The Gayatri treasury.

**Sources of meditation and inspiration**

This chapter is like a transcendental treasure chest where you can find interesting and valuable gems and ornaments. Feel free to pick any of them up and use them for daily illumination and guidance in your *gāyatri-japa.*

## 1. Kṛṣṇa loves the brāhmaṇas

Śri Kṛṣṇa’s particular regard for the *brāhmaṇas* and their culture became evident when He received Nārada Muni in one of His palaces in Dvārakā:

**When the Lord noticed Nārada, He rose at once from Goddess Śri’s bed, bowed His crowned head at Nārada’s feet and, joining His palms, had the sage sit in His own seat. The Lord bathed Nārada’s feet and then put the water on His own head. Although Lord Kṛṣṇa is the supreme spiritual authority of the universe and the master of His devotees, it was proper for Him to behave in this way, for His name is Brahmaṇya-deva, “the Lord who favors the** ***brāhmaṇas*****.” Thus Śri Kṛṣṇa honored the sage Nārada by bathing his feet, even though the water that bathes the Lord’s own feet becomes the Ganges, the ultimate holy shrine.**

We can imagine how disturbed Nārada Muni must have felt to have had his feet bathed by his Lord. But Kṛṣṇa explained to him that His observance of religious principles, including showing respect for a *brāhmaṇa*, is meant to teach the world. Śrila Viśvanātha Cakravarti Thākura explains Lord Kṛṣṇa’s feelings as follows:

**As I state in the Bhagavad-gitā,** ***yad yad ācarati śreṣṭhas tat tad evetaro janaḥ*** **– Whatever a great person does, ordinary people follow. Thus I bathed your feet today in order to help propagate the principles of religion. \[…\] Do not think that you have committed an offense by allowing Me to bathe your feet today and accept the water as holy remnants. Just as a son does not offend his father by touching him with his foot while sitting on the father’s lap, so you should understand that in the same way, My son, you have not offended Me.**

Kṛṣṇa’s extraordinary affection for the *brāhmaṇas* and their culture was manifest in its entirety when He received His old *gurukula* friend Sudāmā in Dvārakā. This pastime can encourage us to make the best use of our sacred initiation, which will qualify us to please the Lord.It was during their time together, living with their *guru*, Sāndipani Muni, that Kṛṣṇa and Sudāmā became friends. After Sudāmā’s education was finished, he married and lived happily while fulfilling a *brāhmaṇa’s* six activities. However, he was unsuccessful in the last two of these activities, namely, receiving alms and distributing the accumulated wealth in charity. Let us hear the reason for that, and how the story continues, in Śrila Prabhupāda’s words:

**Although the brāhmaṇa friend of Lord Kṛṣṇa was a householder, he was not busy accumulating wealth for very comfortable living; therefore he was satisfied by the income that automatically came to him according to his destiny. Because their material condition was not very opulent, they were not even eating sufficiently, and thus both he and his wife appeared very thin. The wife was not anxious for her personal comfort, but she felt concerned for her husband. Thus she spoke as follows:**

**“My dear lord, I know that Lord Kṛṣṇa is your personal friend. You are also His devotee, and He is always ready to help His devotee. Please, therefore, go to Him. I am sure that He will immediately understand your impoverished position. You are a householder; therefore without money you are in distress. But as soon as He understands your position, He will certainly give you sufficient riches so that you can live very comfortably. I think that in your case He knows perfectly well how much you are fixed in devotional service. Therefore He will not hesitate to award you some material benefit for the bare necessities of life.”**

**The** ***brāhmaṇa*** **thought that there was no need to ask any material benefit from Lord Śri Kṛṣṇa, but he was induced by the repeated requests of his wife. Moreover, he thought, “If I go there I shall be able to see the Lord personally. That will be a great opportunity, even if I don’t ask any material benefit from Him.”**

**When he had decided to go to Kṛṣṇa, he asked his wife if she had anything in the home that he could offer to Kṛṣṇa, because he must take some presentation for his friend. The wife immediately collected four handfuls of chipped rice from her neighboring friends and tied it in a small cloth, like a handkerchief, and gave it to her husband to present to Kṛṣṇa. Without waiting any longer, the** ***brāhmaṇa*** **took the presentation and proceeded toward Dvārakā to see his Lord.**

**It was of course very difficult to reach the palaces of the kings of the Yadu dynasty, but brāhmaṇas were allowed to visit. The** ***brāhmaṇa*** **entered one palace that was very gorgeously decorated.**

**At that time, Lord Kṛṣṇa was sitting on the bedstead of Queen Rukmiṇi. Even from a considerable distance He immediately left His seat and came forward to receive His** ***brāhmaṇa*** **friend and, upon reaching him, embraced the** ***brāhmaṇa*** **with His two arms. Then He had him seated on His own bedstead and personally brought him all kinds of fruits and drinks to offer him, as is proper in receiving a worshipable guest. He washed the** ***brāhmaṇa’s*** **feet and, for His own purification, sprinkled the water onto His head. After this the Lord smeared the body of the** ***brāhmaṇa*** **with different kinds of scented pulp, such as sandalwood, aguru and saffron. He burned several kinds of scented incense and, as is usual, offered him** ***ārati*** **with burning lamps. After thus offering him an adequate welcome and after the** ***brāhmaṇa*** **had taken food and drink, Lord Kṛṣṇa said, “My dear friend, it is a great fortune that you have come here.”**

**After the** ***brāhmaṇa*** **had been received nicely and seated on Lord Kṛṣṇa’s own cushioned bed, he and Kṛṣṇa took each other’s hands and began to talk about their early life, when they had both lived under the protection of the** ***gurukula*****.**

**Lord Kṛṣṇa engaged for a long time in talking with Sudāmā Vipra about their past association. Then, just to enjoy the company of an old friend, He began to smile and asked, “My dear friend, what have you brought for Me? Has your wife given you some nice eatable for Me?” While addressing His friend, Lord Kṛṣṇa looked upon him and smiled with great love. He continued, “My dear friend, you must have brought some presentation for Me from your home.”**

**Lord Kṛṣṇa knew that Sudāmā was hesitating to present Him the paltry chipped rice, which was actually unfit for His eating. Understanding the mind of Sudāmā Vipra, the Lord said, “My dear friend, I am certainly not in need of anything, but if My pure devotee offers Me even the most insignificant things – a little flower, a little piece of leaf, a little water – but saturates the offering in devotional love, then not only do I gladly accept such an offering, but I eat it with great pleasure.”**

**Lord Kṛṣṇa assured Sudāmā Vipra that He would be very glad to accept the chipped rice he had brought from home, yet out of great shyness, Sudāmā Vipra hesitated to present it to the Lord. He was thinking, “How can I offer such insignificant things to Kṛṣṇa?” and he simply bowed his head.**

**Lord Kṛṣṇa knew the reason for Sudāmā Vipra’s coming to Him. He knew that, driven by extreme poverty, he had come there at the request of his wife. Thinking of Sudāmā as His very dear class friend, He knew that Sudāmā’s love for Him as a friend was never tainted by any desire for material benefit. He therefore decided that He would give more material opulence to Sudāmā Vipra than could be imagined even by the King of heaven.**

**He then snatched the bundle of chipped rice that was hanging on the shoulder of the poor** ***brāhmaṇa*****, packed in one corner of his wrapper, and said, “What is this? My dear friend, you have brought Me nice, palatable chipped rice!” He encouraged Sudāmā Vipra, saying, “I consider that this quantity of chipped rice will satisfy not only Me but the whole creation.”**

**While Lord Kṛṣṇa was speaking to Sudāmā Vipra, He ate one morsel of chipped rice from his bundle, and when He attempted to eat a second morsel, Rukmiṇi Devi, the goddess of fortune herself, checked the Lord by catching hold of His hand and saying, “My Lord, You are so kind to Your devotee that even this one morsel of chipped rice pleases You very greatly, and Your pleasure assures the devotee opulence both in this life and in the next.”**

**Sudāmā passed that night at the house of Lord Kṛṣṇa, and while there he felt as if he were living in a Vaikuṇṭha planet. He did not appear to have received anything substantial from Lord Kṛṣṇa while at His palace, yet he did not ask anything from the Lord. The next morning he started for his home, thinking always about his reception by Kṛṣṇa, and thus he merged in transcendental bliss. All the way home he simply remembered the dealings of Lord Kṛṣṇa, and he felt very happy to have seen the Lord.**

**The** ***brāhmaṇa*** **thought, “It is most pleasurable to see Lord Kṛṣṇa, who is most devoted to the** ***brāhmaṇas*****. How great a lover He is of the brahminical culture!”**

**Thinking in this way, the learned** ***brāhmaṇa*** **gradually reached his own home. But there he saw that everything was wonderfully changed. He saw that in place of his cottage there were big palaces made of valuable stones and jewels, glittering like the sun, moon and rays of fire. At intervals there were beautifully decorated parks, in which many beautiful men and women were strolling. In those parks there were nice lakes full of lotus flowers and beautiful lilies, and there were flocks of multicolored birds. Seeing the wonderful conversion of his native place, the** ***brāhmaṇa*** **began to think to himself, “How am I seeing all these changes? Does this place belong to me or to someone else? If it is the same place where I used to live, then how has it so wonderfully changed?”**

**While he was considering this, a group of beautiful men and women with features resembling those of the demigods, accompanied by musical chanters, approached to welcome him. All were singing auspicious songs. The wife of the** ***brāhmaṇa*** **was very glad on hearing the tidings of her husband’s arrival, and with great haste she also came out of the palace. She appeared so beautiful that it seemed as if the goddess of fortune herself had come to receive him. With great love and affection she bowed down before her husband, and within herself she thought of embracing him. The** ***brāhmaṇa*** **was surprised to see his wife so beautiful, and in great affection and without saying a word he entered the palace with her.**

**When the** ***brāhmaṇa*** **entered his personal apartment in the palace, he saw that it was not an apartment but the residence of the King of heaven. When the** ***brāhmaṇa*** **saw his position suddenly changed to one of opulence, and when he could not determine the cause for such a sudden change, he began to consider very gravely how it had happened.**

**He thus began to think, “From the beginning of my life I have been extremely poverty-stricken, so what could be the cause of such great and sudden opulence? I do not find any cause other than the allmerciful glance of my friend Lord Kṛṣṇa. Certainly these are gifts of His causeless mercy. I simply offered Him a morsel of chipped rice, and in exchange He has given me opulence greater than the that of the King of heaven.”**

**Sudāmā Vipra was a qualified** ***brāhmaṇa*****, unattached to all sorts of material sense enjoyment, and at the same time a great devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Śri Kṛṣṇa is very fond of a** ***brāhmaṇa*** **like Sudāmā Vipra, and we have seen by the actual behavior of the Lord how much He adores such a** ***brāhmaṇa*****. Therefore, the ideal stage of human perfection is to become a** ***brāhmaṇa-vaiṣṇava*** **like Sudāmā Vipra.**

**śukadeva Gosvāmi has stated that all persons who hear this history will know how affectionate Kṛṣṇa is to the** ***brāhmaṇa*** **devotees like Sudāmā. Therefore anyone who hears this history gradually becomes as qualified as Sudāmā Vipra, and he is thus transferred to the spiritual kingdom of Lord Kṛṣṇa.**

## 2. How to accept a guru

In the following lecture, Śrila Prabhupāda hints at some important principles we should observe when accepting a *guru*:

**The Vedic injunction is, “You must go to a spiritual master.”** ***Tad-vijñānārthaṁ sa gurum evābhigacchet.*** ***Abhigacchet*** **means, “you must.” There is no alternative. If you are actually interested in transcendental knowledge, you have to approach a** ***guru*****. And the human life is meant for** ***tad-vijñānam*****, for understanding transcendental knowledge.**

**How to learn from** ***guru*****, that is also stated.** ***Tad viddhi*****: try to learn from him …** ***tad viddhi praṇipātena*****. First of all surrender yourself. You cannot go to any person for learning anything where there is no sense of surrender. I accept a** ***guru*****, but in my mind I remain independent, “Oh, why shall I abide by the orders of** ***guru*****?” That means there is no surrender. There is no surrender. This is false. It is just to satisfy some motive, but that will not help. If you have got any duplicity in your mind, then you’ll never be successful in any field of activities. So the process is,** ***tad viddhi praṇipatena*****. One must be surrendered. And therefore, unless you find somebody where you can fully surrender, don’t accept a** ***guru*****. That is cheating. Unless you are fully confident that “I am surrendering to this person. He is superior to me. He can give proper knowledge…”**

**So therefore the process is before accepting a** ***guru*****, one must hear him at least for one year. And when one is convinced that “Here is actually a guru who can teach me,” then you accept him. Don’t accept whimsically. This system should stop now that somebody has been coming for three days and then I hear, “Prabhupāda, initiate him.” Why? First of all see whether he is fit for becoming a disciple; then recommend him. Otherwise, don’t recommend him. \[…\]**

**Therefore in the Hari-bhakti-vilāsa by Sanātana Gosvāmi it is directed that the spiritual master and the disciple must meet together at least for one year so that the disciple may also understand, “Here is a person whom I can accept as my** ***guru*****,” and the guru also can see, “Here is a person who is fit for becoming my disciple.” Then the business is nice. Because the business is** ***tad viddhi praṇipatena*****, one must be prepared to surrender. So unless that surrendering character is there, how can one become a disciple? It is not possible.**

**These are very difficult things. But because it is the age of Kali, a very difficult time, Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu has said,** ***kalau nāsty eva nāsty eva nāsty eva gatir anyathā*****. Simply chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra. This will clarify your heart. Then you can understand who is** ***guru*****, where to surrender, where to take lesson. In this way we can make it…but if we accept haphazardly, then there will be failure.**

**So** ***satyaṁ śaucaṁ dayā*****. To accept a** ***guru*****, there is also service. And to give service to an inferior person who is suffering, that is also service. That service is called** ***dayā*****, and the other service is called** ***seva*****. So if you want to realize God, then you have to do the other service,** ***seva*****, not** ***dayā*****. You cannot show your mercy to God. God is full. There is no need of your showing mercy.** ***Sevonmukhe hi jihvādau svayam eva sphuraty adaḥ*****. If you want to know what is God, then do the other service,** ***seva*****.**

**That service begins from the tongue. Generally, when we think of service, we think of our hand and legs. “I can render service with my hands, with my legs.” No. The science of understanding God says service begins with the tongue.** ***Ataḥ śri-kṛṣṇa-nāmādi na bhaved grāhyam indriyaiḥ*****. You cannot understand \[by the gross senses\] what is God, what is Kṛṣṇa, His name, His activities…*****nāma-ādi*** **means His name, form, qualities, activities, pastimes, entourage, so many things,** ***ādi*****.**

**Just like we are chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, but we cannot understand what is the meaning of Hare Kṛṣṇa. We cannot understand.** ***Na bhaved grāhyam indriyaiḥ. Indriyaiḥ*** **means the senses. There are so many people who are hearing this sound vibration, but why they’re not becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious? Because there is no** ***seva*****, there is no service. Therefore it is said,** ***ataḥ śri-kṛṣṇa-nāmādi na bhaved grāhyam indriyaiḥ.*** **But how it will be perceived by the indriyas, the senses?** ***Sevonmukhe hi jihvādau.*** **By service. And wherefrom the service begins? It begins with** ***jihvādau*****, with the tongue. It begins with the tongue. This is the service.**

“Don’t accept whimsically.” The *Muktāvali* confirms Śrila Prabhupāda’s statement. There it is said that *guru* and disciple should study one another’s nature for one full year. If after this period the guru is convinced of the disciple’s eligibility, only then will he bestow the sacred initiation upon his tested and proven disciple.

This examination time also offers the prospective disciple a chance to examine and, if necessary, strengthen his faith in his future *guru*. Only then will he be able to invest his full energy in their mutual relationship.

In some cases the disciple might find out that his attraction to a certain spiritual master was based on unrealistic expectations, passing impressions, superficial emotions, or even wrong understandings. In such a case the examination time protects the aspiring disciple from making the wrong choice – there is still time and opportunity to undertake a reorientation, based on a more matured understanding of the *guru*-disciple relationship.

It may not always be possible to maintain this ancient precept under modern circumstances, and certainly there are exceptional cases where both *guru* and disciple are so obviously qualified that it is all right to forgo the examination period. Still, we should adhere to the spirit of this injunction if we want to develop genuine and mature *guru*-disciple relationships. This is a serious matter, because the relationship must be based on loving seva, which requires a solid base of firm faith. Discipleship is not an insignificant consideration.

The examination period is important not only for the disciple but for the spiritual master. The scriptures repeatedly stress that the *guru* becomes responsible for his disciples’ behavior, which, as the following injunction underlines, is a huge commitment:

*rājñi cāmātyajā doṣaḥ patni-pāpaṁ sva-bhartari*  
*tathā śiṣyārjitaṁ pāpaṁ guruḥ prāpnoti niścitam*

**Like a king who reaps the results of his ministers’ activities, or a husband who shares in his wife’s misdeeds the spiritual master must accept a portion of the reactions from the disciple’s sins. That is certain.**

Of course, this does not minimize the disciple’s responsibility for his or her good and bad deeds. Devotional service is always based on free will.

**The qualities of a bona fide disciple**

The Hari-bhakti-vilāsa offers the following elaborate list of qualities a sincere disciple should aspire for:

\- Disciples should be devoted to the spiritual master’s lotus feet.

\- They should be fully capable of upholding the vows they have made to the spiritual master.

\- They should be engaged day and night in the Lord’s service with body, mind, and words.

\- They should desire to know the Absolute Truth.

\- They should be spotless in character, truthful, gentle, and polite, pleasing in appearance, and intelligent.

\- They should show proper respect to elders, to initiated Vaiṣṇavas, and to the Lord. They should be silent on material subjects and control their senses.

\- They should have no attraction to committing sinful activities.

The bona fide disciples should not possess the following characteristics:

\- They should not be lazy, dirty, sickly, continually afflicted or lamenting, angry, proud, lusty, and they should not covet material desires.

\- They should not reveal a mood of enjoyment. They should not indulge in sinful activities, such as meat-eating, intoxication, gambling, or illicit sex. They should not earn a living by unjust means.

\- They should not use vulgar or coarse language, be critical of others, or be miserly, malicious, devious, or envious. They should not give pain to others or be addicted to cruel activities.

\- They should not be ignorant, nor should they be proud of their material learning. They should not indulge in studying or speaking non-vaiṣṇava philosophies. They should not be addicted to over-eating.

The following rules of etiquette should be observed by bona fide disciples in relation to their *guru*:

\- They should fall down like an uprooted tree (*daṇdavat*) upon seeing the *guru*.

\- When the spiritual master approaches, they should face him. When he leaves, they should follow him.

\- They should not leave the spiritual master’s presence without asking his permission.

\- They should not utter the spiritual master’s name inattentively but always with respect.

\- They should not imitate the gait, activities, or voice of the spiritual master.

\- They should always hold the spiritual master’s words in great esteem.

\- Although the spiritual master may punish them, they should not take that punishment as unkindness.

\- They should not bear hearing the spiritual master, the scriptures, or the Lord criticized. If they are faced with hearing such criticism, they should leave the place immediately.

\- They should not step on the spiritual master’s garland, bed, shoes, *āsana*, shadow, or eating table.

\- They should not spread their legs before the spiritual master, nor should they *yawn*, laugh, or make disrespectful noises in his presence.

\- They should not sit on an *āsana* or bed in front of the spiritual master.

\- They should not worship others in preference to the spiritual master.

\- They should not give *dikṣā*, scriptural explanations, or show superiority in front of the spiritual master.

\- They should not give orders to the spiritual master but should always obey him.

\- They should treat the spiritual master’s *guru* with similar respect.

\- They should treat the wife, son, or relatives of the spiritual master as if they were the spiritual master, but they should not clean the son’s body, take his remnants, or wash his feet.

## 3. Gāyatri and the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam

The *gāyatri-mantras* – especially the *brahma-gāyatri* – are closely connected to the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, which is regarded as a commentary on the *gāyatri-mantras* and will thus help us understand them better:

*artho ’yaṁ brahma-sūtrāṇāṁ bhāratārtha-vinirṇayaḥ*  
*gāyatri-bhāṣya-rūpo ’sau vedārtha-paribhṛṁhitaḥ*

**The meaning of the Brahma-sūtra \[Vedānta-sūtra\] is present in Śrimad-Bhāgavatam. The full purport of the Mahābhārata is also there. The commentary of the** ***brahma-gāyatri-mantra*** **is also there and fully expanded with all Vedic knowledge.**

Śrila Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākura makes the same point:

**The meaning or deep significance of the** ***gāyatri-mantra*** **has been presented in the first verse of the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, which is known as** ***maṅgalācaraṇa*****, or the auspicious invocation.**

Śrila Jiva Gosvāmi explains more clearly how the *brahma-gāyatri* is related to the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam:

*yasmād evaṁ bhagavat-paraḥ tasmād eva “yatrādhikṛtya gāyatrim”*  
*iti kṛta-lakṣaṇa-śri-bhāgavata-nāmā granthaḥ śri-bhagavat-parāyā*  
*gāyatryā bhāṣya-rūpo ’sau.*

**The Śrimad-Bhāgavatam is characterized as “beginning with the** ***brahma-gāyatri-mantra*****.” Thus it serves as a commentary \[*****bhāṣya*****\] to the** ***brahma-gāyatri*****. And since the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam is solely dedicated to Śri Bhagavān, Lord Kṛṣṇa, the** ***brahma-gāyatri*** **also is dedicated to Śri Kṛṣṇa.**

*brahmādināṁ buddhi-vṛtti-prerakatvenābhidhānād gāyatry-*  
*artho palakṣitena dhimatiti gāyatri-padenaiva yathopakramam*  
*upasaṁharan gāyatryā apy artho ’yaṁ grantha iti darśayati*

**By uttering the word** ***dhimahi*** **at the beginning (1.1.1) and at the end (12.13.19), Śrimad-Bhāgavatam refers to the** ***brahma-gāyatri-mantra*****, which is a prayer for enlightenment and is spoken by great personalities such as Lord Brahmā. In this way it is shown that Śrimad-Bhāgavatam is a commentary on the** ***brahma-gāyatri-mantra*****.**

How can we understand that Śrimad-Bhāgavatam begins with the *brahma-gāyatri*? Jiva Gosvāmi explains that each word of the *brahma-gāyatri* (except *devasya*) has a counterpart in the first *śloka* of the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, which further explains the meaning of the *brahma-gāyatri*. This is illustrated in the following chart:

**The** ***Brahma-gāyatri*** **and** ***Śrimad-Bhāgavatam 1.1.1***

*janmādy asya yato ’nvayād itarataś cārtheṣv abhijñaḥ svarāṭ*  
*tene brahma hṛdā ya ādi-kavaye muhyanti yat sūrayaḥ*  
*tejo-vāri-mṛdāṁ yathā vinimayo yatra tri-sargo ’mṛṣā*  
*dhāmnā svena sadā nirasta-kuhakaṁ satyaṁ paraṁ dhimahi*

**Brahma-gāyatri**  **Śrimad-Bhāgavatam 1.1.1**    *oṁ*  *janmādy asya yataḥ*    *bhūr bhuvaḥ svaḥ tat*   *yatra tri-sargo ’mṛśā*    *savitur*  *svarāṭ*    *vareṇyam*  *param*    *bhargaḥ*  *satyam*    *dhimahi*  *dhimahi*    *dhiyo yo naḥ pracodayāt*  *tene brahma hṛdā yaḥ*

Rādhāmohana Tarkavācaspati, a Gaudiya commentator from the eighteenth century, gives a slightly different schema in his commentary on the first verse. His correlations are as follows:

Brahma-gāyatri  Śrimad-Bhāgavatam 1.1.1    *tat savitur*  *janmādy asya yataḥ*  (*savituḥ* refers to root, origin of all)    *devasya*  *svarāṭ*  (the self-effulgent; not taking power from anywhere else)    *dhiyo yo naḥ pracodayāt*  *tene brahma hṛdā*  (relating to *pracodayāt* in the sense of “to inspire”)    *oṁ bhūr bhuvaḥ svaḥ*  *yatra tri-sargo ’mṛśā*  (*tri-sarga* means the three types of creation)    *bhargaḥ*  *satyam*  (the effulgent / maintainer)    *vareṇyam*  *param*  (the supreme, best)    *dhimahi*  *dhimahi*

Not only does the first verse of Śrimad-Bhāgavatam refer to *brahma-gāyatri*, but its four most essential verses – called *catuḥ-ślokāḥ* – are also related to this mantra. These four verses were originally spoken by Kṛṣṇa to Brahmā; they contain the entire truth of the Bhāgavatam in a nutshell. In His teachings to Prakāśānanda Sarasvati, Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu explains why Śrila Vyāsadeva compiled the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam as a commentary on the *gāyatri-mantras*:

**The meaning of the sound vibration** ***oṁkāra*** **is present in the** ***gāyatri-mantra.*** **The same is elaborately explained in the four ślokas of Śrimad-Bhāgavatam known as the** ***catuḥ-ślokāḥ*****.**

**Whatever was spoken by the Supreme Personality of Godhead to Lord Brahmā in those four verses of Śrimad-Bhāgavatam was also explained to Nārada by Lord Brahmā. Whatever Lord Brahmā told Nārada Muni was again explained by Nārada Muni to Vyāsadeva.**

**Śrila Vyāsadeva considered that whatever he had received from Nārada Muni as an explanation of** ***oṁkāra*** **he would elaborately explain in his book Śrimad-Bhāgavatam as a commentary on the Brahma-sūtra.**

Śrila Prabhupāda comments:

**The sound vibration** ***oṁkāra*** **is the root of Vedic knowledge. Oṁkāra is known as the** ***mahā-vākya*****, or supreme sound. Whatever meaning is in the supreme sound** ***oṁkāra*** **is further understood in the** ***gāyatri-mantra*****. Again, this same meaning is explained in Śrimad-Bhāgavatam in the four** ***ślokas*** **known as the** ***catuḥ-ślokāḥ*****. \[…\] The import of Vedic knowledge, the original word** ***praṇava*****, has been explained in Śrimad-Bhāgavatam. The conclusion is that the Brahma-sūtra \[and consequently the** ***brahma-gāyatri*** **as well\] is explained in Śrimad-Bhāgavatam.**

Let us examine how the Vedic conclusion is developed in its various stages:

1\. Vedic truth starts with its most condensed aphorism, the *oṁkāra*, the Vedic symbol ॐ.

2\. The same truth is further expressed as the *gāyatri-mantras*, especially the *brahma-gāyatri*.

3\. It then appears in the form of the Vedas.

4\. Thereafter this knowledge has been summarized as the Vedānta-sūtra, the essence of the Vedas.

5\. Finally, the Vedic conclusion is given its fullest expression in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, the spotless evidence by which one can know the Absolute Truth (*śrimad-bhāgavataṁ* *pramāṇam amalam*) and the perfect commentary on the Vedānta-sūtra.

## 4. The glories of the Gopāla-mantra

**A Summary of the first chapter of the Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad**

For those who wish to gain a deeper philosophical understanding of the *gopāla-mantra* and the *tattva* of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, the following discussion may be a valuable source of information. Of all the Upaniṣads, the Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad is considered the most authoritative regarding the confidential truth about Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa in Goloka Vṛndāvana. This is because this Upaniṣad’s presiding goddess has had first-hand experience in Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes: she has had personal access to that divine realm as a *gopi*.

The Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad describes the Supreme Lord Śri Kṛṣṇa’s transcendental form and pastimes. The title of this work means “the Upaniṣad that illuminates (*tāpani*) the truth of Gopāla, or Kṛṣṇa.” According to Viśvanātha Cakravarti Thākura’s Gopāla-tāpani-vivṛtti commentary, the Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad is part of the Atharva-veda.

Śrila Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa has also written a commentary on this Upaniṣad called Śri Gopāla-tāpani-bhāṣya. There he explains that the Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad specifically reveals the sweet mādhurya form of Kṛṣṇa in Vṛndāvana, something rarely found in the other Upaniṣads. He quotes the following verse to underline who is being referred to by the name Kṛṣṇa:

*kṛṣṇo varṇaś ca me yasmāt tasmāt kṛṣṇo ’ham arjuna*

**O Arjuna, because My complexion is blackish \[like a dark atasi flower, as Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa remarks\], I am known as Kṛṣṇa.**

This description of Vṛndāvana Kṛṣṇa is inseparable from the *gopāla-mantra*, which is specifically mentioned in the first chapter of the Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad. The text begins with the four Kumāras asking Lord Brahmā four questions:

1\. Who is the Supreme Deva?  
2\. Whom does death fear?  
3\. By knowing whom can everything else be fully known?  
4\. By whom is this world brought into being?

Lord Brahmā answers these questions by naming the four words that appear in the *gopāla-mantra*:

1\. Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Deva.  
2\. Death fears Govinda.  
3\. By knowing Gopijanavallabha, everything else becomes known.  
4\. By pronouncing the word *svāhā* the world became manifest.

Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa notes that by answering their questions in this way, Lord Brahmā actually initiated them into the *gopāla-gāyatri-mantra*. Since at the time of *dikṣā* the *guru* not only reveals the mantra’s identity but explains its significance, worship, and meditation, the reader of Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad can witness in this dialogue an authentic initiation process. We see how the *guru*, after having imparted the mantra, will offer further spiritual instruction based on his disciples’ questions. Being asked for further information about the meaning of the words, Lord Brahmā replies:

**1. Kṛṣṇa means He who delivers from sin (*****pāpa-karṣaṇa*****).**

**2. Govinda is the knower of the earth, the Vedas, and the surabhi cows of Vraja. He is famous among them.**

**3. Gopijanavallabha means “He who inspires the knowledge and the artful skills of the** ***gopis*****.”**

**4.** ***Svāhā*** **is the Lord’s potency by which everything is manifest.**

**All these names refer to the Supreme Personality of Godhead.**

Śrila Viśvanātha Cakravarti Thākura explains in this regard:

**1. The name** ***pāpa-karṣaṇa*** **\[which Brahmā used to explain the word Kṛṣṇa\] conveys two ideas:**

**a) Kṛṣṇa, whose transcendental pastimes attract the minds of even the sinful, conditioned souls;**

**b) Kṛṣṇa, the merciful Supreme Person who liberated Pūtanā, Agha, Keśi, and the other demons He killed.**

**2. Govinda means “the Supreme Lord who is famous among Vraja’s surabhi cows.” Govinda is glorified in the Vedas and famous on earth because He removed the burden of demoniac kings from the earth. Govinda also means “He who protects the cows, land, and Vedas.” Govinda is the supreme protector who rescues the living entities from the cruel hands of death.**

**3. Gopijanavallabha means “He who enraptures the** ***gopis*****, who are all manifestations of His** ***hlādini-śakti*****.” Gopijanavallabha enchants the** ***gopis*** **with His sweet beauty and other transcendental qualities. He removes their resistance to full surrender and gives them ecstatic love for Him. Therefore He has become very dear (*****vallabha*****) to them.**

**4. The word** ***svāhā*** **means the Lord’s potency by which everything is manifest.**

As Lord Brahmā explained before, death personified is afraid of Govinda, and that by knowing Gopijanavallabha one knows all things. Śrila Jiva Gosvāmi sheds more light on these two names in the *gopāla-mantra*:

1\. There is a difference between Kṛṣṇa’s killing demons in Vṛndāvana (as the cowherd boy Govinda), and in Dvārakā or Mathurā. As the royal descendent of the Yadu dynasty, He is more respectful and obedient to the moral codes of Vedic *dharma*; therefore, the demons He kills in Mathurā or Dvārakā are granted only ascendance to heaven, or at best liberation. In Vṛndāvana, however, Kṛṣṇa is free to act as He likes; no moral or social rules influence His *lilās*. Thus the demons killed there are sometimes even given the highest boon, pure love for Him. The best example is Pūtanā, who was elevated to the stage of parental love after having been killed by Kṛṣṇa.

For this reason, death is particularly afraid of Govinda, whose *lilās* can rescue even the most abominable demoniac sinner from his clutches.

2\. By quoting Gautamiya-tantra, Jiva Gosvāmi explains:

*gopiti prakṛtiṁ vidyāj janas tattva-sammūhakaḥ*

**The word** ***gopi*** **can be understood as** ***prakṛti*** **(material nature), and the word jana as its twenty-five elements.**

And *vallabha* means He who is the basis of these two, who pervades them and causes them to be manifest in the variety of material creation. Thus by knowing Gopijanavallabha one not only knows the most confidential aspects of the Absolute – the conjugal love between Kṛṣṇa and the *gopis*, by which everything else in Kṛṣṇa’s lilā becomes revealed – but one also becomes acquainted with the creator, maintainer, and sustainer of prakṛti and its manifestations. Thus one knows all things, material and spiritual.Lord Brahmā continues to explain: “By chanting the *gopāla-mantra*, which consists of five sections, one attains the Supreme Brahman, which consists of five limbs (*pañca-aṅgam*), namely, heaven, earth, sun, moon, and fire.” Prabodhānanda Sarasvati Thākura explains that while chanting the five sections of this mantra one should meditate on the presiding deities of the five respective limbs of Brahman, as indicated above (heaven, earth, etc.), knowing they are nothing but different forms of the Lord. He gives the following correlation between the mantra’s five *aṅgas* and those of Brahman:

**1. The Supreme Brahman’s fundamental name is Kṛṣṇa (*****kṛṣṇas tu bhagavān svayam*****). Thus the name Kṛṣṇa stands above all, as heaven stands above all in the material world.**

**2. Since Govinda enacts His** ***lilā*** **on earth, His name means that He gives joy to the earth and enhances her glory.**

**3. The word** ***gopijana*** **indicates Kṛṣṇa’s primeval** ***śakti*** **in the form of His gopis. In their presence He appears most complete. Thus gopijana can be related to the sun, the light of all lights.**

**4. Vallabha, the beloved of the gopis, bestows the highest pleasure upon them by playing His flute during the autumn full moon. Because the moon rays give joy to all living beings, vallabha is related to the moon.**

**5. Svāhā indicates the fire of sacrifice in complete self-surrender.**

Brahmā emphasizes that each of these four names refers to Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Brahman. Those who meditate on Him, chant His mantra \[the *gopāla-mantra*\] with relish, and worship Him become liberated. The path is open to all: those who sincerely desire to worship Kṛṣṇa by chanting the mantra have the *adhikāra* to do so (*tatra laulyam api mūlyam ekalam*).

It is worth considering that the Kumāras are far more than followers of the *jñāna-mārga*, which is how they are usually seen. They are pure *rasika-bhaktas*. As Śrila Rūpa Gosvāmi describes in his Śri Śri Rādhikā-stava: *sanaka-sanatana-varṇita-carite*: “Rādhā’s divine characteristics are described by Sanaka and Sanātana.” It should also be noted that they are said to be the founders of their own *sampradāya*, of which the *nimbārka-sampradāya* is very prominent in Vṛndāvana. Thus in this conversation they show great interest in *rādhā-kṛṣṇa-lilā*.

As Brahmā concludes, one should constantly chant the *gopāla-mantra* while meditating on the above description of the mantra’s five words. A person who even once chants this mantra will quickly attain Lord Kṛṣṇa’s association, because by such meditation one becomes liberated. Here, liberation means to become free from material desires, a stage that leads to the desire and ability to perform devotional service. Viśvanātha Cakravarti Thākura confirms:

*harāv aikāntiki bhaktir muktir ity abhidhiyate*

**Pure devotional service to Lord Hari is actual liberation.**

Viśvanātha Cakravarti Thākura also states that some brāhmaṇas chant the *gopāla-mantra* under the shelter of rules and regulations prescribed in *śāstra* (*vaidhi-mārga*), whereas others follow the path of spontaneous attraction (*rāga-mārga*). Those on the vaidhi path concentrate mainly on the name “Kṛṣṇa” in the *gopāla-mantra*, whereas those on the *rāga* path turn to the more confidential names, “Govinda” and “Gopijanavallabha.” This is expressed in text 15 of the Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad in the words *kṛṣṇaṁ yajanti*, “they worship Kṛṣṇa,” and, *govindam ārādhayanti*, “they worship Govinda.” According to Prabodhānanda Sarasvati Thākura, *yajanti* refers to ritualistic worship through sacrifice (emphasizing the *vaidhi-mārga*), whereas *ārādhayanti* refers to worship based on loving attraction (emphasizing the *rāga-mārga*). From this we can see that there is a gradation in the confidentiality of the names in the *gopāla-mantra*:

1\. The name Kṛṣṇa refers to the *lilās* of all His expansions in all *rasas*.

2\. The name Govinda is reserved exclusively for His *lilās* in Vṛndāvana. Thus it is more confidential than the name Kṛṣṇa.

3\. Whereas the name Govinda is relevant for all four *rasas* which are prominent in Vṛndāvana, the name Gopijanavallabha is directly related to *mādhurya-rasa*. Therefore the name Gopijanavallabha is the most confidential name in the *gopāla-mantra.* It appeals to those who are aspiring for the conjugal mood of the *gopis*.

In this connection it is significant that Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu chanted the ten-syllable mantra, *kliṁ gopi-jana-valla bhāya svāhā*, rather than the full eighteen-syllable *gopāla-mantra*, since He aspired exclusively for the *gopis*’ mood of conjugal love.

As we can see, by going through the names Kṛṣṇa, Govinda, and Gopijanavallabha, one ascends in a progression of sweetness and confidentiality. This ascension reflects the gradual progression of one’s *sādhana*, starting from *vaidhi-bhakti* (following rules and regulations) to *rāgānuga-bhakti* (following one’s reawakened desire to hear about Kṛṣṇa and chant His glories), until finally one reaches the stage of *bhāva*, ecstatic love of Kṛṣṇa.

To summarize Brahmā’s teachings, this mantra reveals the Supreme Lord, frees one from the fear of death, and gives all knowledge starting from the material plane and culminating in the most sublime *lilā* of Rādhā, Kṛṣṇa, and Their *sakhis*. It must be chanted in the mood of self-surrender – as indicated by the word *svāhā* – and with devotion to Kṛṣṇa.

Continuing to respond to the Kumāras’ questions, Lord Brahmā next describes the Gopāla-yantra, which is used for worshiping the Lord, and briefly explains the process of Deity worship. Brahmā concludes that to a worshiper who chants the *gopāla-mantra*, the Lord reveals His own transcendental form as a cowherd boy. He says that to benefit the living entities in the material world, Kṛṣṇa, who is originally one, appears as the five words of the *gopāla-mantra* just as air, which is originally one, appears as the body’s five *prāṇas*.

Brahmā also mentions that there are other *govinda-mantras*, such as the ten-syllable mantra chanted by Lord Indra and his followers, but all these other *govinda-mantras* have their origin in the five words of the *gopāla-mantra*.Being asked about the nature of the *gopāla-mantra* Brahmā recounts his own experiences with its unlimited creative power:

**I continually glorified the Lord and meditated upon Him for many millions of years. Finally I was able to understand the transcendental form of the Lord as Śri Kṛṣṇa, in the dress of a cowherd boy, and bowed down before Him. Being compassionate with me, He gave me the eighteen-syllable mantra, which is used for creation. Then He disappeared.**

**When I desired to create the universe He again appeared before me and showed me in these eighteen syllables the universe as it was to be. Then I started the act of creation by meditating on this mantra:**

**I created water from the letter** ***ka*****, earth from** ***la*****, fire from** ***i*****, the moon from the** ***bindu*****, and the sun from the entire word** ***kliṁ*****. From the word** ***kṛṣṇāya*****, I created ether, from** ***govindāya*****, air, from** ***gopijana-vallabhāya*****, knowledge and the** ***surabhi*** **cows. Finally, from** ***svāhā*****, I created men, women, and everything else.**

Brahmā mentions that according to some scriptures, earth was created from the first word (*kliṁ*), water from the second, fire from the third, air from the fourth, and ether from the fifth. However, the essential benefit of this mantra is not to attain creative potency but to obtain a revelation of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s original form. Therefore he concludes the first chapter of the Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad by offering eloquent prayers to Lord Govinda, the youthful cowherd boy who stands under a desire tree in Vṛndāvana and is nondifferent from the five-word *gopāla-mantra*. With his last instruction to the Kumāras he returns to their first question, “Who is the Supreme Deva?”

**As I worship Śri Kṛṣṇa, so should you. Chanting this five-word mantra, and meditating on Lord Kṛṣṇa, you will transcend the world of birth and death. One who chants this mantra will easily attain Śri Govinda’s transcendental abode.**

**Therefore, one should meditate upon Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Deva, glorify Him, serve Him, and worship Him.** ***Oṁ tat sat*****.**

**The** ***Gopāla-yantra***

When Lord Brahmā meditated on the sacred *gopāla-mantra*, he accepted help from a *yantra*, a symbolic representation of the truth expressed in the mantra. A *yantra* is a “transcendental machine,” a means to focus one’s concentration. By fixing one’s glance upon the yantra during *gāyatri-japa* one may experience how the consciousness opens. This esoteric practice, if properly applied, can act as a kind of gateway to spiritual dimensions. *Yantras* are helpful for concentration and offer transcendental revelation.The *gopāla-yantra* is described in the Brahma-saṁhitā as *ṣaṭ-koṇam*, a hexagonal figure. The *gopāla-gāyatri-mantra* with its six divisions is manifested within the six padas or fields of this figure (*ṣad-aṅga-ṣaṭ-padi-sthā-nam*); it is accompanied by the *kāma-bija kliṁ* (*kāma-bijena saṅgatam*). The Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad offers a further description of this *yantra*:

**The** ***yantra*** **should be designed as follows: It should be shaped as a golden, eight-petalled lotus (*****hairaṇya-aṣṭa-palāśam ambujam*****) with a figure in the whorl drawn from two inverted triangles (*****tad-antarālike anala-asra-yugam*****). In the center of this hexagon** ***kliṁ*** **should be written, the first and foremost of all syllables, followed by** ***kṛṣṇāya namaḥ*****.**

**Further, the mantra which gives full meaning to that** ***bija*** **(*****bija-artham*****) should be written within the** ***yantra*****. This is the** ***gopāla-mantra*****. The** ***anaṅga-gāyatri*** **\[*****kāma-gāyatri*****\] should also be properly placed.**

From these descriptions we can derive the following simple representation of the *yantra*. In the center is oṁ *kṛṣṇāya namaḥ,* inside the hexagon is the *gopāla-gāyatri*, and surrounding the hexagonal star is the *kāma-gāyatri*:

## 5. Healing and other blessings of the Gopāla-mantra

Although the *gāyatri-mantras* are meant to liberate the chanter from the material energy, their great power can also grant certain material blessings, as we have already explained by discussing the “twofold blessing” they invoke. The Krama-dipikā states particularly about the *gopāla- mantra*:

*sarveṣu varṇeṣu tathāśrameṣu*  
*nāriṣu nānāhvaya-janmabheṣu*  
*dāta phalānām abhivāñchitānāṁ*  
*drāg eva gopālaka-mantra eṣaḥ*

**The** ***gopāla-mantra*** **immediately gives the desired results to everyone, to all varṇas and** ***āśramas*****, to women, and to everyone born under every birth-star.**

One such materially inclined blessing offered in the Krama-dipikā, which can be usefully applied in our daily lives, is for healing. One can chant the *gopāla-mantra* with the aim of treating specific health problems or other such goals. These meditations are both easy to perform and effective. You may try them by meditating in the way prescribed in the following chart while chanting the *gopāla-mantra*. Then wait and see what results.

Let’s keep in mind, however, that the actual orientation in our life should be spiritual. We will never be able to adjust this world and turn it into somewhere comfortable, not even with *mantras* and *tantras*. The final solution for material suffering is to attain unalloyed *bhakti* and then become reinstated in our spiritual position. Although the meditations offered in the list below can be seen as a kind of “emergency first-aid,” their real values lie in redirecting our attention to Kṛṣṇa even in the midst of material disturbances.

**Concern**  **Meditation while chanting the Gopāla-mantra**    1 need for food  Gopāla-Kṛṣṇa is lying in His cradle, gently swayed to and fro by the *gopis* and being fed with milk    2 adversities / illness/ misfortune  the infant Kṛṣṇa is easily killing the demon Pūtanā    3 long life / good health  youthful Kṛṣṇa is carrying milk and curd in His hands    4 maladies caused by poisoning  Kṛṣṇa is dancing on the hoods of the serpent Kāliya.    5 fever among children  Kṛṣṇa is holding the duck demon Baka by the beak and tearing him to pieces    6 excessive rain / storm / lightning  Kṛṣṇa is holding up Giri Govardhana with His left hand and playing His flute with His right    7 drought / fever  Kṛṣṇa is sporting in the Yamunā river with the *gopis* and splashing water on them    8 vanquishing opponents   Lord Viṣṇu is riding on Garuda and routing His enemies in a great victory

## 6. Śrimati Rādhārāṇi in the Gāyatri-mantras

The ultimate orientation in our spiritual life should be to seek Śrimati Rādhārāṇi’s pleasure by becoming Her servant. Śrila Prabhupāda has made this point strongly:

*kṛṣṇa taba puṇya habe bhāi*  
*e-puṇya karibe jabe rādhārāṇi khusi habe*  
*dhruva ati boli tomā tāi*

**I emphatically say to you, O brothers, you will obtain your good fortune from the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa only when Śrimati Rādhārāṇi becomes pleased with you.**

It is impossible to attain the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa and the inner chambers of His devotional service without taking shelter of Śrimati Rādhārāṇi’s lotus feet. Śrila Raghunātha Dāsa Gosvāmi expresses this idea in a beautiful verse:

*anārādhya rādhā-padāmbhoja-reṇum*  
*anāśritya vṛndāṭaviṁ tat-padāṅkām*  
*asambhāsya tad-bhāva-gambhira-cittān*  
*kutaḥ śyāma-sindho rasasyāvagāhaḥ*

**How is it possible to immerse oneself in the ocean of** ***śyāma-rasa*** **\[love for Kṛṣṇa in** ***mādhurya-rasa*****\] without worshiping the dust of the lotus feet of Śrimati Rādhārāṇi, or without taking shelter of Vṛndāvana, which is decorated with her footprints, or without serving her devotees whose grave hearts are always absorbed in love for her.**

In his Śri Svaniyama-daśaka (“My Ten Vows”), Raghunātha Dāsa Gosvāmi promises:

**Not for a moment shall I go near a pretender who worships only Lord Govinda and does not worship the exalted Rādhārāṇi, whose glories are sung by the Vedas and the great sages headed by Nārada, and who is most dear to Kṛṣṇa. This is my vow.**

However, Śrimati Rādhārāṇi’s glories are hidden both in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam and in the *gāyatri-mantras.* This is because confidential subjects are meant to glow in one’s heart like a carefully protected lamp, hidden from the public eye. They are not to be exposed in an environment where the winds of opinion, disharmony, and misconception blow.

Also, there are those who do not have the spiritual *adhikāra* (qualification) and who therefore find it too difficult to understand Śrimati Rādhārāṇi. Śrila Prabhupāda cautioned us not to prematurely delve into intimate discussions about Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, or to speak about such confidential topics in public. Otherwise, we may fall prey to turning the highest into the lowest and thus making an unpardonable offense – either personally, or indirectly by inducing others to do so.

But Śrimati Rādhārāṇi is certainly present in the *gāyatri-mantras*, and by Her mercy we will one day obtain the *adhikāra* to perceive Her.

The easiest place to recognize Her presence is in the *kāma-gāyatri,* in which the transcendental *mādhurya-rasa* pastimes are revealed. Śrila Viśvanātha Cakravarti Thākura relates how he received Śrimati Rādhārāṇi’s personal *darśana* while studying the *kāma-gāyatri*. With Her help he was able to solve a mystery relating to the *kāma-gāyatri* that was disturbing him. Śrimati Rādhārāṇi told him the following:

**This** ***kāma-gāyatri-mantra*** **is meant for worshiping Me. Knowing the syllables of this mantra, one can know Me – but only by My mercy is this possible!**

Viśvanātha Cakravarti Thākura also explains that the *kāma-gāyatri* directly hints at the influence of all-attractive Kṛṣṇa on Rādhā and the *gopis*:

\- The word *kliṁ* indicates that Kṛṣṇa steals the minds of the *gopis* headed by Śrimati Rādhikā with the sweetness of His flute (*veṇu-mādhurya*).

\- The word *kāmadeva* indicates that Kṛṣṇa steals their discrimination with the sweetness of His pastimes (*lilā-mādhurya*).

\- The word *puṣpabāṇa* is used to indicate how Kṛṣṇa awakens their excitement upon meeting with Him in *sambhoga-rasa*. This happens by the sweetness of His lovely charm and other qualities (*lāvaṇya-guṇa-mād-huya-ādi*).

Rādhā is also present in the *gopāla-mantra* in the transcendental syllables *gopi-jana-vallabha*, which refer to Her Lord Kṛṣṇa, the lover of the *gopis*, of whom She is the foremost. Devotees of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa can also find Her in the *gaura-gāyatri*, because Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu is nondifferent from Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

Devoted worshipers of Śrimati Rādhārāṇi also find Her hidden in the *guru-gāyatri*, specifically in the word *kṛṣṇānandāya*. According to Sanskrit grammatical rules, *kṛṣṇānandāya* can be read in two ways:

1\. *krsn****a****-anandaya* “he who gives *ananda* to Krsna”’

2\. *krsn****a****-anandaya* “he who gives *ananda* to Krsna” Krsna (long ***a***) is Radha.

In order to see Rādhārāṇi’s presence in the *brahma-gāyatri*, however, we require the guidance of great spiritual personalities. She cannot be found there by mere grammatical logic. According to Śridhara Svāmi (Śrila Prabhupāda’s godbrother), the word bhargo devasya in the *brahma-gāyatri* means the *svarūpa-śakti* of *deva*, or Kṛṣṇa’s own spiritual potency. This is Śrimati Rādhārāṇi. As we have already heard from Śrila Prabhupāda, the word *savitur* in the *brahma-gāyatri* means Kṛṣṇa, the transcendental sungod. And Kṛṣṇa’s confidential devotees know that wherever there is Kṛṣṇa, there is Rādhā. Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmi gives two convincing analogies:

*mṛgamada, tāra gandha – yaiche aviccheda*  
*agni, jvālāte – yaiche kabhu nāhi bheda*

**They are indeed the same, just as musk and its scent are inseparable, or as fire and its heat are nondifferent.**

These analogies nicely express the relationship between *deva* and *bharga*. Wherever there is fire, there is heat. Wherever there is Kṛṣṇa, there is Rādhārāṇi. This brings us to the natural conclusion that the ultimate meaning of *bhargaḥ* in the *brahma-gāyatri* is Śrimati Rādhārāṇi.

**The glories of serving Śrimati Rādhārāṇi**

When Parikṣit’s mother, Uttarā, requested her son to describe the various destinations of Kṛṣṇa’s devotees, she showed special interest in those who are exclusively devoted to the performer of the *rāsa* dance. Having observed such *rasika* Vaiṣṇavas, she concluded:

*ye sarva-nairapekṣyeṇa rādhā-dāsyecchavaḥ param*  
*saṅkirtayanti tan-nāma tādṛśa-priyatā-mayaḥ*

**With indifference to everything else, these devotees want only to become servants of Śri Rādhā. They chant the names of the Lord of the** ***rāsa*** **dance and are full of unique love for him.**

Sanātana Gosvāmi explains the words *sarva-nairapekṣyeṇa*, “with indifference to anything else”:

**The devotees of Śri Rādhā, the dearmost beloved of Lord Madana-gopāla, are not impersonalists devoid of desires, but they have full confidence that if they are accepted by Śri Rādhikā as Her servants, everything they desire will be achieved automatically along with much more than they can imagine desiring. The privilege of serving Śri Rādhā is thus the rarest of all goals of life, and it is right that this privilege can only be earned by executing the most stringent of** ***sādhanas*****. That is, one can please Her Divine Grace by performing the sacrifice of purely chanting the names of Śri Rāsa-rasika, the hero of the** ***rāsa*** **dance. In sweet voices, the servants of Śri Rādhā are always melodiously chanting aloud Śri Kṛṣṇa’s names. They are fit to engage in such elevated service because their hearts are filled with the supreme variety of spontaneous ecstatic love for Kṛṣṇa.**

In other words, in the more advanced stages of Kṛṣṇa consciousness a devotee will search after Kṛṣṇa by performing *saṅkirtana* in the mood of *rādhā-dāsya*, servitude to Śrimati Rādhārāṇi:

**By always remembering Kṛṣṇa, who wears a peacock feather in his** ***mukuṭa*** **(crown), by performing** ***saṅkirtana*****, and engaging in the service of His lotus feet, by chanting the best of mantras, and by remembering the most cherished service of the lotus feet of Rādhā within my heart, when will I relish the most amazing festival of attachment to Rādhā?**

These are certainly high aspirations, and we are again and again reminded to first deserve and then desire. Let us therefore remember Sanātana Gosvāmi’s beautiful prayer:

*na premā śravaṇādi-bhaktir api vā yogo ’thavā vaiṣṇavo*  
*jñānaṁ vā śubha-karma vā kiyad aho saj-jātir apy asti vā*  
*hinārthādhika-sādhake tvayi tathāpy acchedya-mūlā sati*  
*he gopi-jana-vallabha vyathayate hā hā mad-āśaiva mām*

**O my Lord, I do not have any love for you, nor am I qualified for discharging devotional service by chanting and hearing. Nor do I possess the mystical power of a Vaiṣṇava, knowledge, or pious activities, nor do I belong to a very high-caste family. On the whole, I do not possess anything. Still, O beloved of the** ***gopis*****, because You bestow Your mercy on the most fallen, I have an unbreakable hope that is constantly in my heart. That hope is always giving me pain.**

**Conclusion**

This discussion about Śrimati Rādhārāṇi points toward an important subject: the mantras always contain deeper and deeper meanings, and those meanings become revealed as we mature in spiritual realization. Just as a seed contains a beautiful tree that will grow in due course of time, these mantras can reveal more and more spiritual secrets to the qualified chanter as his or her spiritual realization matures. Within a seed are the leaves, branches, bark, flowers, buds, fruits, and more seeds of a plant. The wood of the tree has the potential to create many wooden forms, including the form of the Deities, but that potential is manifest over time. It is important not to jump prematurely over necessary levels of progress. At the same time, we should never lose sight of the goal and the desire to ultimately attain it.

## 7. The Kāma-gāyatri and spontaneous devotional service

Spontaneous loving devotional service to Kṛṣṇa manifests when a devotee feels an irresistible attraction toward the devotional service performed by the eternal residents of Vṛndāvana. Such transcendental attraction is called *rāga*, and the *sādhana* of a devotee following transcendental attraction is called *rāgānuga-bhakti* (*rāga-anuga* – following one’s transcendental *rāga*). This level is attained when *sādhakas* have transcended their material conditioning and are free from all unwanted traits (*anarthas*).

In his Mantrārtha-dipikā, Śrila Viśvanātha Cakravarti Thākura analyzes the *kāma-gāyatri* from the more confidential viewpoint of *rāgānuga-bhakti.* First, he points out that the *kāma-gāyatri* is a *mahā-mantra* and that devotees who chant it as their regular *sādhana* will be born in Vṛndāvana. Second, he states that the mantra’s worshipable Deity (*iṣṭa-deva*) is Śri Kṛṣṇa, the king of *mādhurya-rasa* and the transcendental Cupid. Then he explains the mantra’s words as follows:

\- The syllable *kliṁ* expresses how the minds of Rādhā and the other *gopis* are captured by the sound of Kṛṣṇa’s flute.

\- *Kāma-devāya* stands for Rādhā and the other *gopis* being captured by Kṛṣṇa’s sweet pastimes.

\- *Kāma* has to be read in the sense of *sambandha* (relationship) and *anuga* (obedient following). This indicates that worship by chanting *kāma-gāyatri* isperformedin *kāma-anuga*, or *rāga-anuga-bhakti*.Thismakes sense if we consider that the effect of chanting the mantra is to illuminate and intensify the manifestation of one’s innermost desire to serve Kṛṣṇa in pure spontaneous love.

\- Another way to look on the word *kāmadeva* is divyati *kāmān*, “He shows His desires.” Or, *kāmena divyati*, “by His desire alone He plays in different pastimes.”

\- How is Kṛṣṇa Kāmadeva? Because He is *puṣpabāṇa*, He shoots five flower arrows from his five-threaded *śārṅga* bow. Dhimahi then means “I meditate upon Him as the possessor of these five arrows.”

\- In this way, *puṣpa-bāṇāya* expresses how the lovely sweetness and other features of Kṛṣṇa’s qualities nourish the excitement for conjugal love in Rādhā and the other *gopis*.

\- Kṛṣṇa is present in Vraja as *anaṅga*, the invisible, all-pervading Cupid, ever-youthful and enchanting. He is the only object of worship of the *kāma-bija* and *kāma-gāyatri*. It is His form only that attracts the worshiper. This transcendental form of Kṛṣṇa has no equal among all the Lord’s forms, and it is the embodiment of loving devotion in *mādhurya-rasa.*

\- Tan no *pracodayāt* means “may He inspire us.” Or, reading it as tan no *prakarṣeṇa codayāt*, “may He kindly please us (*codayāt*) with His excellent superior qualities (*prakarṣeṇa*) by engaging us in His loving devotional service.”

\- According to Prabodhānanda Sarasvati, the word *pracodayāt* can be read as *prakṛṣṭa-rūpeṇa ca* *udayāt*, “may He appear (*udayāt*) in His full form.” This form is described as possessing twenty-four-and-a-half moons.

When Kṛṣṇa performs His celebrated *rāsa* dance, He is requesting His *yoga-māyā* potency to step to the side and simply watch as He enters the stage of love as the supremely independent Kāmadeva. This is a way for the *rāgānuga-sādhaka* to meditate on *kāma-gāyatri*. As Śrila Bhaktivinoda Thākura explains:

**When** ***yoga-māyā*** **is watching the** ***rāsa dance*** **– this is the form that** ***gāyatri*** **takes: She observes Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes.**

This *rāsa-lilā*, the most celebrated of all the Lord’s pastimes, is cherished in numerous confidential Vaiṣṇava scriptures. You may enter more deeply into this sublime matter, under the spiritual master’s guidance, by making yourself familiar with the *rāsa-lilā*, after having carefully assessed your own qualification and, your ability to “digest” such highly confidential subjects.

## 8. A meditation on Kṛṣṇa

The following text, taken from Hari-bhakti-vilāsa, provides an extensive meditation on Kṛṣṇa in Vṛndāvana. As mentioned earlier, both the *gopāla-mantra* and particularly the *kāma-gāyatri* are meditations on the Divine Couple, Śri Śri Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, who are situated within the beautiful forest groves of Vraja. In order to absorb our mind in such meditation, it is necessary to become familiar with this divine reality. While chanting *gāyatri*, qualified *sādhakas* will then be able to manifest Śri Vṛndāvana-dhāma before their mind’s eye and mentally roam about seeking the Lord and Mistress of their heart.

**The pure land of Vṛndāvana is cooled by the presence of trees whose tender branches are laden with clusters of new buds and the most attractive flowers. These trees are flowing with honey and saturated with heavenly fragrances, and their trunks are entwined with vines decorated with clusters of fresh flowers.**

**The heart of Vṛndāvana resounds with the humming of swarming bees that have come to taste the nectar from the blossoming flowers, and with the cries of the doves, parrots,** ***sārikās*****, and cuckoos. Everywhere the peacocks dance.**

**Vṛndāvana is served by a soft, soothing breeze carrying a fine spray from the lapping waves of the Yamunā. That breeze, bearing the pollen dust from fully blown lotuses, playfully shakes the garments of the** ***gopis*****, whose minds then become agitated with desire for their Lord.**

**In the midst of Vṛndāvana stands a towering** ***kalpa-vṛkṣa*** **tree, be-stower of all desires, whose branches are coral, leaves are emeralds, bouquets of buds are diamonds and pearls, and fruits are rubies. Served by the seasons personified, this tree simultaneously produces all kinds of flowers.**

**At the base of the nectar-showering** ***kalpa-vṛkṣa*** **tree is an area that shines as brilliantly as the sun rising over a range of solid gold mountains. Sparkling with inlaid jewels and glowing with heaps of golden pollen, this place is completely free from the waves of lamentation, illusion, old age, death, hunger, and thirst.**

**On the jewel-inlaid floor is an exquisite** ***āsana*** **in the form of an eightpetalled lotus tinted the color of dawn. In the center, as resplendent as the rising sun, sits Lord Mukunda in a comfortable pose.**

**He shines like a brilliant, dark sapphire, as deep black as lampblack, as dark as a mountain of monsoon clouds, as delicate as a blue lotus. On His black mass of hair, which is thick, glossy, and curled, sits a luminous peacock feather.**

**On His head rest ornaments of** ***pārijāta*** **blossoms attended eagerly by swarms of bees, and fresh flowers are on His ears. In the middle of His forehead, surrounded by stray locks of hair, shines vertical bright yellow tilaka formed between a pair of long, creeper-like eyebrows. His face shines like the flawless moon of the autumn season, and His eyes are wide like lotus petals. His mirror-like cheeks glitter with the rays from His jeweled, shark-shaped earrings. His beautiful nose points gracefully upward, and His tender smile, resembling the moon, a jasmine, or a** ***mandāra*** **flower, illuminates all His features.**

**Around His conch-like neck hangs a string of coral flowers and young leaves, and from His shoulders to His feet extends a garland of** ***kalpa-vṛkṣa*** **flowers swarming with intoxicated bees. On His broad chest rest a sparkling string of pearls, like a constellation of stars, the shining Kaustubha jewel, bright like the sun in the sky, and His distinguishing mark, the Śrivatsa.**

**His shoulders are high, His well-formed thick arms reach to His knees, and His stomach is slightly indented and raised, with a handsome, deep navel at its center. Extending upward from His navel is a delicate line of black hair. He is decorated with armbands, bracelets, necklaces, anklets, cords, and a golden belt studded with jewels. Various designs are painted on different parts of His body. Around His waist is wrapped a yellow cloth.**

**He has beautiful thighs and knees, charming ankles, and feet more lustrous than polished turtle shells. His toenails shine like ruby mirrors, and His toes are like jeweled leaves. On His pink hands and feet are the pleasing signs of the fish, elephant goad,** ***cakra*****, conch, flag, lotus, thunderbolt, and barleycorn. His body, comprising the topmost elements of beauty, conquers the physical attraction of Cupid, the god of love.**

**Śri Kṛṣṇa is an ocean of unlimited bliss. From His flute – made perfect by association with His lotus face – arise transcendental** ***rāgas*** **as He moves His fingers in a simple way. Hearing those** ***rāgas*** **all the creatures and their offspring become attracted and stunned, their hearts melted.**

**Śri Kṛṣṇa is surrounded by cows with long, slender tails. They approach Him with an unsteady gait, for their udders are swollen with milk. Their large eyes become glued to the lotus face of the Lord while half-chewed tender shoots of grass remain poised on the ends of their teeth. The newborn calves remain motionless, their beauty enhanced by the milk foam flowing from their small lips and teeth, with which they had so eagerly sucked at the full udders. They cock their little ears to hear the deep, enchanting tones from His flute. The older calves, with colorful blankets around their throats and little horns emerging from their soft heads, raise their tails, butt each other, playfully gallop this way and that, and finally gather around the Lord. The massive bulls, laden with the burden of their huge humps, lowing deeply, approach Him lazily. But when the liquid nectar from the flute enters their cocked ears, they flare their nostrils and tilt up their heads.**

**The cowherd boys, similar to Kṛṣṇa in character, blissful nature, age, activities, and dress, play sweet, low melodies on their flutes and viṇas. They sing graceful tunes, and with their arms extended they dance expertly. The babies, with little lisping words in their mouths, tiger claws hanging from their necks, and jingling bells attached to their ankles and fat bellies, also approach the Lord. The attractive cowherd girls, their waists marked with three lines, come forward, eager to serve Him. They sway with the weight of their broad, heavy hips, and their heads bow beneath their piles of abundant hair. The desire trees of their bodies, drenched with the nectar from His flute, sprout goose bumps like flowers blooming on a vine. The ocean of** ***prema*** **in the** ***gopis*****’ hearts expands due to the moonbeam smile of Nanda’s son, and the splashing from those rushing waves of** ***prema*** **appears as drops of perspiration on the bodies of the laboring** ***gopis*****.**

**From His long, bowed eyebrows He shoots sharp glances – a shower of deadly arrows of love that break the vulnerable points in the composure of the** ***gopis*****. Disturbed but unsubmitting, they try to suppress the painful trembling that begins to afflict all parts of their bodies. But eager to drink the nectar of the incomparable form of Kṛṣṇa, they furtively glance at Him with half-closed, bashful eyes and thus continue to float in the streams of** ***prema*****.**

**Swarms of bees surround the** ***gopis*****, eager to drink the honey flowing from the flowers that have fallen from their loosened tresses. In their intense frenzy the gopis utter inaudible, tender words, and their waist bells jingle from their uncontrollable trembling. Their fine silk clothes, loosened by their heavy breathing, reveal parts of their lustrous bodies. The soft sound of their hesitant footsteps mixes with the sound from their jeweled anklets and echoes in all directions. Their lips quiver, their earrings glitter, and their eyes, rimmed with graceful eyelashes, stay half-closed out of shyness. The agitation of their deep breathing causes their pink lips to darken. In their delicate, sportive hands they hold various presentations for their Lord, whom they constantly attend.**

**The Lord, a reservoir of pleasure, is decorated with a garland of full-blown blue lotuses in the form of the** ***gopis*****’ dark restless eyes, which fix themselves on His form. Those eyes, like a garland of love-hungry bees, madly drink the liquid honey of His all-attractive face.**

**The** ***gopas*****,** ***gopis*****, and cows remain at some distance, while the** ***devatas*** **stand in front with Brahmā, Śiva, and Indra in the lead, reciting verses to attain material wealth (*****artha*****). To the right side stand the assembled munis, intent on fulfilling the codes of dharma with Vedic knowledge. Behind, Sanaka and other** ***yogindras*****, wanting liberation (*****mokṣa*****), remain in a state of** ***samādhi*****. To the left stand the Yakṣas, Siddhas, Gandharvas, Vidyādharas, and Cāraṇas with their wives, along with the Kinnaras and the best of the Apsarās. In pursuit of** ***kāma*** **(pleasure), they sing, dance, and play musical instruments.**

**In the sky appears Nārada Muni as white as a conch and shining like the moon, a jasmine, and a lightning bolt. He is decorated with matted locks of reddish hair. Knowing the conclusion of all scriptures, he serves Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet with undeviating devotion, having given up all material pleasures. He pleases the Lord by plucking his** ***viṇa*****, which produces wonderful melodies arranged in skillful** ***rāgas*****.**

## 9. Saṅkṣepa-arcana-paddhati

**An abbreviated manual for deity worship**

**From Śrila Bhaktivinoda Thākura’s Bhajana-rahasya**

Devotees of Śri Kṛṣṇa naturally desire to worship the Lord properly. Although chanting the holy name ultimately leads to all perfection, the activities of *arcana* also have great significance and are closely related to the chanting of *gāyatri*. Brāhmaṇa initiated devotees can greatly benefit from the following guidelines established by Śrila Bhaktivinoda Thākura. They offer an enlightening step-by-step process of worship, which can be performed physically before an altar or within the mind.

Devotees who do not desire to follow this manual may still like to read the two prayers called Padya-pañcaka and Vijñāpti-pañcaka, which are included in this section on pages 260-261. These prayers are wonderful sources of inspiration for daily devotional practice, and they help us absorb the mind in a prayerful mood, surrendering ourselves at the lotus feet of the Divine Couple and humbly imploring Them for Their causeless mercy.

After bathing in the morning, the *sādhaka* should sit on an *āsana* facing east. Touching the water in the *pañca-patra* he should call all the tirthas by chanting this mantra:

*gaṅge ca yamune caiva godāvari sarasvati*  
*narmade sindho kāveri jale ’smin sannidhiṁ kuru*

**May water from the holy rivers Gaṅgā, Yamunā, Godāvari, Sarasvati, Narmadā, Sindhu, and Kāveri kindly be present.**

Sprinkling this water on his head, he should then perform *ācamana* while chanting *oṁ śri viṣṇuḥ, oṁ śri viṣṇuḥ, oṁ śri viṣṇuḥ*. Then he should mark his body with *gopi-candana* in twelve places while chanting the following mantras:

*lalāṭe keśavaṁ dhyāyen nārāyaṇam athodare*  
*vakṣaḥ-sthale mādhavaṁ tu govindaṁ kaṇṭha-kūpake*  
*viṣṇuṁ ca dakṣiṇe kukṣau bāhau ca madhusūdanam*  
*trivikramaṁ kandhare tu vāmanaṁ vāma-pārśvake*  
*śridharaṁ vāma-bāhau tu hṛṣikeśaṁ tu kandhare*  
*pṛṣṭhe ca padmanābhaṁ ca kaṭyāṁ dāmodaraṁ nyaset*  
*tat-prakṣalana-toyaṁ tu vāsudevāya mūrdhani*

**When one marks the forehead with** ***tilaka*** **he must remember Keśava. When one marks the lower abdomen he must remember Nārāyaṇa. Marking the chest he should remember Mādhava. When marking the hollow of the neck he should remember Govinda. Lord Viṣṇu should be remembered while marking the right side of the belly, and Madhusūdana while marking the right arm. Trivikrama should be remembered when marking the right shoulder, and Vāmana while marking the left side of the belly. Śridhara should be remembered while marking the left arm, and Hṛṣikeśa while marking the left shoulder. Padmanābha and Dāmodara should be remembered when marking the back. The remaining water \[with** ***gopi-candana*****\] should be placed on the head while remembering Vāsudeva.**

Before worshiping the Lord, one should perform *guru-pūjā*, meditating on his *guru* as follows:

*prātaḥ śriman-navadvipe dvi-netraṁ dvi-bhujaṁ gurum*  
*varābhaya-pradaṁ śāntaṁ smaret tan-nāma-pūrvakam*

**In the morning one should remember the spiritual master as being situated in Śri Navadvipa, having two eyes and two arms, as being peaceful, and as bestowing all benediction and fearlessness upon chanting his name.**

Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu is sitting in a jeweled pavilion at Yoga-piṭha in the eternal abode of Māyāpura, Navadvipa. On His right side sits Nityānanda Prabhu and on His left Gadādhara Paṇdita. Advaita Prabhu is standing with folded hands and offering prayers next to Nityānanda on the right. On the left Śrivāsa Paṇdita stands holding an umbrella over the Lord’s head. Śri Gurudeva is sitting on an altar below them.

A devotee should meditate on sitting near Śri Gurudeva and worshiping him by offering sixteen items with appropriate mantras:

***Śri Guru-pūjā***

**1. Offering a seat to Śri Gurudeva:**  
***idam āsanam aiṁ gurudevāya namaḥ***

**2. Washing his lotus feet:**  
***etat pādyam aiṁ gurudevāya namaḥ***

**3. Offering arghya water:**  
***idam arghyam aiṁ gurudevāya namaḥ***

**4. Offering water for ācamana:**  
***ācamaniyam aiṁ gurudevāya namaḥ***

**5. Offering a madhuparka drink (yogurt with honey):**  
***eṣa madhuparkaḥ aiṁ gurudevāya namaḥ***

**6. Again offering water for ācamana:**  
***idaṁ punar ācamaniyam aiṁ gurudevāya namaḥ***

**7. Offering a bath:**  
***idaṁ snāniyam aiṁ gurudevāya namaḥ***

**8. Offering clothes for dressing:**  
***idaṁ sottariya-vastram aiṁ gurudevāya namaḥ***

**9. Offering ornaments:**  
***idam ābharaṇam aiṁ gurudevāya namaḥ***

**10. Offering sandalwood paste:**  
***eṣa gandhaḥ aiṁ gurudevāya namaḥ***

**11. Offering incense:**  
***eṣa dhūpaḥ aiṁ gurudevāya namaḥ***

**12. Offering lamps:**  
***eṣa dipaḥ aiṁ gurudevāya namaḥ***

**13. Offering flowers:**  
***idaṁ sacandana-puṣpam aiṁ gurudevāya namaḥ***

**14. Offering eatables:**  
***idaṁ naivedyam aiṁ gurudevāya namaḥ***

**15. Offering a drink:**  
***idaṁ pāniya-jalam aiṁ gurudevāya namaḥ***

**16. Again offering water for ācamana:**  
***idaṁ punar ācamaniyam aiṁ gurudevāya namaḥ***

**17. Offering betel:**  
***idaṁ tāmbulam aiṁ gurudevāya namaḥ***

**18. Finally, offering everything to Śri Gurudeva:**  
***idaṁ sarvam aiṁ gurudevāya namaḥ***

After offering these articles, one should chant the guru-gāyatri-mantra and then offer obeisances to the guru with the following mantra:

*om ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmilitaṁ yena tasmai Śri-gurave namaḥ*

**I was born in the darkest ignorance, and my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torch of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him.**

Then one should offer obeisances to the Vaiṣṇavas:

*vāñchā-kalpa-tarubhyaś ca kṛpā-sindhubhya eva ca patitānāṁ pāvanebhyo vaiṣṇavebhyo namo namaḥ*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto all the Vaiṣṇava devotees of the Lord. They are just like desire trees that can fulfill the desires of everyone. They are oceans of mercy and are the purifiers of the fallen souls.**

Then one should worship Lord Gaurāṅga, who is complete as Pañca-tattva. Meditate on Lord Gaurāṅga with this mantra:

*śriman-mauktika-dāma-baddha-cikuraṁ su-smera-candrānanaṁ*  
*śri-khaṇdāguru-cāru-citra-vasanaṁ srag-divya-bhūṣāñcitam*  
*nṛtyāveśa-rasānumoda-madhuraṁ kandarpa-veśojjvalaṁ*  
*caitanyaṁ kanaka-dyutiṁ nija-janaiḥ saṁsevyamānaṁ bhaje*

**I worship Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu whose hair is bound with strings of pearls and whose moonlike face bears the nectar of his gentle smile. His beautiful golden body is adorned with lovely garments, anointed with sandalwood and** ***aguru*** **pulp and bedecked with garlands and various glistening ornaments.
