He is extremely charming absorbed as He is in enjoying the sweet mellows of dancing. His dress is more splendid than even Cupid’s, and He is being served by all his devotees and associates.**

Then worship Śri Gaurāṅga in this way with the appropriate mantras:

***Śri Gaura-pūjā***

**1. Offer a seat to Śri Kṛṣṇa Caitanya:**  
***idam āsanaṁ kliṁ kṛṣṇa-caitanyāya namaḥ***

**2. Wash His lotus feet:**  
***etat pādyaṁ kliṁ kṛṣṇa-caitanyāya namaḥ***

**3. Offer arghya water:**  
***idam arghyaṁ kliṁ kṛṣṇa-caitanyāya namaḥ***

**4. Offer water for ācamana:**  
***idam ācamaniyaṁ kliṁ kṛṣṇa-caitanyāya namaḥ***

**5. Offering a madhuparka drink (yogurt with honey):**  
***eṣa madhuparkaḥ kliṁ kṛṣṇa-caitanyāya namaḥ***

**6. Again offer water for ācamana:**  
***idaṁ punar ācamaniyaṁ kliṁ kṛṣṇa-caitanyāya namaḥ***

**7. Offer a bath:**  
***idaṁ snāniyaṁ kliṁ kṛṣṇa-caitanyāya namaḥ***

**8. Offer clothes for dressing:**  
***idaṁ sottariya-vastraṁ kliṁ kṛṣṇa-caitanyāya namaḥ***

**9. Offer ornaments:**  
***idam ābharaṇaṁ kliṁ kṛṣṇa-caitanyāya namaḥ***

**10. Offer sandalwood paste:**  
***eṣa gandhaḥ kliṁ kṛṣṇa-caitanyāya namaḥ***

**11. Offer incense:**  
***eṣa dhūpaḥ kliṁ kṛṣṇa-caitanyāya namaḥ***

**12. Offer lamps:**  
***eṣa dipaḥ kliṁ kṛṣṇa-caitanyāya namaḥ***

**13. Offer flowers:**  
***idaṁ sacandana-puṣpaṁ kliṁ kṛṣṇa-caitanyāya namaḥ***

**14. Offer Tulasi:**  
***idaṁ sacandana-tulasi-patraṁ kliṁ kṛṣṇa-caitanyāya namaḥ***

**15. Offer eatables:**  
***idaṁ naivedyaṁ kliṁ kṛṣṇa-caitanyāya namaḥ***

**16. Offer a drink:**  
***idaṁ pāniya-jalaṁ kliṁ kṛṣṇa-caitanyāya namaḥ***

**17. Again offer water for ācamana:**  
***idaṁ punar ācamaniya kliṁ kṛṣṇa-caitanyāya namaḥ***

**18. Offer betel:**  
***idaṁ tāmbulaṁ kliṁ kṛṣṇa-caitanyāya namaḥ***

**19. Offer a garland:**  
***idaṁ mālyaṁ kliṁ kṛṣṇa-caitanyāya namaḥ***

**20. Finally, offer everything to Śri Kṛṣṇa Caitanya:**  
***idaṁ sarvaṁ kliṁ kṛṣṇa-caitanyāya namaḥ***

After *Gaura-pūjā*, chant the *gaura-gāyatri-mantra*, then offer obeisances to Lord Gaurasundara with the following mantra:

*ānanda-lilā-maya-vigrahāya*  
*hemābha-divyac-chavi-sundarāya*  
*tasmai mahā-prema-rasa-pradāya*  
*caitanya-candrāya namo namas te*

**Obeisances unto Him, Śri Caitanya-candra, the giver of the mellow of the highest love of Godhead, who is the embodiment of blissful pastimes, and who is so beautiful, having a dazzling luster, like gold.**

Now worship Śri Śri Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa while contemplating that one is doing so only by the mercy of Śri Gurudeva and Śri Gaurāṅga. Begin with a meditation on Vṛndāvana with the following mantras:

*tato vṛndavānaṁ dhyāyet paramānanda-vardhanam*  
*sarvārtu-kusumopetaṁ    patatri-gāna-nāditam*  
*bhramad-bhramara-jhaṅkāra-mukhari-kṛta-diṅ-mukham*  
*kālindi-jala-kallola-saṅgi-māruta-sevitam*  
*nānā-puṣpa-latā-baddha-vṛkṣa-ṣaṇdaiś ca maṇditam*  
*kamalotpala-kahlāra-dhūli-dhusaritāntaram*  
*tan-madhye ratna-bhūmiṁ ca sūryāyuta-sama-prabham*  
*tatra kalpa-tarūdyanaṁ    niyataṁ prema-varṣiṇam*  
*māṇikya-śikhārālambi tan-madhye maṇi-maṇdapam*  
*nānā-ratna-gaṇaiś citraṁ    sarvartu-suvirājitam*  
*nānā-ratna-lasac-citra- vitanair upaśobhitam*  
*ratna-toraṇa-gopura- maṇikyācchādanānvitam*  
*koṭi-sūrya-samābhāsaṁ viuktaṁ sat-taraṅgakaiḥ*  
*tan-madhye ratna-khacitaṁ     svarṇa-siṁhāsanaṁ mahat*  
*kamalotpala-kahlāra-dhūli-dhūsaritāntaram*

**Vṛndāvana-dhāma is an abode of ever-increasing joy. In that transcendental land grow flowers and fruits of all seasons, and various birds fill it with sweet sounds. Cool breezes and the Yamunā’s waters enhance the atmosphere, and all directions resound with the humming of bumblebees.**

**Vṛndāvana is decorated with wish-fulfilling trees embraced by many varieties of flower-bearing creepers, and its divine beauty is ornamented with the pollen of red, blue, and white lotuses. The ground is made of jewels that shine with the brilliance of ten million suns rising in the sky at once. On that ground is a garden of desire trees forever showering divine love, and in that garden sits a bejeweled temple with a pinnacle of rubies. Bedecked as it is with various jewels, that temple shines brilliantly in every season. It is beautified with brightly colored canopies, glittering with various gems, and endowed with coverings decorated with rubies, as well as gateways and arches inlaid with gems. That temple shines with the splendor of millions of suns and is eternally free from the six waves of material miseries. Within it sits a great golden, gem-inlaid throne.**

**In this way one should meditate on Śri Vṛndāvana-dhāma, the divine realm of the Supreme Lord.**

Next, meditate on Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa sitting on a golden throne studded with jewels while chanting the following mantra:

*śri-kṛṣṇaṁ śri-ghana-śyāmaṁ pūrṇānanda-kalevaram*  
*dvi-bhujaṁ sarva-deveśaṁ rādhā-liṅgita-vigraham*

**Śri Kṛṣṇa, Śri Ghana-śyāma, Your body consists of pure bliss. You have two arms and are the Lord of all the demigods. You are lovingly embraced by Śrimati Rādhārāṇi.**

Then offer worship with sixteen items and their appropriate mantras:

***Śri Śri Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa-pūjā***

**1. Offer a seat to Śri Śri Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa:**  
***idam āsanaṁ Śriṁ kliṁ rādhā-kṛṣṇābhyāṁ namaḥ***

**2. Wash Their lotus feet:**  
***etat pādyaṁ śriṁ kliṁ rādhā-kṛṣṇābhyāṁ namaḥ***

**3. Offer arghya water:**  
***idam arghyaṁ śriṁ kliṁ rādhā-kṛṣṇābhyāṁ namaḥ***

**4. Offering water for ācamana:i**  
***dam ācamaniyaṁ śriṁ kliṁ rādhā-kṛṣṇābhyāṁ namaḥ***

**5. Offer a madhuparka drink (yogurt with honey):**  
***eṣa madhuparkaḥ śriṁ kliṁ rādhā-kṛṣṇābhyāṁ namaḥ***

**6. Again offer water for ācamana:**  
***idaṁ punar ācamaniyaṁ śriṁ kliṁ rādhā-kṛṣṇābhyāṁ namaḥ***

**7. Offer a bath:idaṁ**  
***snāniyaṁ śriṁ kliṁ rādhā-kṛṣṇābhyāṁ namaḥ***

**8. Offer clothes for dressing:**  
***idaṁ sottariya-vastraṁ śriṁ kliṁ rādhā-kṛṣṇābhyāṁ namaḥ***

**9. Offer ornaments:**  
***idam ābharaṇaṁ śriṁ kliṁ rādhā-kṛṣṇābhyāṁ namaḥ***

**10. Offer sandalwood paste:**  
***eṣa gandhaḥ śriṁ kliṁ rādhā-kṛṣṇābhyāṁ namaḥ***

**11. Offer incense:eṣ**  
***a dhūpaḥ śriṁ kliṁ rādhā-kṛṣṇābhyāṁ namaḥ***

**12. Offer lamps:**  
***eṣa dipaḥ śriṁ kliṁ rādhā-kṛṣṇābhyāṁ namaḥ***

**13. Offer flowers:**  
***idaṁ sacandana-puṣpaṁ śriṁ kliṁ rādhā-kṛṣṇābhyāṁ namaḥ***

**14. Offer Tulasi:**  
***idaṁ sacandana-tulasi-patraṁ***

**śriṁ kliṁ rādhā-kṛṣṇābhyāṁ namaḥ**

**15. Offer eatables:**  
***idaṁ naivedyaṁ śriṁ kliṁ rādhā-kṛṣṇābhyāṁ namaḥ***

**16. Offer a drink:**  
***idaṁ pāniya-jalaṁ śriṁ kliṁ rādhā-kṛṣṇābhyāṁ namaḥ***

**17. Again offer water for ācamana:**  
***idaṁ punar ācamaniyaṁ śriṁ kliṁ rādhā-kṛṣṇābhyāṁ namaḥ***

**18. Offer betel:id**  
***aṁ tāmbulaṁ śriṁ kliṁ rādhā-kṛṣṇābhyāṁ namaḥ***

**19. Offer a garland:**  
***idaṁ mālyaṁ śriṁ kliṁ rādhā-kṛṣṇābhyāṁ namaḥ***

**20. Finally, offer everything to Śri Śri Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa:**  
***idaṁ sarvaṁ śriṁ kliṁ rādhā-kṛṣṇābhyāṁ namaḥ***

After finishing the *pūjā*, chant the *yugala-gāyatri-mantra*:

*kliṁ kṛṣṇāya vidmahe dāmodarāya dhimahi*  
*tan no kṛṣṇaḥ pracodayāt*

**Let us try to know the all-attractive Śri Kṛṣṇa. Let us meditate upon Dāmodara, who is bound by the love of His devotees. May that Kṛṣṇa enthuse us.**

*śriṁ rādhikāyai vidmahe prema-rūpāyai dhimahi*  
*tan no rādhā pracodayāt*

**Let us try to understand Śrimati Rādhikā. Let us meditate upon Her, who is the embodiment of** ***prema*****. May that Rādhā enthuse us.**

Then offer obeisances first to Śri Kṛṣṇa, then to Śrimati Rādhārāṇi:

*he kṛṣṇa karuṇā-sindho dina-bandho jagat-pate*  
*gopeśa gopikā-kānta rādhā-kānta namo ’stu te*

**O my dear Kṛṣṇa, You are the friend of the distressed and the source of creation. You are the master of the** ***gopis*** **and the lover of Rādhārāṇi. I offer my respectful obeisances unto You.**

*tapta-kāñcana-gaurāṅgi rādhe vṛndāvaneśvari*  
*vṛṣabhānu-sute devi praṇamāmi hari-priye*

**I offer my respects to Rādhārāṇi, whose bodily complexion is like molten gold and who is the Queen of Vṛndāvana. You are the daughter of King Vṛṣabhānu, and You are very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa.**

Then chant *kāma-bija* and *kāma-gāyatri*. With feelings of distress, read the following prayers:

***Padya-pañcaka***

*saṁsāra-sagarānātha putra-mitra-gṛhāgaṇāt*  
*goptārau me yuvām eva prapanna-bhaya-bhañjanau*

**O Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, You are my protectors from the ocean of material existence, which is filled with sons, friends, household, and land. Therefore You are known as the destroyers of fear for those who are surrendered to You.**

*yo ’haṁ mamāsti yat kiñcit iha loke paratra ca*  
*tat sravaṁ bhavato ’dyaiva caraṇeṣu samarpitam*

**Your Lordships, me and whatever little bit is mine in this world and the next I now offer at Your lotus feet.**

*aham apy aparādhānām ālāyas tyakta-sādhanaḥ*  
*agatiś ca tato nāthau bhavantau me parā gatiḥ*

**Your Lordships, I am certainly the abode of many offenses and am completely devoid of any devotional practices. Nor do I have any resort or shelter. Therefore, I regard You as my ultimate goal.**

*tavāsmi rādhikā-nātha    karmaṇā manasā girā*  
*kṛṣṇa-kānte tavaivāsmi    yuvām eva gatir mama*  
*śaraṇaṁ vāṁ prapanno ’smi    karuṇā-nikarākarau*  
*prasādaṁ kuru dāsyaṁ bho    mayi duṣṭe ’parādhini*

**I am Yours, O Lord of Śrimati Rādhārāṇi, as are my actions, mind, and words. O Śrimati Rādhārāṇi, lover of Śri Kṛṣṇa, I belong to You alone. You are both my only destination. O Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, O ocean of mercy, You are my only shelter, and thus I take shelter of You. Although I am such a fallen offender, please be merciful to me and make me Your servant.**

***Vijñapti-pañcaka***

*mat-samo nāsti pāpātmā nāparādhi ca kaścana*  
*parihāre ’pi lajjā me kiṁ bruve puruṣottama*

**O Puruṣottama, no sinner or offender is as bad as I am. How can I describe my shame?**

*yuvatināṁ yathā yūni yūnāñcāṁ yuvatau yathā*  
*mano ’bhiramate tadvad mano me ramatāṁ tvayi*

**Just as the minds of young women take pleasure in young men, and the minds of young men take pleasure in young women, kindly let my mind take pleasure in You alone.**

*bhūmau skhalita-pādānāṁ bhūmir evāvalambanam*  
*tvayi jātāparādhānāṁ tvam eva śaraṇaṁ prabho*

**Just as the ground is the only shelter for those whose feet have slipped, You are the only shelter for those who have offended You.**

*govinda-vallabhe rādhe prārthaye tvām ahaṁ sadā*  
*tvadiyam iti jānātu govindo māṁ tvayā sadā*

**O Śrimati Rādhārāṇi, beloved of Lord Govinda, this is my request: May You and Lord Govinda consider me one of Your assistants.**

*rādhe vṛndāvanādhiśe karuṇāmṛta-vāhini*  
*kṛpayā nija-pādābja-dāsyaṁ mahyaṁ pradiyatām*

**O Śrimati Rādhārāṇi, O queen of Vṛndāvana, you are a river flowing with the nectar of mercy. Please be kind to me and give me a little service at your lotus feet.**

Then offer the remnants of the *pūjā* to Śri Gurudeva and the Vaiṣṇavas with the following mantras:

**1. The** ***maha-*****garlands to the spiritual master:**  
***etad mahā-prasāda-nirmālyaṁ śri-gurave namaḥ***

**2. The** ***maha*** **drink and water to the spiritual master:**  
***etat pāniya-jalaṁ śri-gurave namaḥ***

**3. The** ***maha*** **betel to the spiritual master:**  
***etat prasāda-tāmbulaṁ Śri-gurave namaḥ***

**4. Everything to the** ***sakhis*****:**  
***etat sarvaṁ sarva-sakhibhyo namaḥ***

**5. Obeisances to Śrimati Paurṇamāsi:**  
***śri-paurṇamāsyai namaḥ***

**6. Obeisances to all the** ***vraja-vāsis*****:**  
***sarva-vraja-vāsibhyo namaḥ***

**7. Obeisances to all the Vaiṣṇavas:**  
***sarva-vaiṣṇavebhyo namaḥ***

Tulasi leaves are required when performing *arcana*. The mantra for picking Tulasi is:

*oṁ tulasy amṛta-janmāsi sadā tvaṁ keśava-priyā*  
*keśavārthaṁ cinomi tvaṁ vara-dā bhava śobhane*

**O Tulasi, you are born from nectar. You are always very dear to Lord Keśava. Now to worship Lord Keśava, I am collecting your leaves and** ***mañjaris*****. Please bless me.**

After picking her leaves, worship Śrimati Tulasi Devi as follows:

***Tulasi-pūjā***

*nirmālya-gandha-puṣpādi-pāniya-jalaṁ*  
*idam arghyaṁ ṣri-tulasyai namah*

**I offer obeisances to Śrimati Tulasi Devi by offering her a garland, fragrant flowers, drinking water, and** ***arghyam*****.**

***Tulasi-mantra***

*nirmitā tvaṁ purā devair arcitā tvaṁ surāsuraiḥ*  
*tulasi hara me ’vidyāṁ pūjāṁ gṛhṇa namo ’stu te*

**O Śrimati Tulasi Devi, you were previously created by the demigods and worshiped by both them and the demons. Kindly remove my ignorance and accept my worship. I offer you my humble obeisances again and again.**

***Tulasi-praṇāma***

*yā dṛṣṭā nikhilāgha-saṅga-śamani spṛṣṭā vapuḥ pāvani*  
*rogānām abhivanditā nirasani siktā ’ntaka-trāsini*  
*pratyāsatti-vidhāyini bhagavataḥ kṛṣṇasya saṁropitā*  
*nyastā tac-caraṇe su-bhakti-phala-dā tasyai tulasyai namaḥ*

**I offer my respectful obeisances to Śrimati Tulasi Devi. By seeing her, she devours all sinful reactions. By touching her, she purifies the body. By worshiping her, she takes away all diseases. By watering her, she rescues one from the cycle of birth and death. By planting her, she brings one in the close proximity of Lord Kṛṣṇa, and by bowing down at her lotus feet, she bestows one with the fruit of pure** ***bhakti*****.**

After offering obeisances to Tulasi Devi, chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* on Tulasi beads with *sambandha-jñāna*. There are no hard and fast rules in terms of time, place, purity, or impurity while chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa. The holy name is the most auspicious eternal truth.

Next chant the following mantra and honor *śri-kṛṣṇa-caraṇāmṛta* by drinking a little and then touching it to the head:

*aśeṣa-kleśa-niḥśeṣa- kāraṇaṁ śuddha-bhakti-dam*  
*kṛṣṇa-pādodakaṁ pitvā śirasā dhārayāmy aham*

**Having drunk the water from the lotus feet of Śri Kṛṣṇa, which bestows pure devotional service and causes the destruction of unlimited troubles, I take that water on my head.**

One should then honor *mahā-prasāda*:

*rudanti pātakāḥ sarve niśvasanti muhur muhuḥ*  
*hā hā kṛtvā palāyanti jagannāthānna-bhakṣaṇāt*

**If one eats Lord Jagannātha’s** ***prasāda*****, all one’s sins will be crying and sighing again and again and running away shouting, “Alas, alas.”**

Then one should offer full, prostrate obeisances with the following mantra:

*dorbhyāṁ padbhyāṁ ca jānubhyāṁ urasā śirasā dṛśā*  
*manasā vacasā ceti praṇāmo ’ṣṭāṅga iritaḥ*

**I offer obeisances with the eight parts of my body: the arms, the feet, the knees, the chest, the forehead, the vision, the mind, and the speech.**

Thus end the duties prescribed for morning *pūjā*.

Generally, one should observe the appropriate vratas on Ekādaśi and on *viṣṇu-tattva* appearance days as far as possible and according to Hari-bhakti-vilāsa. Never mix unnecessarily with nondevotees. Avoiding bad association is one of the main Vaiṣṇava qualities.

## Questions and answers

Included in this section are answers to questions concerning the *gāyatri-mantras* that I have been asked in classes or letters. In some cases, I have added more information to the responses that I gave at the time. Most of the text, however, has been taken directly from classes.

I hope this material will encourage the readers to dive more deeply into the topic of *gāyatri*. My answers are by no means complete, but I hope they will inspire you to learn more, and in some cases, to do your own research.

## 1. Gāyatri practice

**The** ***Brāhmaṇa*** **Thread**

**Question**: When should we change the *brāhmaṇa* thread? How do we change it, and where do we dispose of the old thread?

**Answer**: Whenever the *brāhmaṇa* thread is old and worn or the individual strings break, or when it is so dirty that it cannot be washed, it is time to change it. If possible, one should try to change his thread on an Ekādaśi, *viṣṇu-tattva* appearance day, or on another spiritual and festive occasion.

The *Arcana-paddhati* provides detailed information on how to replace the *upavita*:

1\. Sit down on an *āsana*, as you would for chanting *gāyatri*.

2\. Perform *ācamana* as usual.

3\. Sprinkle some water from the *pañcapatra* on your new sacred thread. Undo it and stretch it around your knees.

4\. Remove the new sacred thread from your knees and hold it horizontally open with the palms of your hands. Turn the thread vertically so that your right hand is up, with the palm turned upwards, and your left hand down, with the palm turned downwards.

5\. Chant the following mantra:

*oṁ yajñopavitaṁ paramaṁ pavitraṁ*  
*prajāpater yat sahajaṁ purastāt*  
*āyuṣyam agryaṁ pratimuñca śubhraṁ*  
*yajñopavitaṁ balam astu tejaḥ*

**”May this sacred thread (the** ***yajñopavita*****), the foremost of all means for a long life, bestow all auspiciousness unto me. This** ***yajñopavita*** **is the supreme of all pavitras, or auspicious items. It has been created in the beginning of creation, along with Brahmā, the lord of all creatures. May its power grant me all strength.”**

6\. Put on the new sacred thread.

7\. Wearing both old thread and new, chant the *gāyatri-mantras* ten times each.

8\. After that, chant the following mantra before removing the old thread:

*upavitaṁ chinna-tantuṁ jirṇaṁ kaśmala-dūṣitam*  
*visṛjāmi punar brahman varco dirghāyur astu me*

**Since the threads of this** ***upavita*** **have been broken, or it has become old, or has been spoiled by impurity, I release it again into Brahman. May it grant vital strength and a long life unto me.**

9\. Remove the old thread from the left shoulder. Bring it down to waist level and break it string by string with both hands.

The best way to dispose of the old thread is to throw it into a holy river. There are few holy rivers outside India, but since rivers in general are considered pure, you may place your thread in any river. If there are no nearby rivers, keep your thread until someone goes to India and can throw it in the Gaṅgā or Yamunā for you. In order to avoid harming birds or other animals that may become tangled in the thread, cut it into pieces before throwing it in the river.

If this is too complicated, you can burn your *upavita*. Under no circumstances, however, should you throw it in the garbage. That would be too disrespectful.

**The proper times**

**Question**: If one misses chanting *gāyatri* at the proper time, what is the actual cut-off time after which he or she should wait for the next *sandhyā*? If sunrise is around 6 A.M., for example, and due to service or forgetfulness someone can’t chant their morning mantras until 8, should they wait and chant two sets of mantras at noon? Or is 8 A.M. still a proper time for the morning *gāyatri*? Where is the “border line”?

**Answer**: It is difficult to give a “border line” for missing the proper *sandhyās*. I can only offer a subjective answer from common sense. We can observe the sun rising, passing through the zenith, and setting. The *sandhyās* are around these times. How far you can expand these periods has to be determined by your own sense of time, which will develop within yourself in time. In other words, by practical experience you will learn to feel when you are “in” or “out” of the *sandhyā* periods. Such experience grows over the years. If you chant the *gāyatri-mantras* during the proper times, you will feel the supportive energy of the respective *sandhyās*; if you miss the *sandhyās* you will feel the waning or even absence of such support.

Best, of course, is to be aware of the benefits of chanting *gāyatri* at the proper times. That will make it easier not to miss the *sandhyās*.

**Facing the proper direction**

**Question**: Regarding which direction one should face while chanting *gāyatri*, You state, “If one is in the presence of the Deity or the *guru*, facing them takes precedence over compass directions.” Does this also hold true of a picture of the Deity or the *guru*?

**Answer**: Yes. For example, if you have established a temporary altar, as when you are traveling or spending the night somewhere away from home, you can face the pictures of the Deities and/or the spiritual master on this altar. The principle is clear: Always show the highest respect to *guru* and Kṛṣṇa, especially when chanting their mantras.

**Why do we cover the hand while chanting?**

**Question**: There are numerous photos of Śrila Prabhupāda in which he is not covering his hand while chanting *gāyatri*. I have heard that this instruction to cover the hand did not come from Prabhupāda or even from the Gaudiya-Vaiṣṇavas but from devotees going to India and bringing back “culture.” Do you want to address this?

**Answer**: In several cases Śrila Prabhupāda established a general principle and allowed the practical application to develop in course of time. As a general principle, for instance, Śrila Prabhupāda said we should always think of Kṛṣṇa. In order to do so, he advised us to read books like Kṛṣṇa-bhāvanāmṛta by Śrila Viśvanātha Cakravarti Thākura, a book that had not even been published at that time. Only when the book was later translated could devotees follow this instruction and thus also honor the principle Prabhupāda was trying to teach.

By establishing general principles, Prabhupāda showed the way; therefore we should never lose sight of these principles. Only then will we be sure that we are always on the path when going into more detailed instructions.

A general principle in chanting one’s *dikṣā-mantra* is privacy. This is strongly confirmed in our scriptures:

*sva-mantro nopadeṣṭavyo vaktavyaś ca na saṁsadi*  
*gopaniyaṁ tathā śāstraṁ rakṣaṇiyaṁ śarira-vat*

**One should not instruct anyone in the mantra publicly, nor reveal his mantra in a public assembly. One should always keep the mantra and the** ***śāstras*** **related to it secret – protecting them as one would his own body.**

Therefore, Śrila Prabhupāda instructed us only to whisper the mantras and not to speak them aloud or tell them to others. To count the mantras secretly by covering the hand is a further detail that underlines the principle of privacy. I have never seen anyone counting his *gāyatri-mantras* openly. And I have seen many pictures of Śrila Prabhupāda covering his hand while chanting his *gāyatri*.

**Cultivating** ***sattva-guṇa***

**Question**: You are emphasizing the necessity to rise to the mode of goodness. Could you please give some practical advice how this can be accomplished?

**Answer**: This topic gives rise to a more important question: How do you develop a certain mode?

The modes of nature are developed by activities, association, and by eating the food related to them. For example, one can develop the mode of ignorance by drinking alcohol, which will soon spread the influence of *tamo-guṇa* throughout the body. *Rajo-guṇa* is increased by working strenuously while remaining strongly attached to the results for one’s own gain. Similarly, *sattva-guṇa* is cultivated by certain activities and attitudes. The following list offers a few practical guidelines on how the mode of goodness can be developed:

1\. mind and sense control

2\. tolerance toward others

3\. discrimination

4\. sticking to one’s prescribed duty

5\. truthfulness

6\. mercy

7\. careful study of the past and future (so one can learn both from what one did well as well as from his or her mistakes)

8\. satisfaction in any condition (recognize everything as Kṛṣṇa’s mercy)

9\. generosity

10\. renunciation of sense gratification

11\. faith in the spiritual master

12\. being embarrassed by one’s improper actions (a willingness to correct mistakes rather than to deny them )

13\. charity

14\. simplicity

15\. humbleness

16\. self-satisfaction

If one practices these principles one will immediately see him or herself becoming elevated. These practices are almost magical: By performing an activity in the mode of goodness, you will experience the purifying influence of this mode. It’s almost like riding an elevator.

Other important means to invite the mode of goodness are to prepare and offer food in the mode of goodness and to seek the association of persons situated in *sattva-guṇa.*

Do you know that in India, mental hospitals always keep cows? When a mentally ill person is restless, he or she can go to the cow and immediately experience relief from the mode of goodness. Try it in your life. You don’t have to be mentally ill; it works for normal people too. Just pet a cow every now and then!

***Gāyatri-japa*** **reform in ten steps**

**Question**: My *gāyatri* chanting has become stale and uninspiring. Could you please suggest how I can reform my practice?

**Answer**: Thank you for your honest question. I think you are not the only one in need of reform. If we are serious about improving our *gāyatri-japa,* we should first try to increase our motivation by deeply understanding the value of the *gāyatri-mantras.* For this I sincerely recommend that you read Bṛhad-Bhāgavatāmṛta, especially the second part where Gopa Kumāra’s enlivening experiences with the *gopāla-mantra* are described.

After having become entirely convinced, you can walk the demanding path of reform. A reform is not always easy. It almost always requires struggling with habits that have almost taken on a life of their own. You will need to be determined and to endeavor in an appropriate way. Only then will you quickly feel the encouragement of success.

I have dedicated a whole chapter to this subject (“Methods of Proper Chanting”). In this section, therefore, I will summarize the reform in ten step:

1\. Become resolved to improve your chanting. For inspiration, read books like Bṛhad-Bhāgavatāmṛta or the Gāyatri Book. They will help you find new appreciation for the *gāyatri-mantras*.

2\. Chant in a place free of distraction, preferably always in the same place.

3\. Chant at the proper times.

4\. Assume a prayerful mood by either first chanting appropriate prayers to Lord Kṛṣṇa or simply by becoming aware of your present state: You are separated from the Lord and these mantras offer you a great opportunity to become reconnected through meditation.

5\. Softly whisper the mantras with clear but inaudible pronunciation, as Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākura suggests (see page 98). This takes practice. You will find that it is much easier to concentrate when you whisper the mantras inaudibly rather than merely reeling them off in an unclear flow of mental recitations, which often skip syllables.

6\. Make sure that you chant all the mantras by counting each mantra ten times on the finger limbs or joints, and that you pronounce each syllable properly.

7\. Become familiar with the meanings of the mantras. While chanting, meditate on their content and significance.

8\. Chant the mantras in a mood of surrender and dedication (*svāhā*).

9\. Feel yourself close to your spiritual master who has imparted the mantras unto you.

10\. Try and try again! Your sincere endeavor will melt the Lord’s heart. Why? Because by chanting sincerely you will show Him that you are not indifferent to having received the great mercy and revelation of these mantras.

**Question**: Is there anything I should avoid while chanting the *gāyatri-mantras*?

**Answer**: Avoid any kind of distraction! Once I saw a devotee with his *brāhmaṇa* thread wrapped around his thumb talking, eating, looking here and there, and then getting up to pace back and forth. He really caught my attention! Finally, I asked him what he was doing. Seriously, and incredulous as to how I could ask such an inappropriate question, he said, “I am chanting my *gāyatri*, Prabhu!”

No comment.

Further, avoid yawning, dozing, scratching, and so on. Only if a very senior Vaiṣṇava approaches you while you are chanting *gāyatri* should you interrupt your chanting and offer him your attention. Afterwards, with the senior Vaiṣṇava’s permission, you should resume your chanting.

**Six steps to control the mind**

**Question**: How can I control my restless mind, which often wanders when I’m chanting *gāyatri*, especially when I’m distressed or feel unenthusiastic?

**Answer**: Whenever spiritual practice becomes lifeless, a devotee has to correct the course of his *sādhana*. Such a devotee can be compared to an airplane pilot who notices that his plane is deviating from the course, perhaps pushed astray by powerful winds. Without hesitation, and fixing his destination in his mind, the pilot should correct his course.

Śrila Sanātana Gosvāmi offers six rules for those who wish to reform their chanting. Each is a powerful way to redirect *japa* back to the course of perfection. These rules are usually used in reference to the *mahā-mantra*, but they are also helpful for those wishing to reform their *gāyatri-japa*:

*manaḥ-saṁskaraṇaṁ śaucaṁ maunaṁ mantrārtha-cintanam*  
*avyagratvam anirvedo japa-sampatti-hetavaḥ*

**In order to perfect one’s japa, one needs to cultivate the following qualities:**

**1.** ***manaḥ-saṁskaraṇa*** **the ability to control the mind**  
**2.** ***śauca*** **internal and external purity**  
**3.** ***mauna*** **the ability to be silent**  
**4.** ***mantra-artha-cintana*** **the ability to contemplate the meaning of the mantra**  
**5.** ***a-vyagratva*** **patience**  
**6.** ***a-nirveda*** **steadfastness**

Let us go through each of these qualities:

1\. *Manaḥ-saṁskaraṇa* (control of the mind) means we must repeatedly bring the mind back whenever it wanders into the world of sense objects.

2\. *Śauca* (purity) refers to external cleanliness and internal purity. We all know that mental contamination sticks to us more firmly than dust, sweat, and mud. To become internally clean, it is important to weed the garden of the heart, removing the six types of weeds mentioned in the Caitanya-caritāmṛta.

3\. *Mauna* (to practice silence) particularly means to strictly avoid unfavorable talk (*prajalpa*). Remaining silent, of course, refers first to practicing silence during *mantra-japa,* but we should be silent at other times as well. An uncontrolled tongue engaged in *prajalpa* will have a long-lasting unfavorable influence on the mind.

4\. *Mantra-artha-cintana* means to contemplate the meaning of one’s mantra (*mantra* – of the mantra; *artha* – on the meaning; *cintana* – contemplation). This is important. In Chapter Five of The Gāyatri Book you will find detailed explanations and meditations on each of the *gāyatri-mantras*.

5\. *A-vyagratva* (patience) means that we should not rush through our chanting simply to finish it quickly (*a* – not; *vi-agra* – distracted, agitated, excited; *tva* – being in such a state). In the Bhagavad-gitā Arjuna calls the mind flickering (*cancala*). *Vyagratva* also refers to the mind’s restless quality. When Arjuna asked how the strong, restless, turbulent, and obstinate mind could be controlled, Kṛṣṇa offered two means: suitable practice (*abhyāsa*) and detachment (*vairāgya*). In his purport to this verse, Śrila Prabhupāda explains that if we regularly hear about Lord Kṛṣṇa, we will become more and more enlightened and thus automatically detached from everything not connected to Kṛṣṇa. These two remedies work together. When the mind is inspired and enlightened by regularly hearing about Kṛṣṇa, and when it is enlivened by the spiritual influence received when we taste food offered to Lord Kṛṣṇa, it will automatically be satisfied and we will get a grip on our impatience.

6\. *A-nirveda* (steadfastness) refers to persistence (*a* – not; *nir-veda* – indifference, disregard, depression). We should not lose hope when we do not attain expected results quickly.

Śrila Bhaktivinoda Thākura offers a similar advice:

*japa-kāle manake ekāgra-bhāve lao*  
*citte śuddha thāka vṛthā kathā nāhi kao*  
*nāmārtha cintana sadā dhairyāśraya kara*  
*nāmete ādara kari kṛṣṇa-nāma smara*

**While chanting** ***japa*****, remember Kṛṣṇa’s name with affectionate respect, concentrate the mind on the chanting alone, be pure in mind, and avoid speaking uselessly. Contemplate the mantra’s meaning and remain patient.**

Every serious chanter of the *gāyatri-mantras* should treasure these jewellike techniques and qualities. Their effulgence lights up the path and makes it possible to see clearly and avoid obstacles. If we treasure these qualities, our chanting will soon become steady and full of spiritual nourishment. Please try to absorb these into your practice.

## 2. Gāyatri philosophy

**The meaning of the word** ***Gāyatri***

**Question**: What is the literal meaning of the word *gāyatri*?

**Answer**: *Gāyatri* is the feminine form of *gāyatra*, which means “song, hymn.” It is derived from the roots *gai* (“to sing, recite in a singing, metrical manner”), and *trai* (“to rescue, protect, deliver”). Thus *gāyatri* is that melodious sound sung in a certain meter that gives protection or delivers us. The Brahma-saṁhitā (5.27) confirms this by stating that the *kāma-gāyatri-mantra* emanated from Kṛṣṇa’s flute; it is a melody sweeter than any other sound, most artful and most sacred. It then entered Brahmā’s ears. The Agni Purāṇa explains further:

*gāyaty uktāni śāstrāṇi tataḥ smṛtyeyaṁ gāyatri prakāśini sa savitur*  
*bhargaṁ prāṇāṁs tathaiva ca sāvitri yatra eva ca vāg-rūpa-tvāt sarasvati*

**Because it sings about or praises \[*****gāyati*****\] the Vedic mantras, the** ***śāstras*****, the Supreme Lord, and one’s life force or intelligence, it is called** ***gāyatri*****. And because it reveals** ***savitṛ*****, the sun-god, it is called** ***sāvitri*****. And it is called** ***sarasvati*** **because it is the embodiment of all speech.**

In the Harivaṁśa, Gāyatri Devi is personified as the wife of Brahmā. She is the mother of the four Vedas as well as of the *dvijas*, the twice-born who form the first three *varṇas* in *varṇāśrama-dharma* (*brāhmaṇas, kṣatriyas*, and *vaiśyas*).

**The meaning of the word** ***Pañcarātra***

**Question**: Could you please explain the meaning of the word *pañcarātra* as well as the origin of this literature?

**Answer**: The literal meaning of *pañcarātra* is “that which has been spoken in five nights” (*pañca* – five; *rātra* – night). Lord Keśava (Viṣṇu or Nārāyaṇa) is said to have taught the esoteric science of the Pañcarātra to Ananta, Garuda, Viṣvaksena, Brahmā, and Rudra over a period of five nights. Śri Nārada Muni provides another explanation:

*rātrañ ca jñāna-vacanaṁ jñānaṁ pañca-vidhaṁ smṛtam tenedaṁ pañcarātraṁ ca pravadanti maniṣiṇaḥ*

***Rātra*** **is a word for** ***jñāna*****, or knowledge, and** ***pañca*** **means five. According to persons with a highly developed mind, there are five kinds of knowledge.**

They are:

1\. knowledge of the Vedas

2\. knowledge of the yoga process

3\. knowledge of the material energy (*māyā*)

4\. knowledge of liberation (*mukti*)

5\. knowledge of devotional service to Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa (*bhakti*)

Alternatively, since the Pañcarātra teaches about the five aspects of God – *para* (highest), *vyūha* (emanation), *vibhava* (incarnation), *antaryāmin* (indweller), and *arca* (form of worship) – it is called Pañcarātra.

The Nārada-pañcarātra deals specifically with knowledge of *bhakti-yoga*. Thus it is important for all Vaiṣṇavas, as Śrila Rūpa Gosvāmi explains:

*śruti-smṛti-purāṇādi- pañcarātra-vidhiṁ vinā aikantiki harer bhaktir utpātāyaiva kalpate*

**Devotional service of the Lord that ignores the authorized Vedic literatures like the Upaniṣads, Purāṇas and Pañcarātra is simply an unnecessary disturbance in society.**

The reason why the *pañcarātra* literature is so important is that it has been spoken directly by Śri Kṛṣṇa. This is confirmed by Śrila Jiva Gosvāmi, who quotes the following verse from Mahābhārata:

*pañcarātrasya kṛtsnasya sarveṣu ca nṛpa-śreṣṭha*  
*vaktā tu bhagavān svayam jñāneṣv eteṣu dṛśyate*

**Kṛṣṇa is the speaker of the whole** ***pañcarātra*** **literature. Therefore, O best of kings,** ***pañcarātra*** **is considered most excellent among all kinds of knowledge \[i.e., the revealed scriptures\].**

Nārada Muni explains how the Nārada-pañcarātra has been compiled:

*jñānaṁ paramātma-tattvaṁ ca tato mrtyuñjayaḥ śambhuḥ dṛṣṭyā sarvaṁ samālokya jñānārthaṁ pañcarātraṁ hi*  
*janma-mṛtyu-jarāpaham samprāpya kṛṣṇa-vaktrataḥ jñānaṁ samprāpya śaṅkarāt cakāra nārado muniḥ*

**The knowledge contained in** ***Nārada-pañcarātra*** **not only puts an end to birth, death, and old age, but it also reveals the Supreme Truth. The most fortunate** ***śambhu*** **(Lord Śiva), who is able to conquer death, heard this knowledge directly from Kṛṣṇa.**  
**After having studied all the revealed scriptures, Nārada Muni heard this knowledge from** ***śaṅkara*** **(Lord Śiva). Thereafter he compiled the** ***Nārada-pañcarātra*****, which deals with the essence of this knowledge.**

**The necessity of** ***brāhmaṇa*** **initiation**

**Question**: If the holy name gives everything, why is *brāhmaṇa* initiation necessary?

**Answer**: *Brāhmaṇa* initiation or *mantra-dikṣā* is important because it enables one to worship the Supreme Personality of Godhead in His *arcā-vigraha,* for which the chanting of *bija*- and *gāyatri-mantras* are necessary.

In the beginning of spiritual practice, the soul is tremendously elevated by chanting the holy name – so much so that even one’s *prārabdha-karma* (manifest reactions) changes. Devahūti offers a wonderful glorification of the holy name in Śrimad-Bhāgavatam:

*yan-nāmadheya-śravaṇānukirtanād yat-prahvaṇād yat-smaraṇād api kvacit śvādo ’pi sadyaḥ savanāya kalpate kutaḥ punas te bhagavan nu darśanāt*

**To say nothing of the spiritual advancement of persons who see the Supreme Person face to face, even a person born in a family of dogeaters immediately becomes eligible to perform Vedic sacrifices if he once utters the holy name of the Supreme Personality of Godhead or chants about Him, hears about His pastimes, offers Him obeisances or even remembers Him.**

Here she says, *sadyaḥ savanāya kalpate*, the chanter immediately becomes eligible to perform Vedic *yajñas*. By the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra a person is brought up to the level of performing *yajñas*, just as a boy who is born in a *brāhmaṇa* family is automatically offered the same opportunity.

But in order to actually sit before the fire and perform the *yajña*, the *brāhmaṇa* boy needs to receive the *upanayana-saṁskāra*, the *mantra-dikṣā*. Similarly, try to understand our own situation: by performing *harināma*, our *karma* is removed and we are considered like “*brāhmaṇa* boys;” that is, the opportunity to worship the Deity comes within reach. But before we are factually allowed to do so, we must first qualify ourselves by receiving the *gāyatri-mantras* through *brāhmaṇa* initiation.

It is true: if simply by chanting the holy name, a devotee attains the highest level of chanting, *śuddha-nāma*, he or she does not need to be formally initiated as a *brāhmaṇa*. Such a devotee has already attained the platform of pure love of Godhead. The ordinary methods of gradual purification become irrelevant to such a person. For ordinary souls like ourselves, however, it is better to be humble and avail ourselves of the great benefits of *mantra-dikṣā.*

We should also note that the *gāyatri-mantras* quickly direct us toward Lord Kṛṣṇa and the spiritual world. The *gāyatri-mantras*, especially the *gopāla*-and *kāma-gāyatri-mantras*, are filled with the Supreme Personality’s names and activities. Thus they add spice to our understanding of the holy name and help us find the holy name more relishable. For dedicated Vaiṣṇavas who cherish the treasure of *nāma-niṣṭhā,* the *gāyatri-mantras* are therefore never seen separately from the holy name. We have discussed this point elaborately in Chapter I of this book.

I would like to strongly request my readers to quickly qualify themselves to receive these sacred mantras, even if we recognize that it is the ecstatic chanting of *śuddha-nāma* that will finally fill our eyes with tears of love.

***Gāyatri*** **and** ***Harināma***

**Question**: In his books, Śrila Prabhupāda almost exclusively glorifies the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra as the very means to attain all spiritual perfection. Thus in his movement the *harināma* or first initiation is emphasized. It is during the first initiation that the disciple receives a new name and enters into a major spiritual commitment by promising to chant sixteen rounds of the *mahā-mantra* daily and to follow the four regulative principles for the rest of his or her life. The *brāhmaṇa* or second initiation, on the other hand, is much less emphasized in Śrila Prabhupāda’s books, and the ceremonies around it are generally less spectacular than those performed for first initiation. So how important, actually, is *mantra-dikṣā* for Śrila Prabhupāda’s followers?

**Answer**: From Śrila Prabhupāda’s books and teachings, and from the statements of *śāstra*, we can clearly see that the holy name is most important. Without chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa no one can attain *kṛṣṇa-prema*. Therefore the *harināma* initiation is a crucial turning point in a *sādhaka’s* life.

To my knowledge, the first *harināma-dikṣā* in our Gaudiya line took place when Advaita *ācārya* initiated Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mother, Śaci Devi. Śri Gaurāṅga, as the young child Nimāi, arranged this *lilā* to stress the necessity of *harināma-dikṣā.* One day Nimāi refused to drink the milk from Mother Śaci’s breast. In great fear and distress she approached Advaita *ācārya* for help. After carefully studying the case, Advaita *ācārya* concluded that the only remedy was to initiate Mother Śaci into the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra.* After Advaita *ācārya* performed the ceremony, child Nimāi again drank his mother’s breast milk with great joy, demonstrating that He would accept food only from devotees who are initiated into the chanting of the holy name.

Bhaktivinoda Thākura also stressed the necessity of *harināma-dikṣā,* and following his and other *ācāryas*’ example Śrila Prabhupāda made this initiation a central aspect of the *guru*-disciple relationship.

However, *mantra-dikṣā* is also important, because it helps a devotee become so spiritually elevated that he or she can chant the holy name purely. As Śrila Prabhupāda explains:

**Unless one is initiated as a** ***brāhmaṇa*****, one cannot worship the Holy Name properly. \[…\] In other words, the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa** ***mahā-mantra*** **is so powerful that it does not depend on official initiation, but if one is initiated and engages in** ***pañcarātra-vidhi*** **(Deity worship, for which one requires the** ***brāhmaṇa*** **initiation), his Kṛṣṇa consciousness will awaken very soon, and his identification with the material world will be vanquished. The more one is free from material identification, the more one can realize that the spirit soul is qualitatively as good as the Supreme Soul. At such a time, when one is situated on the absolute platform, one can understand that the holy name of the Lord and the Lord Himself are identical. At that stage of realization, the holy name of the Lord, the Hare Kṛṣṇa** ***mahā-mantra,*** **cannot be identified with any material sound.**

In other words, *mantra-dikṣā* is extremely helpful when one is trying to realize the higher levels of chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra.* My understanding is that we should take all the help we can in our attempt to go back to Kṛṣṇa. If we accept help in the form of first and second initiation, we can complete our journey back home, back to Godhead in the best possible way.

**Question**: But if the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* is the most important process of spiritual realization, and the *gāyatri-mantras* only support this process, why is an extra initiation necessary to impart the mantra? Since one has already taken shelter at the lotus feet of a bona fide *guru* by first initiation, couldn’t one just take the *gāyatri-mantras* from books and practice them according to what he or she has read?

**Answer**: The initiation process is more than a mere communication of information. Along with the sound vibration of the mantra, the *guru* imparts to a qualified disciple his own realization, conviction, and spiritual determination. Let me give you an example: Once there was a boy who had severe diabetes. Although he was strictly forbidden to eat sugar, he could not desist, and finally he was on the verge of dying from his overindulgence in sugar. One day his mother brought him to a *sādhu* and asked the *sādhu* to forbid her son from eating sweets. She hoped the authority of this famous and revered personality would win over her stubborn son. The *sādhu*, however, did not speak with the boy; instead he advised the mother to come back with him one week later. Slightly discouraged, the mother returned home. But when she came back a week later, the *sādhu* stood sternly before the boy and commanded: “Stop eating sweets!” Deeply impressed and intimidated, the boy agreed to obey and never again ate any sweets.

Later, the woman asked the *sādhu* why instead of helping the boy on their first meeting, he had told them to come back in a week. The *sādhu* smilingly replied, “Mere words would not have convinced your son. I had to impart my determination to him. Thus I first had to stop eating sweets myself. I did so when you left the first time, and a week later my words had the weight to help your boy.”

Do you understand the principle behind this story? Initiation is more than an exchange of words; it is a process of spiritual empowerment. When the qualified *guru* imparts the mantra unto his disciple, it carries the guru’s entire understanding, particular spirit, and his own surrender.

In India, initiation is explained by a quite curious example. It is said that only the seeds of a banyan tree that have been swallowed by a sparrow and have passed through its body will fructify and grow into a new banyan tree. When the seed passes through the sparrow, a coating is applied that enables the seed to grow into a tree. The same happens with the mantra. It has to come to us through a guru in a proper *sampradāya*. Only then can it reveal the spiritual truths it contains.

**Question**: In this regard the question has often been asked that if the *gāyatri-mantras* contain Kṛṣṇa’s names, why don’t they have the same power as *kṛṣṇa-nāma*?Answer: *Kṛṣṇa-nāma* belongs to the culture of spontaneous devotional service. Thus it achieves something different than the culture of *mantra-dikṣā,* which is more on the side of devotion according to the rules. Śrila Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākura makes an interesting statement in this regard:

**The fourth case \[the dative case, indicated by the grammatical ending -*****aya*****, as in** ***namaḥ kṛṣṇāya*****\] is used to indicate** ***śaraṇāgati*** **or** ***ātma-samarpaṇa*****, or self-surrender. \[…\] If one wants to chant with full surrender and without duplicity by using the fourth, or dative, case of the holy names of the Lord, then he is chanting mantra. If one chants the holy names of the Lord as an address, by using the vocative case \[indicated by the endings -*****a*** **or -*****e*****, as in** ***hare*****,** ***kṛṣṇa*****, and** ***rāma*****\], then he is engaged in** ***bhajana*****, or worship, of the holy names.**

**Chanting the holy names in their fourth case \[*****mantra*****\] indicates full surrender, whereas chanting the holy names in the form of an address \[*****nāma*****\] indicates a desire for eternal service. An initiated person is liberated from the bondage of material existence by chanting mantra, and a liberated soul exhibits his eternal** ***bhajana*** **by chanting the holy names as an address.**

In other words, the grammatical construction of *mantra* (dative case) and *nāma* (vocative case) indicates a different mood and orientation in each. *Mantra* emphasizes self-surrender, where Kṛṣṇa is formally addressed as the Supreme Lord. *Nāma*, however, aims at the intimate relationship with Kṛṣṇa in one of the confidential *rasas*. Caitanya Mahāprabhu explains this particular feature of *nāma* in his conversation with Prakāśānanda Sarasvati:

*kṛṣṇa-nāma-mahā-mantrera ei ta’ svabhāva*  
*yei jape, tāra kṛṣṇe upajaye bhāva*

**It is the nature of the Hare Kṛṣṇa** ***mahā-mantra*** **that anyone who chants it immediately develops his loving ecstasy for Kṛṣṇa.16**

*Nāma-seva, nāma-bhajana,* and ultimately, ecstatic *nāma-saṅkirtana* churn the ocean of devotion to such a degree that the chanter is carried by its high waves beyond the peaceful but limiting coasts of ordinary spirituality, based on the more formal conduct of worship. This end can only be achieved by the holy name chanted in a mood of spontaneous attraction. In other words, it is achieved when the holy name is chanted with loving devotion, like a child calling out for its mother.

Let us again look at the glorious example of Gopa-kumāra. At one stage of his spiritual journey he added the performance of *nāma-saṅkirtana* to his chanting of the *dikṣā-mantras*, and only then was he immediately carried to the highest spiritual destination, the Lord’s divine pastimes.

**The** ***Brahma-gāyatri*** **and Caitanya Mahāprabhu**

**Question**: Is there a way to see Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu in the *brahma-gāyatri-mantra*?

**Answer**: There is indeed a hint of Caitanya Mahāprabhu in the *brahma-gāyatri*. After having pronounced the famous *kṛṣṇa-varṇaṁ tviṣākṛṣṇam* verse, the sage Śri Karabhājana continues:

*dhyeyaṁ sadā paribhava-ghnam abhiṣṭa-dohaṁ*  
*tirthāspadaṁ Śiva-viriñci-nutaṁ śaraṇyam*  
*bhṛtyārti-haṁ praṇata-pāla bhavābdhi-potaṁ*  
*vande mahā-puruṣa te caraṇāravindam*

**My dear Lord, You are the Mahā-puruṣa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and I worship Your lotus feet, which are the only eternal object of meditation. Those feet destroy the embarrassing conditions of material life and freely award the greatest desire of the soul, the attainment of pure love of Godhead. My dear Lord, Your lotus feet are the shelter of all holy places and of all saintly authorities in the line of devotional service and are honored by powerful demigods like Lord Śiva and Lord Brahmā. My Lord, You are so kind that You willingly protect all those who simply bow down to You with respect, and thus You mercifully relieve all the distress of Your servants. In conclusion, my Lord, Your lotus feet are actually the suitable boat for crossing over the ocean of birth and death, and therefore even Lord Brahmā and Lord Śiva seek shelter at Your lotus feet.**

According to Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākura, the word *maha-puruṣa* in the last line of this verse directly indicates Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu, whereas the first word of this verse, *dhyeyam*, means *dhimahi*, referring to the *brahma-gāyatri-mantra.* Thus according to our *ācārya* Śrila Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākura, Śri Caitanya is the hidden subject of the *brahma-gāyatri*.

**How many syllables are in** ***Kāma-gāyatri*****?**

**Question**: According to the Caitanya-caritāmṛta, the *kāma-gāyatri* is composed of twenty-four-and-a-half syllables. However, by counting the syllables according to Sanskrit grammatical rules, it seems to actually be twenty-five syllables. How is that explained? Where is the half-syllable in *kāma-gāyatri*?

**Answer**: This is indeed a difficult question. Our great *ācārya*, Śrila Viśvanātha Cakravarti Thākura, was once confronted with the same problem. Actually, this problem plunged him into an ocean of distress until he was miraculously saved by Śrimati Rādhārāṇi.As you have mentioned, Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu explains in His teachings to Sanātana Gosvāmi that there are twenty-four-and-a-half moons or syllables in the *kāma-gāyatri*:

**Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, is identical with the Vedic hymn known as the** ***kāma-gāyatri*****, which is composed of twenty-four and a half syllables. Those syllables are compared to moons that arise in Kṛṣṇa. Thus all three worlds are filled with desire.**

**The face of Kṛṣṇa is the king of all moons, and the body of Kṛṣṇa is the throne. Thus the king governs a society of moons.**

**Kṛṣṇa has two cheeks that shine like glowing gems. Both are considered full moons. His forehead is considered a half moon, and the spot of sandalwood there is considered a full moon.**

**His fingernails are many full moons, and they dance on the flute in His hands. Their song is the melody of that flute. His toenails are also many full moons, and they dance on the ground. Their song is the jingling of His ankle bells.**

Accepting Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmi’s words as authoritative, Viśvanātha Cakravarti Thākura first accepted Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s statement about the number of syllables without question. Then a doubt crossed his mind as he counted the syllables:

\- kliṁ 1 syllable

\- vidmahe 3 syllables

\- kāmadevāya 5 syllables

\- dhimahi 3 syllables

\- puṣpabāṇāya 5 syllables

\- pracodayāt 4 syllables

\- tan no ’naṅgaḥ 4 syllablesThis makes twenty-five syllables instead of twenty-four-and-a-half. Viśvanātha Cakravarti Thākura writes:

**I wondered on the basis of what scriptural proof did Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmi say that the mantra has only twenty-four-and-a-half syllables? The reason for this remained beyond the comprehension of my intelligence.**

**Upon reviewing various scriptures, I found no hint at all to the possibility that there could be a half syllable in the mantra. Therefore I remained deeply immersed in the greatest ocean of doubt. \[…\] Searching through all the works describing vowels and consonants, I could not find any statement about this mantra consisting of twenty-four-and-a-half syllables.**

**Is it possible that Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmi was not aware of this fact? No, this is not possible! He is definitely in full knowledge of everything, and he is therefore completely free from the common material defects of error or illusion.**

Viśvanātha Cakravarti Thākura then looked at the verses from a number of points of view, but he found no solution. Finally, he gave up all attempts to find an answer. He became so dejected that he refused to eat and drink, and in the end when it was clear he could not resolve his doubt, decided that it would be better to give up his life. Such was his determination. He went to Rādhā-kuṇda and remained immersed in his sad thoughts. He writes: “If one is not even properly aware of the syllables of the mantra, how will it ever be possible to become conscious of the Lord? Therefore it is proper to give up one’s body.”

That night, Śrimati Rādhikā revealed Herself to him in a dream and said, “O Viśvanātha, Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja has written the truth! He is My confidential maidservant and thus knows everything about the secrets of My innermost mood. This *kāma-gāyatri-mantra* is meant for worshiping Me. One can know Me by knowing the syllables of this mantra, but only by My mercy is this possible!”

Śrimati Rādhikā then revealed that the solution to Viśvanātha’s question could be found in the book called Varṇāgama-bhāsvadi, “Illumination on Counting the Letters.” Knowing this book, Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmi wrote as he did. She told Viśvanātha that upon waking he should refer to this book and then compile the evidence to settle this question for others’ benefit.

Guided by Śrimati Rādhārāṇi, Viśvanātha Cakravarti Thākura finally found the evidence he sought and properly established the number of syllables in the *kāma-gāyatri-mantra* by quoting the following verse:

*vi-kārānta-ya kāreṇa cārdhākṣaraṁ prakirtitam*

**The letter** ***ya*****, if followed by the letter** ***vi*****, is considered a half-syllable (*****ardha-akṣaram*****).**

Thus the *ya* in *kāmadevāya* counts as a half-syllable because it is followed by the *vi* in *vidmahe*. Thus the *kāma-gāyatri-mantra* has twenty-four-and-a-half syllables. Similarly, the *gaura-gāyatri-mantra* consists of twenty-three-and-a-half syllables, as you may count for yourself.

## 3. Gāyatri culture

**The history of** ***Gāyatri***

**Question**: It would be interesting to know the history of the *gāyatri-mantras* and the *brāhmaṇa* thread. What is the meaning of the *upavita*, and what role does it play in a Vaiṣṇava’s life? How were the *gāyatri-mantras* introduced into the Gaudiya tradition? Since after Lord Caitanya most Vaiṣṇavas accepted *bābaji* initiation and *bābaji-veśa* and did not wear *brāhmaṇa* threads, it would be interesting to know why we do so now.

**Answer**: The *brāhmaṇa* thread is not mentioned in Vaiṣṇava scriptures. Wearing it is a Vedic custom. Historians hypothesize that it is a symbolic representation of the deerskin or upper cloth that was once worn by Vedic *brāhmaṇas*. In any case, it is not related directly to Vaiṣṇava culture.

However, the thread and *brahma-gāyatri-mantra* were introduced by Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākura to show that anyone who is initiated as a Vaiṣṇava is above the castes and can perform all the rituals normally performed only by *brāhmaṇas*. So along with giving *vaiṣṇava-mantras*, Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākura also performed the Vedic *saṁskāra* of *upanayana* (giving the mantra and thread in a *yajña* ceremony).

The *brahma-gāyatri* is Vedic. The *kṛṣṇa-mantras* are mentioned in Caitanya-caritāmṛta and Brahma-saṁhitā. The *gaura-mantras* have been traced to a scripture called *iśāna-saṁhitā.* We do not know the origin of the *guru-mantra*.

According to other sources, the *guru-* and *gaura-gāyatris* were introduced by Jāhnavā Devi, Nityānanda Prabhu’s wife.

***Sandhyā-vandana*** **in the vedic tradition**

**Question**: Could you please tell something more about the significance of the sacred thread ceremony (*upanayana*-*saṁskāra*) and the worship of the Lord during the *sandhyās* (*sandhyā-vandana*) as seen from the Vedic tradition?**Answer**: This is a complex topic. As an introductory information, it may be interesting to hear from a *brāhmaṇa* of the Śri-sampradāya, since the followers of Rāmānujācārya in South India are still closely connected to the ancient Vedic tradition. Therefore I will quote from an article written by a Śri-Vaiṣṇava author, where he speaks about the traditional origin of *sandhyā-vandana* and its significance in terms of Vedic concepts. It shows how deeply the purpose of human civilization – spiritual upliftment – was woven into Vedic society:

**Everyone is born a** ***prakṛta-puruṣa*****, not a** ***saṁskṛta-puruṣa.*** **The former lives according to his** ***prakṛti*****, nature or instinct. Therefore, for all practical purposes he is an ”animal man.” His life is not governed by any discipline. It is an unrefined life. The Veda allows a person to be so. But only for eight or ten years. Afterwards, one has to enter the second stage.**

**This second stage is called the** ***dvija*** **stage. What makes a person a** ***dvija*****? The** ***dvija’s*** **life is governed by** ***dharma-adharma*****, right and wrong. He should avoid what is wrong, according to the Veda.**

**Discipline means to develop certain habits as early in life as possible, starting with getting up early in the morning. This discipline should be based on knowledge and conviction.**

**Veda converts an unrefined person into a refined person. Thus Veda becomes the mother. If a mother is to give birth, a father is required. Here the** ***ācārya*** **is the father. The mother can give birth to a child only with the help of the father. Veda can be conveyed only with the help of an** ***ācārya*****. The first** ***janma*** **takes place from the natural mother. For the second birth, the mother is Mātā Gāyatri – the Mother Veda. The father is the teacher or** ***guru*****. The** ***upanayanam*** **– the sacred thread – marks the transference from the first stage to the second stage.**

**S*****andhyā-vandana is nitya-karma*****, activity prescribed in the Veda. It varies according to a person’s** ***varṇa*** **and** ***veda*****. It is different for** ***brāh-maṇas*****,** ***kṣatriyas*****, and** ***vaiśyas*****.**

**What benefits do we derive from** ***sandhyā-vandana*****? According to Veda,** ***nitya-karmas*** **are to be done purely for the sake of spiritual upliftment. If done regularly, we will make spiritual progress, which consists of** ***citta-śuddhi*** **(purity of consciousness),** ***bhagavad-anu-grahā*** **(attachment to the Supreme Lord), and** ***citta-viśāradā*** **(spiritual discrimination).**

**Still, even though we are not supposed to ask for material benefit, there are many material benefits that come from practicing** ***sandhyā-vandana.*** **In the prayers that form part of the** ***sandhyā-vandana,*** **we ask the Lord for long life, health, and prosperity, and ultimately, we ask for more** ***jñāna*****.**

**What is the meaning of the word** ***sandhyā-vandana*****?** ***Vandana*** **means worship or prayer. Both worship and meditation are involved in** ***sandhyā-vandana*****. What is the meaning of** ***sandhyā*****?** ***Sandhyā*** **means the time of union. It means dawn, noon, and dusk.** ***Sandhyā-vandana*** **means a ritual done at the time of these three junctions, dawn, noon, and dusk.**

**On the spiritual level, however,** ***sandhyā-vandana*** **is a junction between the devotee and the Lord. Between** ***jivātmā*** **and** ***Paramātmā*****.**

Śrila Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākura quotes several verses from various scriptures that underline how *sandhyā-vandana* is firmly routed in the Vedic tradition:

**It is stated in the Vyāsa-gitā of the Kūrma Purāṇa: “One should properly sit facing east on a mat made of** ***kuśa*** **grass. With a controlled mind he should practice** ***prāṇāyāma*** **three times a day before engaging in** ***sandhyā*** **meditation. This is the injunction of the Vedic literature.”**

**It is further described in the Manu-saṁhitā: “A** ***brāhmaṇa*** **should meditate on and chant the mantra of the goddess Sāvitri, who is situated within the sun globe. Thereafter he should perform** ***sandhyā*** **worship.”**

**One of the** ***sandhyā-mantras*** **is as follows:**

*oṁ drupād iva mumucānaḥ svinnaḥ snāto malād iva*  
*pūtaṁ pavitreṇevājyam āpaḥ śuddhantu mainasaḥ*

**“Just as by taking bath in the pure water of a holy river one becomes cleansed of all dirt, similarly may this water with its pure qualities purify me from my sinful reactions.”**

**If one does not perform** ***sandhyā*** **then he incurs sin. It is stated: “A person devoid of** ***sandhyā*** **is permanently impure and useless for everything. Whatever he does will not yield any result. O best of the** ***brāhmaṇas*****, a person who neglects his** ***sandhyā*** **worship and endeavors for other religious activities certainly goes to thousands of hells.”**

With the next quotes, Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākura underlines the significance of worshiping Savitā, the transcendental sun-god, as an essential part of the traditional *sandhyā-vandana*:

**It is stated in Baudhāyana-smṛti: “A learned person worships Lord Hari by pouring oblations of** ***ghi*** **in the fire, by offering flowers in water, by meditating on Him in the heart, and by meditating on Him as being present in the sun globe.”**

**In the conversation between Śri Vyāsadeva and Ambariṣa Mahārāja described in the Padma Purāṇa it is stated: “Offering water to Hari in the sun planet and in the water is the best form of worship.”**

**The process of Tantric** ***sandhyā*** **is as follows: “Thereafter one should chant the** ***mūla-mantra*** **and meditate on the lotus feet of Śri Kṛṣṇa while chanting, ‘I am offering you this oblation of water.’ Chanting this, an intelligent person should offer oblations of water to the Lord three times. One should properly meditate on Kṛṣṇa, who is situated in the sun globe, while chanting the** ***kāma-gāyatri-mantra*** **ten times. Thereafter one should pray to Kṛṣṇa by saying, ‘Please forgive my offenses,’ and then he should offer oblations to the sun.”**

**The origin of** ***sandhyā-vandana***

**Question**: I heard that the practice of *sandhyā-vandana* is rooted in an ancient account described in the Vedas. Is this true?

**Answer**: The origin of *sandhyā-vandana* is indeed described in scripture, particularly in the Taittiriya-āraṇyaka. There the *veda-puruṣa* (presiding deity of the Vedas) recounts an event that gave rise to the practice of *sandhyā-vandana*:

Once upon a time, the *asuras* performed sincere and intense penance. *Prajāpati* appeared before them and asked what they wanted. The asuras told Prajāpati that they wished to fight with Aditya, the sun-god, who is also known as Sūryadeva. “So be it,” Prajāpati pronounced.

From that day on, the *asuras* began to fight with Sūrya at every sunrise and sunset. Because of this constant battle, Sūrya began to weaken. Since disabling Sūrya could have disastrous consequences for the whole universe, Prajāpati devised a way to save Sūrya from his gradual decline. He ordered the *brāhmaṇas* to perform *sandhyā-vandana* at sunrise and sunset, with the important component of *arghya-pradāna* (throwing water held in their joined palms at the sun). This concerted throwing of water by all the *brāhmaṇas* became a weapon as powerful as the *vajrāyudha* (Indra’s thunderbolt). The water attacked the *asuras* and sent them to the remote island known as Mandiharuṇa-dvipa. There they were vanquished.

However, they were not permanently defeated. Their defeat lasted only until the next *sandhyā*, because the boon Prajāpati had granted them could not go in vain. Thus by the next sunrise the *asuras* were back in full vigor to fight with the sun. Again the *brāhmaṇas* performed *sandhyā- vandana* and again the *asuras* were banished. This process goes on indefinitely.

This story emphasizes the importance of a timely performance of *sandhyā-vandana*. It must be done precisely at sunrise and sunset, since that is when the sun is in danger from the *asuras*. Only timely *arghya-pradānam* vanquishes the *asuras*.

Of course, this somewhat esoteric explanation is not the real reason we observe the *sandhyās*.

***Brahma-gāyatri*** **in the** ***gaudiya*** **tradition**

**Question**: I have heard from a traditional South Indian *brāhmaṇa* that women should not be initiated into *brahma-gāyatri*. Chanting this mantra would harden their hearts. If this is true, then why did Śrila Prabhupāda change this tradition?

**Answer**: In answering your question I would like to go back in time a little. Although Lord Caitanya has emphasized hearing and chanting the holy name as the principle *sādhana* for all devotees, He nevertheless showed by His own example that those interested in spiritual progress must take initiation into the *vaiṣṇava-pañcarātrika* system. Thus In Lord Caitanya’s movement both chanting the holy name and receiving *pañcarātrika-mantras* from a bona fide *guru* are fundamental.

However, the traditional Vedic initiation (*upanayana-saṁskāra*), in which a devotee receives the *brahma-gāyatri-mantra*, did not play a crucial role in the *gaudiya-sampradāya* at that time. Almost all Vaiṣṇavas would chant the *gopāla-mantra* and *kāma-gāyatri*, but even those born in *brāhmaṇa* families would generally stop chanting the *brahma-gāyatri* upon receiving *vaiṣṇava-dikṣā*. Often they would even give up their sacred threads, which marked them as high-born *brāhmaṇas*. Abandoning such traditional practices that were performed by all types of religious people and not only by those who worshiped Lord Krsna was how these persons exhibited their full and exclusive surrender to Kṛṣṇa. This was before Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākura’s time.

Finally, the social and religious results of this custom proved discouraging. The caste *brāhmaṇas* took over the religious scene, stating that Vaiṣṇavas were not qualified brāhmaṇas and thus not entitled to worship the Deities or administer religious services. Consequently, Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākura undertook great effort to raise Vaiṣṇavism to a socially accepted status. To accomplish this, he propagated the *daivi-varṇāśrama-dharma*, which is divine and not based on birthright. Arguing on the basis of *pañcarātrika* and *bhāgavata* philosophy he proved that an initiated Vaiṣṇava is even more qualified than a *brāhmaṇa*. To substantiate his position he introduced Vedic *dikṣā-saṁskāra* to his disciples, including the *brahma-gāyatri-mantra* and the *brāhmaṇa* thread. His idea was to establish Vaiṣṇavism in society by bringing *brāhmaṇa* threads and the *brahma-gāyatri-mantra* to those who were qualified by their qualities and activities, even if they were not born *brāhmaṇas*. It was, in other words, a public demonstration of the statement in *śāstra* that Vaiṣṇavism is the highest form of religious expression.

Still, in order to keep his *daivi-varṇāśrama* system close to tradition, Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākura followed the old Vedic rule and gave *brahma-gāyatri* only to men, not to women. The fact that he gave *upanayana-dikṣā* to his (*male*) disciples regardless of their social background caused enough turmoil among the *smārtas* (caste *brāhmaṇas*). Neither were the women at any spiritual disadvantage; they still received the much more potent *gopāla-mantra* and *kāma-gāyatri.*

So Śrila Sarasvati Thākura changed the Gaudiya tradition because he wanted to establish Vaiṣṇavism not only as a religion fully accepted by society but as the highest form of worship, superior even to traditional Brahmanism.

One generation later, his most prominent disciple made yet another change. Śrila Prabhupāda mercifully considered that in the age of Kali, men and women need whatever help is available to them. He didn’t consider the danger of women’s hearts becoming hard significant, since in *kali-yuga* the hearts of men and women are equally hard. Both genders have become absorbed in the mood of dominating material nature. From the spiritual viewpoint, therefore, both have acquired *puruṣa-bhāva*, the mentality of a male. Therefore both need to worship the Supreme Savitā, the sun-god Kṛṣṇa, so that they can melt the hard ice packed around their hearts.

Once in New York on a morning walk in the cold winter air, Śrila Prabhupāda repeatedly left the footpath to break the ice covering the puddles, much to the astonishment of his disciples. When they later asked him why he had done so, Śrila Prabhupāda answered that just as the water had frozen in the cold winter atmosphere, the hearts of the conditioned souls are also frozen in the icy atmosphere of the false ego. It was his duty, he said, to help them become free from this calamity. To make his point clear he had cracked the winter ice.

Another important consideration why qualified women can chant the *brahma-gāyatri* without fearing any negative influence is that the Gaudiya-Vaiṣṇavas chant *gāyatri* with an entirely different conception than the members of the old Vedic *varṇāśrama* society. For us, the sun-god in the *brahma-gāyatri* is Kṛṣṇa. And as we have seen before (page 237) those with finer spiritual insight can even detect Śrimati Rādhārāṇi’s presence in the *brahma-gāyatri*.

***Brāhmaṇa*** **and** ***vaiṣṇava***

**Question**: With all due respect to your sincere attempt to spread dharma, your ISKCON *brāhmaṇas* cannot really be considered real *brāhmaṇas*. You were not born *brāhmaṇas* but Westerners, and the materialistic influences of Western culture are still deep in your genes. I am a *brāhmaṇa* from Hyderabad. My father was a *brāhmaṇa*, his father was a *brāhmaṇa* but you are pretenders. \[Questioner becomes angry and begins to shout.\]

**Answer**: Thank you for asking your question. Please calm down and don’t be agitated. That is certainly not very brahminical. Śrila Śridhara Svāmi said:

*śamādibhir eva brāhmaṇadi-vyavahāro mukhyaḥ,*  
*na jāti-mātrādity aha – yasyeti. yad yadi anyatra varṇāntare ’pi dṛśyeta,*  
*tad-varṇāntaraṁ tenaiva lakṣaṇa-nimittenaiva varṇena vinirdiśet,*  
*na tu jāti-nimittenety arthaḥ.*

**The most important criterion for deciding whether to deal with someone as a** ***brāhmaṇa*** **or as a member of another** ***varṇa*** **is the presence or absence of self-control and similar qualities.**

Let me try to answer your question by first addressing your assumptions. You seem to be deciding someone’s status based on birth, but this criterion is unsatisfactory. Imagine if we designated someone a lawyer simply because his father was a lawyer. The designated “lawyer” might disappoint us. Nārada Muni, one of the twelve *mahājanas*, has stated that we should judge a person by his qualities rather than by his physical birth:

*yasya yal lakṣaṇaṁ proktaṁ puṁso varṇābhivyañjakam*  
*yad anyatrāpi dṛśyeta tat tenaiva vinirdiśet*

**If one shows the symptoms of being a** ***brāhmaṇa*****,** ***kṣatriya*****,** ***vaiśya*** **or** ***śūdra*****, even if he has appeared in a different class, he should be accepted according to those symptoms of classification.**

The great *ācārya*, Śrila Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākura, has stated that it is more glorious to be *dvija* (twice-born by initiation) than to simply be born in a *brāhmaṇa* family, because a dvija has obtained the qualification to enter the path of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, which leads to the spiritual world:

**The status of a twice-born that is obtained in accordance with one’s worldly nature and lineage by the fettered souls in this mundane world is far inferior to that of the twice-born who obtains admission into the transcendental world; because the initiation or acquisition of transcendental birth as a result of spiritual initiation is the highest of glories in as much as the** ***jiva*** **is thereby enabled to attain to the transcendental realm.**

There are many great personalities in our Vaiṣṇava tradition who have accepted this idea, even though they had the prestige of being born *brāhmaṇas*. Therefore they begged for initiation from great Vaiṣṇavas even if they were not descendants of *brāhmaṇas* but were born even in *śūdra* families.

**Question**: Thank you for your eloquent answer. But I have heard and seen that some of your ISKCON *brāhmaṇas* are not behaving according to common moral standards.**Answer**: Unfortunately, you will find such problems in all societies. Not all members of a society act according to the society’s expectations. Śrila Prabhupāda was well aware of this problem and would sometimes admonish his *brāhmaṇa* disciples, as in the following example:

**Anyone can purchase a thread from the market, two paisa worth or one cent worth and become a** ***brāhmaṇa*****. “You are a** ***brāhmaṇa*****?” “Yes, you see my sacred thread?” Finished. “What you are doing?” “Never mind!”**

**Don’t be such** ***brāhmaṇas*****, at least in our camp. You must follow the rules and regulations. Don’t show that “I am now doubly initiated, sacred thread.” Don’t cheat in that way.”**

Our spiritual master demanded that we become qualified. He wanted his *brāhmaṇa* disciples to follow the appropriate standards of behavior and practice. Let’s not criticize all ISKCON *brāhmaṇas* just because a few here and there have behaved improperly. If you get to know the ISKCON devotees more closely, you will see that there are real jewels among them, and some are also qualified *brāhmaṇas*.

**Study questions**

In order to help you understand, remember and apply some of the central points of the Gayatri Book, we have included a list of study questions for each chapter. You may find this list in appendix 1.

## Epilogue

A book usually has two kinds of readers, those who discover a new world of information, and those who read to gain a new perspective on information they already had. I would like to take this opportunity to address both kinds of readers.

We started this book with an analogy comparing the world of the mantras with the complexity of an ancient temple’s architecture. A person who is entering the fascinating world of the *gāyatri-mantras* for the first time will be intrigued, if not overwhelmed, by the amazing “architecture” of the mantras. Soon he will realize that he needs a guide if he is to properly learn of the details.

But we have not yet come to the end of our analogy. The most important part of our tour through the temple of *gāyatri* is still awaiting us: the *darśana* of the Deity! Any temple, however beautifully built, is interesting mainly because of the Deities who are worshiped there. Unlike ordinary tourists, who are interested only in an aesthetic or cultural aspects of a temple, transcendentalists seek to contact the sacred dimension. They want to meet the Lord.

Therefore we are not much interested in the *gāyatri-mantras* as grammatical or poetic compositions. They are fascinating because they reveal Śri Śri Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa and Their pastimes, the *guru*, and Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu. If we chant the mantras properly, and if we support our chanting with proper spiritual practice (*sādhana*) and a personal connection with our *guru-paramparā*, these divine personalities will manifest.

One important point made in this book is that the Lord manifests according to one’s meditation. We have tried to help the sincere chanter to properly attune himself so that he may be ready to receive the *gāyatri* revelations coming through the transcendental “wire” of our *paramparā*.

So where is the altar, that sacred center that offers entrance into spiritual revelation, in our temple of the *gāyatri-mantras*? The altar is in the temple of our heart. Śrila Prabhupāda once said:

**So you create such a situation within your heart: “Now Kṛṣṇa has seated Himself. Let me wash His feet with Gaṅgā water and Yamunā water, etc. Now I change His dress to a first class costly garment. Then I decorate Him with ornaments. Then I give Him something for eating.” You can simply think of this. This is meditation.** ***Svantaḥ-sthena gadābhṛtā*****, it is such a nice thing. Anywhere you can sit down and think that Kṛṣṇa is sitting in your heart and you are receiving Him in such a nice way. These thoughts are not false. They are also a fact. It is so easy.**

Ultimately, our *gāyatri-japa* is meant to bring us to the goal of witnessing the Lord’s appearance in the temple of our own heart. I pray that all of us will receive the mercy of *guru* and Kṛṣṇa so that we may attain this end.

## Appendix

## 1. Questions on the Gayatri book

These questions will help you understand, remember and apply some of the central points of the Gayatri Book.

**Chapter 1: What is Gāyatri?**

1\. a) Name all the *gāyatri-mantras* that were given by Śrila Prabhupāda.  
b) Who is worshipped by each of these mantras?

2\. Which mantra is superior – the gāyatri-mantra or the mahā-mantra? Explain your answer and describe the fruits each mantra awards.

3\. Name and explain the two different ways of approaching the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

4\. Name and explain the five saṁskāras which are essential for spiritual initiation. Why is initiation incomplete without them?

5\. The Holy Name has the potency to give us Kṛṣṇa-prema, why then do we need the gāyatri-mantras?

6\. What will the gāyatri- and bija-mantras reveal to a sincere chanter?

**Chapter 2: Preparing the Ground**

1\. How does one attain the goal intended by chanting a mantra?

2\. Why is it important to have a saṅkalpa (fixed goal) while chanting the gāyatri-mantra?

3\. Which two processes are recommended by our sampradāya to help the sādhaka firmly establish his saṅkalpa?

4\. When will the gāyatri-mantra become fully effective?

5\. Name three aspects of the gāyatri-mantra which one should meditate upon.

6\. What are the most important aspects of Lord Caitanya’s siddhanta?

**Chapter 3: Becoming Qualified**
