1\. Explain the five powerful obstacles on the spiritual path which Śrila Viśvanātha Cakravarti Thākura illustrates. What is their cause?

2\. How can one overcome the above mentioned obstacles?

3\. In the Bhāgavad-gitā Śri Kṛṣṇa mentions the qualities of brāhmaṇas. Quote the Sanskrit verse and list these qualities.

4\. Discuss one quality you would personally like to develop further, give reasons for your choice.

5\. What are the six engagements of a brāhmana?

6\. How is a vaiṣṇava-brāhmaṇa different to a varṇāśrama-brāhmaṇa?

7\. Which three practices can bring us quickly to the brahminical platform?

8\. a) Why is one of the gāyatri-mantras dedicated to the guru?  
b) How does a disciple repay his debt to the guru?

9\. What single quality, when practised, will result in all the other twenty-five qualities of a vaiṣṇava?

10\. What is the standard that Śrila Prabhupāda expected from his second initiated disciples?

**Chapter 4: The practice**

1\. Name two external and two internal rules or recommendations for chanting the gāyatri-mantra.

2\. What are the main aṅgas (limbs) of bhakti-sādhana? Why?

3\. Explain the basic types of Pañcarātrika-mantras. What are they based upon according to Śrila Jiva Gosvāmi?

4\. Why is it important to chant the gāyatri-mantras during the sandhyās?

5\. Why should one offer obeisance to Śri Guru after after chanting the gāyatri-mantras?

6\. What is the best way to determine the time of the sandhyā?

7\. Why should one sit on an asana for gāyatri chanting?

8\. When should the gāyatri-mantras not be chanted?

9\. a) Why should smaraṇa always be practiced in conjunction with nāma-saṅkirtana?  
b) Explain the two ways of practicing smaraṇa according to Śrila Jiva Gosvāmi.

**Chapter 5: The bijas**

1\. What is a bija-mantra?

2\. Why do different bija-mantras precede the gāyatri-mantras?

3\. Name the three bija-mantras, the energy and Deity they represent? Who do they invoke?

**Chapter 6: The mantras**

1\. What is the purpose of chanting the mūla mantra during one’s gāyatri?

2\. On which of the numerous meanings of the brahma-gāyatri-mantra will you meditate upon?

3\. How does Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmi explain the meaning of Śri Gaurāṅga’s name Viśvambhara?

4\. What is the principle purpose of chanting the gāyatri-mantra?

5\. In which spirit should one chant the gāyatri-mantra?

6\. Explain the presence of sambandha, abhideya and prayojana in the gopāl gāyatri.

7\. Why is the kāma-gāyatri in a unique position?

8\. What are the three desires of a true kṛṣṇa-bhakta which can be fulfilled by chanting the kāma-gāyatri?

10\. The kāma-gāyatri-mantra is the most excellent of all gāyatri-mantras. Still it should it be chanted together with gopāl-gāyatri-mantra, Why?

11\. What will the gāyatri-mantra help us to overcome?

**Chapter 8: The Gāyatri treasury**

1\. Why is it important for a disciple to take time to get to know his guru?

2\. List the main qualities of a bona-fide disciple that you sincerely want to develop, give reasons.

3\. How does the Śrimad Bhāgavatam serve as a bhasya (commentary) to the brahma- gāyatri? And how is it dedicated to Śri Kṛṣṇa?

4\. What is the gradation in the confidentiality of the names in the gopāl-gayatri-mantra? Please explain it.

5\. How is Śrimati Rādhārāṇi present in the different gāyatri-mantras?

6\. Why is the worship of Guru-Gaurāṅga essential before one worships Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa?

**Question & answers**

1\. Under which circumstances should we interrupt the chanting of our gāyatri-mantras?

2\. Is there a special way of exchanging the brāhmaṇa thread? Where will you find the necessary information?

**Epilogue**

1\. What is the ultimate goal we are seeking by the chanting of the gāyatri-mantras? Please answer this question and kindly include your own ultimate goals in spiritual life.

Write a short essay.

We hope that answering these questions has helped you to better comprehend what you have read. Spiritual truth can be best learnt by going through four steps:

\- Step one: hearing

\- Step two: reflecting on what you have heard

\- Step three: applying the new information, and

\- Step four: praying for realization

Of course, ultimately it is experience that is the best teacher. May all the readers of this book enter the wonderful ocean of realizations that the gāyatri-mantras have in store for us.

## 2. About the author

Śacinandana Swami is a leading monk of Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement from Germany. He received initiation from His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda in 1971, and since that time, has been living as a representative of the ancient spiritual lineage called *gaudiya-vaiṣṇava-sampradāya*. In 1989 he entered the renounced order of life (*sannyāsa*), and since then has traveled all over the world guiding and inspiring people on the spiritual path. Through a number of devotional projects – among them two very successful cultural groups (the Gaurāṅga Bhajana Band and the Nityānanda Bhajana Band), who have played before large audiences throughout Eastern Europe – and through his seminars and public programs, he has become a well-known representative of the Vedic spiritual tradition. In this function he is regularly invited to speak at programs aimed at ecumenical exchange.

In the last ten years Śacinandana Swami has more and more traveled the road within, seeking inspiration and higher guidance on extended pilgrimages and spiritual retreats. These pilgrimages have led him to the Himalayas, to Eastern Tibet, and to many holy places all over India. As a result of his attempt to enter deeper into the *bhakti* tradition, his first book, The Nectarean Ocean of the Holy Name, was published in 1999. This book has become recognized as a standard textbook on the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* in many temples all over the world. This present work, The Gāyatri Book, aims at offering the same type of inspiration and illumination on yet another essential spiritual practice in the Gaudiya tradition.

At present Śacinandana Swami is focusing on establishing an international spiritual academy, with the aim to present the Vedic spiritual path in a convincing, non-dogmatic, and experiential way to a broad spectrum of people. His motto is, “A higher connection results in a higher state of living.”

## 3. Various Gāyatri-mantras

Here are various *gāyatri-mantras* for the expansions and servants of Śri Kṛṣṇa. Once again it is important to note that mantras generally must be received from the mouth of a guru in the disciplic succession: *sampradāya-vihinā ye mantras te niṣphalā matāḥ*. We cannot expect to achieve the desired result just by taking the mantras from a book and chanting them on our own. With this underlying consideration we present the following list for our readers’ inspiration.

***Gāyatris*** **for** ***Śrimati Rādhārāṇi***

*(1)*  
*śriṁ rādhikāyai vidmahe*  
*prema-rūpāyai dhimahi*  
*tan no rādhā pracodayāt*

**May we understand Śrimati Rādhārāṇi. Let us meditate on She who is the embodiment of** ***prema*****. May Śri Rādhā enlighten us.**

*(2)*  
*śriṁ rādhikāyai vidmahe*  
*kṛṣṇa-vallabhāyai dhimahi*  
*tan no gopiḥ pracodayāt*

**May we understand Śrimati Rādhārāṇi. Let us meditate on Her, who is the beloved of Śri Kṛṣṇa. May Śri Rādhā, the topmost** ***gopi*****, enlighten us.**

*(3)*  
*śriṁ rādhikāyai vidmahe*  
*kliṁ vṛṣabhānu-jāyai rūpāyai dhimahi*  
*tan no gopiḥ pracodayāt*

**May we understand Śrimati Rādhārāṇi. Let us meditate on She who is the daughter of King Vṛṣabhānu. May Śri Rādhā, the topmost** ***gopi*****, enlighten us.**

***Balarāma-gāyatri***

*kliṁ baladevāya vidmahe*  
*saṅkarśanāya dhimahi*  
*tan no balaḥ pracodayāt*

**May we understand Lord Baladeva. Let us meditate on He who is identical with Lord Saṅkarṣana. May Lord Balarāma enlighten us.**

***Mantras*** **for** ***Pañca-tattva***

***Nityānanda-gāyatri***

*kliṁ nityānandāya vidmahe*  
*saṅkarśanāya dhimahi*  
*tan no balaḥ pracodayāt*

**May we understand Lord Nityānanda. Let us meditate on He who is identical with Lord Saṅkarṣana. May Śri Nityānanda, who is no one else than Lord Balarāma, enlighten us.**

***Advaita-gāyatri***

*kliṁ advaitāya vidmahe*  
*mahā-viṣṇave dhimahi*  
*tan no ’dvaitaḥ pracodayāt*

**May we understand Śri Advaita ācārya. Let us meditate on Him who is none other than Mahā-Viṣṇu. May Lord Advaita enlighten us.**

***Gadādhara-gāyatri***

*śriṁ gadādharāya vidmahe*  
*paṇditākhyāya dhimahi*  
*tan no gadādharaḥ pracodayāt*

**May we understand Śri Gadādhara Prabhu. Let us meditate on he who is called Paṇdita. May Śri Gadādhara Paṇdita enlighten us.**

***Śrivāsa-gāyatri***

*śriṁ śrivāsāya vidmahe*  
*nāradākhyāya dhimahi*  
*tan naḥ śrivāsaḥ pracodayāt*

**May we understand Śrivāsa Paṇdita. Let us meditate on he who is known as Nārada Muni. May Śri Śrivāsa Paṇdita enlighten us.**

***Mantras*** **for** ***Ayodhya-tattva***

***Rāmacandra-gāyatris***

*(1)*  
*kliṁ raghuvaṁśāya vidmahe*  
*sitā-vallabhāya dhimahi*  
*tan no rāmaḥ pracodayāt*

**May we understand Lord Rāma, the ornament of the Raghu dynasty. Let us meditate on Him, the beloved of Mother Sitā. May Śri Rāma enlighten us.**

*(2)*  
*kliṁ dāśarathāya vidmahe*  
*sitā-ramyāya dhimahi*  
*tan no rāmah pracodayāt*

**May we understand Lord Rāma, the son of King Daśaratha. Let us meditate on Him, who gives pleasure to Mother Sitā. May Śri Rāma enlighten us.**

***Lakṣmaṇa-gāyatri***

*oṁ saṅkarśanāya vidmahe*  
*urmilā-vallabhāya dhimahi*  
*tan no lakṣmaṇaḥ pracodayāt*

**May we understand Śri Lakṣmaṇa, who is Lord Saṅkarṣana. Let us meditate on Him, the beloved son of Mother Urmilā. May Śri Lakṣmaṇa enlighten us.**

***Hanumān-gāyatris***

*(1)*  
*oṁ āñjaneyāya vidmahe*  
*vāyu-putrāya dhimahi*  
*tan no hanumate pracodayāt*

**May we understand Śri Hanuman, the son of Mother Añjanā. Let us meditate on him, the son of Vāyu. May Śri Hanuman enlighten us.**

*(2)*  
*oṁ añjaneyāya vidmahe*  
*rāma-dūtāya dhimahi tan*  
*no mārutiḥ pracodayāt*

**May we understand Śri Hanuman, the son of Mother Añjanā. Let us meditate on him, the faithful servant of Śri Rāma. May Śri Hanuman, who is born from the wind, enlighten us.**

***Mantras*** **for** ***Nārāyaṇa-tattva***

***Nārāyaṇa-gāyatri***

*oṁ nārāyaṇāya vidmahe*  
*vāsudevāya dhimahi*  
*tan no viṣṇuḥ pracodayāt*

**May we understand Lord Nārāyaṇa, the shelter of all living beings. Let us meditate on him as Vāsudeva, the indwelling Supersoul. May Lord Viṣṇu enlighten us.**

***Mahā-Lakṣmi-gāyatri***

*oṁ mahā-lakṣmyai ca vidmahe*  
*viṣṇu-patnyai ca dhimahi*  
*tan no lakṣmi pracodayāt*

**May we understand Mahā-Lakṣmi. Let us meditate on that spouse of Lord Viṣṇu. May Goddess Lakṣmi enlighten us.**

***Mantras*** **for** ***Nṛsiṁhadeva-tattva***

***Nṛsiṁha-gāyatris***

*(1)*  
*oṁ (kṣrauṁ) vajra-nakhāya vidmahe*  
*tikṣṇa-daṁṣṭrāya dhimahi*  
*tan no narasiṁhaḥ pracodayāt*

**May we understand Śri Nṛsiṁhadeva, the man-lion god, with his diamond nails that pierce the veil of ignorance. Let us meditate on that sharp-toothed one who destroys the demon of darkness. May Lord Narasiṁha enlighten us.**

*(2)*  
*kṣrauṁ narasiṁhāya vidmahe*  
*mahā-balāya dhimahi*  
*tan no ’nantaḥ pracodayāt*

**May we understand Śri Narasiṁhadeva. Let us meditate on Him who possesses great power. May Lord Narasiṁha, who is no one else than Śri Ananta, enlighten us.**

***Lakṣmi-gāyatri***

*oṁ mahādevyai vidmahe*  
*viṣṇu-patnyai ca dhimahi*  
*tan no lakṣmi pracodayāt*

**May we understand Lakṣmi Devi, the Great Goddess. Let us meditate on Her, the wife of Lord Viṣṇu. May Śri Lakṣmi Devi enlighten us.**

***Prahlāda-gāyatri***

*oṁ prahlādāya vidmahe*  
*bhakta-rājāya dhimahi*  
*tan no prahlādaḥ pracodayāt*

**May we understand Śri Prahlāda Mahārāja. Let us meditate on him, the King of bhakti. May Śri Prahlāda enlighten us.**

**Mantras for various servants of the Lord**

***Tulasi-gāyatri***

*oṁ śri-tulasyai vidmahe*  
*viṣṇu-priyāyai dhimahi*  
*tan no ’mṛtā pracodayāt*

**May we understand Śrimati Tulasi Devi. Let us meditate on her, the beloved of Lord Viṣṇu. May that beautiful goddess enlighten us.**

***Ananta-gāyatri***

*kliṁ rāja-rājāya vidmahe*  
*nāga-rājāya dhimahi*  
*tan no śeṣaḥ pracodayāt*

**May we understand Lord Ananta, the King of kings. Let us meditate on Him, the King of the serpents. May Lord Ananta Śeṣa enlighten us.**

***Sudarśana-gāyatri***

*oṁ sudarśanāya vidmahe*  
*mahā-jvālāya dhimahi*  
*tan no cakraḥ pracodayāt*

**May we understand Śri Sudarśana. Let us meditate on him, the Great Fire. May that Sudarśana-*****cakra*** **enlighten us.**

***Garuda-gāyatri***

*oṁ tat-puruṣāya vidmahe*  
*suvarṇa-pakṣāya dhimahi*  
*tan no garudaḥ pracodayāt*

**May we understand that** ***tat-puruṣa*****, the supreme principle. Let us meditate on the golden-winged one. May that Garuda enlighten us.**

***Rudra-gāyatri***

*oṁ tat-puruṣāya vidmahe*  
*mahādevāya dhimahi*  
*tan no rudraḥ pracodayāt*

**May we understand the** ***tat-puruṣa,*** **the transcendental person. Let us meditate on that Mahādeva. May that Rudra enlighten us.**

***Durgā-gāyatri***

*oṁ kātyāyanyai vidmahe*  
*kanyā-kumāryai dhimahi*  
*tan no durgā pracodayāt*

**May we understand Kātyāyani, the** ***śakti*** **goddess. Let us meditate on Kanyā-kumāri, the virgin goddess. May that Durgā enlighten us.**

***Gaṇeśa-gāyatri***

*oṁ ekadantāya vidmahe*  
*vakra-tuṇdāya dhimahi*  
*tan no dantiḥ pracodayāt*

**May we understand Lord Gaṇeśa. Let us meditate on that elephant-headed god who removes the hurdles. May that one-tusked god enlighten us.**

***Bhūmi-gāyatris***

*(1)*  
*bhūṁ dhanur-dharāyai vidmahe*  
*sarva-siddhyai ca dhimahi*  
*dharā pracodayāt*

**May we understand Śrimati Bhūmi Devi who is holding a bow. Let us meditate on her who bestows all perfection. May that goddess, who sustains the world, enlighten us.**

*(2)*  
*bhūṁ pṛthivi-devyai vidmahe*  
*sahasra-mūrtyai ca dhimahi*  
*tan naḥ pṛthivi pracodayāt*

**May we understand Śrimati Bhūmi Devi, the presiding goddess of the material energy. Let us meditate on her who possesses thousands of forms. May that goddess, the material energy, enlighten us.**

***Sūrya-gāyatri***

*oṁ bhāskarāya vidmahe*  
*divākarāya dhimahi*  
*tan naḥ sūryaḥ pracodayāt*

**May we understand Bhāskara, the shining one. Let us meditate on that Divākāra, the presiding deity of the sky. May that Sūrya, the sun-god, enlighten us.**

***Agni-gāyatri***

*oṁ vaiśvānarāya vidmahe*  
*lālelāya dhimahi*  
*tan no ’gniḥ pracodayāt*

**May we understand Vaiśvānara, the fire-god. Let us meditate on the seven-tongued, mystic fire. May that Agni, the fire-god, enlighten us.**

***Yama-gāyatri***

*oṁ sūrya-putrāya vidmahe*  
*mahā-kālāya dhimahi*  
*tan no yamaḥ pracodayāt*

**May we understand that son of Sūrya, the sungod. Let us meditate on the Great Time, which ends everything. May that Yama, the god of death, enlighten us.**

## 4. Different Mūla-mantras

The following list provides the standard mūla-mantras used in Deity wor-ship. As already explained, these mantras are the essence of the Deity and are used as a way to address the Lord. They are formed by the respective bija-mantra followed by the Deity’s name and then namaḥ, “I offer obeisances.” Thus their meanings are clear. We have provided translations only for those names not commonly known.

**Mantras for Guru-tattva**

***Guru***  
(The *guru-mūla-mantra*)

**Previous** ***Guru***  
*oṁ parama-gurave namaḥ*

**Previous** ***Guru***  
*oṁ paratpara-gurave namaḥ*

**Previous** ***Guru***  
*oṁ parameṣṭhi-gurave namaḥ*

**All** ***Vaiṣṇavas***  
*oṁ sarva-vaiṣṇavebhyo namaḥ*

**Mantras for Pañca-tattva**

***Lord Caitanya***  
*(The gaura-mūla-mantra)*

***Lord Nitānanda***  
(1)  
*kliṁ nityānandāya namaḥ*  
(2)  
*kliṁ devi-jāhnavā-vallabhāya namaḥ*

***Advaita ācārya***  
*kliṁ advaitāya namaḥ*

***Śri Gadādhara***  
*śriṁ gadādharāya namaḥ*

***Śri Śrivāsa***  
*śriṁ śrivāsāya namaḥ*

**Mantras for Vṛndavana-tattva**

***Lord Kṛṣṇa, Govardhana-śilā, Dvārakā śilā***  
(1)  
(The *gopāla-mantra*)  
(2)  
*kliṁ kṛṣṇāya namaḥ*

***Śrimati Rādhārāṇi***  
(1)  
*śriṁ rāṁ rādhikāyai svaha*  
(2)  
*śriṁ rādhāyai namaḥ*

***Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa***  
*śriṁ kliṁ rādhā-kṛṣṇābhyāṁ namaḥ*

**All the** ***Gopis*** **(or** ***Sakhis*****)**  
*oṁ sarva-sakhibhyo namaḥ*

***Lalitā-Viśākhā***  
*śriṁ śriṁ lalitā-viśākhābhyaṁ namaḥ*

***Lalitā Devi*** ***śriṁ***  
*laṁ lalitāyai namaḥ*

***Viśākhā Devi***  
*aiṁ śauṁ viśakhāyai namaḥ*

***Citrā Devi***  
*śriṁ citrāyai namaḥ*

***Indulekhā***  
*aiṁ indulekhāyai namaḥ*

***Campakalatā***  
*oṁ campakalatāyai namaḥ*

***Raṅgadevi***  
*śriṁ ram raṅgadevyai namaḥ*

***Tuṅgavidyā***  
*śriṁ tuṅgavidyāyai namaḥ*

***Sudevi***  
*aiṁ śrauṁ Śriṁ sudevyai namaḥ*

***Śrimati Tulasi Devi***  
*oṁ tulasyai namaḥ*

***Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma***  
*kliṁ hriṁ Śriṁ bala-kṛṣṇābhyāṁ namaḥ*

***Lord Balarāma***  
*kliṁ balarāmāya namaḥ*

**Mantras for Nilācala-tattva**

***Lord Jagannātha***  
(1)  
(*The gopāla-mantra*)  
(2)  
*kliṁ kṛṣṇāya namaḥ*

***Lord Baladeva***  
*kliṁ baladevāya namaḥ*

***Śrimati Subhad***  
*śriṁ subhadrāyai namaḥ*

***Śri Sudarśana***  
*oṁ sudarśanāya namaḥ*

**Mantras for Ayodhya-tattva**

***Lord Rāmacandra***  
*kliṁ (*or *rāṁ)* *rāmāya namaḥ*

***Śrimati Sitā***  
*śriṁ sitāyai namaḥ*

***Sitā-Rāma***  
*śriṁ rāṁ sitā-rāmābhyāṁ namaḥ*

***Śri Lakṣmaṇa***  
(1)  
*kliṁ saṅkarṣaṇāya namaḥ*  
(2)  
*laṁ lakṣmaṇāya namaḥ*

***Śri Hanuman***  
*huṁ hanumate namaḥ*

**Mantras for Viṣṇu-tattva**

***Viṣṇu-śālagrāma-śilā***  
(1)  
*oṁ namo bhagavate vāsudevāya*  
(2)  
*oṁ namo nārāyaṇāya*

***Lord Nṛsiṁha***  
*kliṁ (*or *kṣrauṁ) nṛsiṁhāya namaḥ*

***Lakṣmi-Nṛsiṁha***  
*Śriṁ kliṁ (*or *kṣrauṁ) lakṣmi-nṛsiṁhābhyāṁ namaḥ*

***Prahlāda***  
*praṁ prahladāya namaḥ*

***Prahlāda-Nṛsiṁha***  
*praṁ kliṁ (*or *kṣrauṁ) prahlāda-nṛsiṁhābhyāṁ namaḥ*
